# AGX backend experiments: results to verify on hardware

Every result below comes from the AGX Performance Simulator's cost model (`agx_cost`), not from a GPU. The
simulator is calibrated on an M2 Max, but several decisions rest on single measured parameters (named per
entry). This file lists each experiment so that it can be re-run on a real M2 (Asahi Linux, a CUDA driver for the GPU,
or any harness that loads the objects `llc` emits and times dispatches).

## Library kernels with realistic launch inputs (`lr` set)

agx-bench costs the agx_libs kernels (the `lb` set) with every argument 0, which measures their prologue only:
the conv and DNN rows of A26 / A27 come from launch inputs set by hand. `tools/lib_shapes.py` writes one file per
kernel listed in `tools/lib_shapes.txt` (realistic shapes: a 1 x 64 x 56 x 56 image and 3 x 3 filters for cuDNN,
256 x 256 x 256 products for cuBLAS), with argument names from the MLIR source and widths from the compiled IR (so
the `_i32` builds take the same line), and a zero-filled buffer at address 0 so that agx_cost traces with its
functional executor (its default trace skips `rcp` / `convert`: loop bounds computed by divisions read as unknown):

    tools/lib_shapes.py --mlir-dir PTX_FRONTEND/agx_libs/kernels --llvm-extract BIN/llvm-extract \
        --shapes tools/lib_shapes.txt -o lr/ libll/*.opt.ll
    agx-bench.py ... --ll lr/*.ll

## How to verify an entry

1. Build `llc` from this branch. Each entry gives an **A/B switch**: an `llc` option (both settings in one
   binary) or, where there is none, the commit to revert.
2. Compile the named kernels both ways: `llc -mtriple=agx -mcpu=agx-g14g -filetype=obj K.ll` (`agx-g14x` for an
   M2 Max/Pro, `agx-g13g` for an M1). `kernels/cuda_*.ll` come from `cuda/*.cu`, whose first line gives the
   launch the model assumed (sizes, grid, workgroup).
3. Time both on the GPU with that launch, scaled up until the dispatch takes at least ~1 ms, median of many runs.
   Also time a **full-occupancy** launch (enough workgroups for every core), because the model reports two
   numbers:
   - **cycles**: one SIMD-group alone (latency; what a small grid sees);
   - **tput**: cycles per SIMD-group with as many groups per core as registers / threadgroup memory allow
     (throughput; what a large grid sees).
4. Check results are bit-identical between A and B (every change here should be semantics-preserving).

Kernel sets used: **rw** = `kernels/*.ll` (8 shapes + 27 real-world CUDA kernels), **co** = the PTX front end's tests
(195 kernels compiled by zoc), **lb** = the `agx_libs` MLIR libraries. Percentages are the change in summed
cycles over a set (negative = faster) unless stated.

## Adopted (in the default pipeline)

|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| A1 | Store clustering in the machine scheduler, AGXMemCluster (73a21ff5a) | `-agx-mem-cluster=0` vs default; `-agx-mem-cluster-kinds=` mask (1 dev load, 2 dev store, 4 tg load, 8 tg store; default 11) | co -0.76% (28 better, 0 worse), lb -9.5% (tile GEMM epilogues), G14G sgemm tile at K=256 -0.1..-1.5% | `mem_quiet_device_store_ref` 33.6: a store right after an ALU op waits ~34 cycles, back-to-back stores ~3 | `cuda_add_extended`-like store tails, `agx_sgemm_tile64*` (agx_libs), `vector_operand` |
| A2 | Device-load clustering (in the mask since "Cluster device loads too") | `-agx-mem-cluster-kinds=10` (no loads) vs 11 | cycles: attn_decode -34%, blur_rgba8 -28%, q4_matvec -28%, q8_matvec -20%, co/lb -3.8%; **tput ~0%** (latency only); index_add on G13 +17% | `mem_quiet_load_ref` 20: a load right after ALU work completes ~20 cycles later | `cuda_attn_decode`, `cuda_q4_matvec`, `cuda_q8_matvec`, `cuda_blur_rgba8` with a **small** grid (e.g. 32 workgroups) and a full one |

| A15 | **Wide constants of kernels in uniform registers** (AGXUniformConstants, `-agx-uniform-constants`, off in `llc`): each 32-bit `mov_imm` of a kernel becomes a uniform preloaded after the arguments (one per value, at most 64), listed in `.agx.kernels` extension 6 for the launcher; AGXFoldOperands folds them into the users. **On in the PTX front end** (`compile_agx`, agx_libs builds; agx_runtime sets the uniforms) | the front end's uniform-constants switch off (rebuild agx_libs too); `-agx-uniform-constants` in `llc` | tput geomean rw -2.4% (sgemm_reg4x4 -27.6%: 70 -> 66 GPRs, ddot -7%), co -2.0% (transcendental -29.9%), lb -1.0%; cycles ~0 (-0.1..-0.5% geomean; gelu_f16 +15% and softmax_online +4% from a different store / stall order, not from uniform reads); specialized CUDA kernels (R21) 2-5 GPRs fewer, tput -2..-4% (rmsnorm still above its unspecialized form); PTX tests and agx_libs simulator tests unchanged; llc time within noise | a uniform source costs the same as a GPR source (the model has no uniform penalty); preloading ~2 more uniform words per constant is free at dispatch | `cuda_sgemm_reg4x4`, `cuda_ddot`, `softmax_row`, `transcendental`, agx_libs dnn / blas; compare with the front end's switch off; watch gelu_f16-like store tails |
| A16 | Post-RA strategy (AGXPostRASchedStrategy): the next access of a memory cluster ranks ahead of latency stalls, and the scheduler waits up to 8 cycles for a pending one instead of putting another instruction between two accesses | `-agx-postra-cluster-wait=0` vs default 8 (3 and 16 give the same) | cycles: gelu_f16 -13.9%, ldmatrix -15.1%, none worse (rw geomean -0.42%, co -0.19%); tput +-1% noise (attn_decode +1.0%: three moves reordered); agx_libs simulator tests unchanged; llc time not measurable | `mem_quiet_device_store_ref` (as A1): an ALU op between stores costs more than a few cycles' stall | `cuda_gelu_f16` (two 16-bit stores per iteration), `ldmatrix*` PTX tests |
| A17 | f32 add / mul / fma whose only use is a conversion to half precision write the 16-bit register themselves (FMUL32_F16_F32, FMADD32_F16_F32, FADD32_F16_F32), unless a source extends half precision (the FMADD32_M forms keep it) | revert "[AGX] Fold f32-to-f16 conversions..." | G14C hgemm tile64 cf16 -3.9..-5.6% cycles (all 8), gelu_f16 tput -2.1%, none worse (G13G hgemm -4..-50%: spilling tiles); PTX check and agx_libs hgemm / epilogue / dnn / conv simulator tests unchanged; llc time not measurable | applegpu: a 32-bit operation with a 16-bit destination rounds to f32 (flushing f32 denormals, which are 0 in f16 anyway) and converts, as fptrunc of it does | cuBLAS hgemm with f16 C, gelu_f16; check f16 results bit-exact against fptrunc of the f32 result |
| A18 | **Store vectorization limited by register pressure** (L3-E1): AGX TTI estimates each function's peak live 32-bit values (SSA liveness, backward dataflow over the IR) once, and when it reaches the subtarget's GPR count the LoadStoreVectorizer may merge stores only up to 4 bytes (a vector store needs its sources in consecutive registers, which forces copies and spills under pressure) | `-agx-store-vector-pressure=0` (off; default -1 = the GPR count); `-agx-max-store-vector-bytes` | both paths: sgemm_reg4x4 tput -27.7%; AIR path mma_m16n8k32_s32_s8_s8_s32_interleave cycles -75% (the L1-E1 +300% case); AIR vs direct co -5.18% cycles / -3.30% tput, rw +0.09% / -0.17%, lb ~0; llc time 4.42 vs 4.38 s over libll (noise) | an IR estimate tracks the final pressure closely enough to separate spilling kernels from the rest | `cuda_sgemm_reg4x4`, `mma_*_interleave` PTX tests, tile GEMM epilogues |

| A19 | Software f64: a float, half or bfloat widened to double exactly by rebiasing the exponent and shifting the fraction (a denormal normalized by one count of leading zeros) instead of the general unpack / round (L4-E3) | revert "[AGX] Exact widening to double..." | cvt_f64_f32 -16.6% cycles / -14.8% tput (direct path); nothing else changes | the conversion is exact, so no rounding step is needed | `agx_f64_check.py fpext` (8192 inputs bit-exact, both NaN modes), `cvt.f64.f32` PTX tests |
| A20 | **A 32-bit divisor's reciprocal computed once** (L5-E1, AGXCodeGenPrepare): the divisor-only part of the 32-bit division expansion (|y|, the f32 reciprocal estimate, its Newton step) is built right after the divisor's definition and shared by every division by it; a divisor that is a cheap pure expression (a PTX front end's `umax(y, 1)`, a trunc in each 64-bit division's 32-bit fast path) is rebuilt after its leaves, so the copies in unrolled iterations share it | `-agx-share-divisors=0` | both paths: permute4d -9.8% cycles / -8.1% tput (46 -> 60 GPRs, still faster); rw geomean -0.30%, co -0.05%; lb -0.31% cycles / -0.12% tput geomean with tile GEMMs moving both ways (sgemm_tile64_u -15.4%, hgemm_tile64_cf32_ta +11.9%: register allocation) | the estimate z depends on the divisor only; the corrections keep each quotient exact | `cuda_permute4d`, `divrem32.ll`, PTX div / rem tests |
| A21 | **64-bit induction variables counted in 32 bits** (L5-E2, AGXNarrowInductions, after loop strength reduction): a 64-bit header phi incremented by a loop-invariant amount whose value, increment and step scalar evolution bounds below 2^31 becomes a 32-bit phi, its 64-bit uses the zero extension (PTX's byte offsets over a threadgroup tile: one iadd and the loop test instead of a 64-bit add and three instructions comparing both halves) | `-agx-narrow-ivs=0` | both paths: nbody -6.1% cycles and tput; nothing else changes | | `cuda_nbody`, `narrow-inductions.ll` |
| A22 | **Bitfield extracts in one bfeil** (L6-E1): `(x >> s) & (2^k - 1)` (32 and 16 bits) is one `bfeil` with offset s and a k-bit mask when the shift has no other use (otherwise the shift stays and a cheaper `and` reads it: fma_bf16x2 +10% tput without that rule); `(x & (m << s)) >> s` becomes that form first (AGXISelLowering) | revert "[AGX] Bitfield extracts..." | both paths: ddot -9.7% cycles (AGXSoftF64), q4_matvec -6.7% (nibbles), rw geomean -0.54% / -0.39% tput, co -0.16% / -0.15%; lb -0.79% total cycles but G13 tile GEMMs at 128 GPRs move +3..+46% (spills: sgemm_tile64_u 1478 -> 1578 instructions), G14 unchanged | bfeil (latency 3) replaces a dependent shift and and (2 + 3) | `agx_f64_check.py` (all operations bit-exact, both NaN modes), `bitfield-extract.ll`, `cuda_q4_matvec` |
| A23 | **Branch conditions in 32 bits** (L6-E3, AGXISelLowering): the zero extension of 16-bit boolean logic (i1 promoted to i16: compares, 0 / 1 truncations, and / or / xor) to 32 bits for a branch is the same logic in 32 bits, where the compares produce it, and an if / loop on `b ^ 1` for a boolean b branches on `b == 0`, which the branch compares itself (bitonic sort's `(a > b) == up`: no 16-bit xor, no move, no xor with 1) | revert "[AGX] Branch conditions in 32 bits..." | both paths: bitonic_smem 818 -> 712 instructions, -2.4% cycles / -4.2% tput; subc_cc_s32 -16.7% tput; add_extended +4.7% tput (8.6 -> 9.0, same 29 instructions), agx_cger +2.0% tput | | `boolean-branch.ll`, `cuda_bitonic_smem` |
| A24 | **minimumnum / maximumnum in three instructions** (L7-E2; PTX `min` / `max`): the hardware's NaN-ignoring `fcmpsel ltn / gtn`, and for operands that compare equal their bits ORed (min) / ANDed (max), which orders -0 below +0 (the generic expansion took six); with `nsz` the fcmpsel alone | revert "[AGX] minimumnum / maximumnum..." | both paths: tput min_f16 -21.3%, softmax_online -2.1%, attn_decode -2.4% cycles, none worse in tput; cycles softmax_online +2.3% | equal operands are the only case where hardware min / max and the IEEE 754-2019 minimumNumber / maximumNumber differ (signed zeros) | `minmaxnum.ll`, PTX min / max tests (408 pass, strict FP) |
| A25 | **minimum / maximum (NaN-propagating) in six instructions** (L8-E1): the minimumnum / maximumnum form of A24, then the default NaN where an operand is not equal to itself (the generic expansion took eight) | revert "[AGX] minimum / maximum in six instructions..." | both paths: min_nan_f16 -26.6% cycles / -17.9% tput, nothing worse | | `minmaxnum.ll`, PTX min / max NaN tests |
| A26 | **cuDNN convolutions with 32-bit indices** (L9-E1): `agx-build-fatbin.py --index-bitwidth=32 --kernel-suffix=_i32` builds conv.mlir a second time (`conv_i32.agxfat`, kernels `<name>_i32`); the runtime's cuDNN runs those when every element offset of x / w / y, the im2col / col2im temporary and the thread count stay below 2^31 (`index32`). With 64-bit `index`, each of the 8 index divisions (thread -> n, k, p, q) carries a run-time 32-bit check and a 64-bit slow path | the 32-bit convolution switch off (runtime) | 1 x 64 x 56 x 56, K 64, 3 x 3, pad 1 (agx_cost with those arguments, G14C and G13G alike): fwd -33% cycles / -52% tput (77 -> 34 GPRs), bwd_data -48% / -59% (102 -> 39), bwd_filter -14% / -44% (76 -> 31). (agx-bench's lb set runs library kernels with zero arguments: its conv rows measure only the index prologue) | 32-bit offsets are exact below 2^31 elements | agx_libs `test_kernels.py --kernel-suffix _i32 conv_i32.agxfat`: 960 / 960 cases on every chip, strict builds included; the runtime's unit test `index32_choice` |
| A27 | **cuDNN pointwise / softmax / pooling / batch-norm kernels with 32-bit indices** (L9-E2): `dnn_i32.agxfat` as A26, chosen per launch by `fits32` (every integer argument and element offset below 2^31); the i64 host arguments give their low halves to the 32-bit parameters | the 32-bit DNN switch off (runtime) | act_fwd over 64 x 56 x 56 (agx_cost with those arguments): -42% cycles / -50% tput, 32 -> 16 GPRs (each index decomposed by three 64-bit divisions before) | as A26 | `test_kernels.py --kernel-suffix _i32 dnn_i32.agxfat`: 1392 / 1392 on every chip |
| A28 | **Convolution loops over the in-image range** (L10-E1, agx_libs conv.mlir): fwd's filter taps (r, s) and bwd_filter's output positions (p, q) run over the range inside the padded image, computed once per thread with signed ceiling divisions, instead of a bounds check per iteration (IRCE, LLVM's inductive range check elimination, finds the checks but needs profile data and then hits a block-frequency assertion here) | revert the commit | `lr` set (functional trace): bwd_filter -33% cycles / -42% tput (64-bit index), -28% / -48% (`_i32`); fwd -10% / -21% and -11% / -37%; bwd_data unchanged. Per iteration: bwd_filter inner loop 16 -> 10 instructions, fwd 20 -> 13. (agx_cost's default trace skips `rcp` / `convert`, so loop bounds from a division read as unknown there: AGX-Performance-Simulator followups item 16; lib_shapes.py now maps a buffer, which selects the functional trace) | strides and dilations are positive (cuDNN) | `test_kernels.py`: 960 / 960 for conv and conv_i32 |
| A29 | **dnn: dense NCHW tensors index directly** (L11-E1, agx_libs dnn.mlir `agx_dnn_off`): when a tensor's strides are (C·H·W, H·W, W, 1) its offset is the linear index, so the divisions by W, H and C that recover (n, c, h, w) and the 4-term dot product are skipped (a uniform branch: the strides are kernel arguments) | revert the commit | `lr` set (functional trace, dense ResNet tensor): act_fwd -37% cycles (64-bit index), -8% (`_i32`); softmax_fwd -44%, -10%. Code grows (act_fwd 3.3 -> 6.4 KB at 64 bits) since both paths stay. pointwise, bn_apply and pool unchanged: they need the coordinates anyway (broadcast operands, per-channel parameters, pooling windows) | none (falls back to the strided path) | `test_kernels.py`: 1664 / 1664 for dnn and dnn_i32, incl. new dense-tensor (`packed`) act and pointwise cases |
| A30 | **Division by a kernel argument with launch-computed multipliers** (L12-E1, `-agx-launch-divisors`, on in zoc and agx_libs builds): a 32-bit udiv / urem / sdiv / srem by an i32 argument (or a 32-bit word of an i64 one, the fast path of a 64-bit division) reads m, s1, s2 from uniforms the driver computes at launch (.agx.kernels extension 7, `llvm.agx.launch.value`), q = (t + ((x - t) >> s1)) >> s2, t = mulhi(x, m): Granlund and Montgomery, PLDI 1994, exact for every 32-bit x. About 6 instructions per division instead of a reciprocal estimate, a Newton step and two corrections (about 17); at most 8 divisors per kernel | the front end's launch-divisors switch off, or drop the option | `lr` set (functional trace): bn_apply_i32 -25% cycles / -31% tput, pointwise_i32 -17% / -31%, softmax_fwd_i32 -8% / -23%, pool_fwd_i32 -7% / -16%, act_fwd_i32 -2% / -5%, conv bwd_data_i32 -4% / -11%; 26 better, none worse (geomean -1.7% cycles, -3.1% tput). rw / co / lb sets unchanged (their divisors are not kernel arguments, or the costed paths have none) | every consumer of .agx.kernels must set the extension (agx_runtime, test_kernels.py, agx-bench, run_checked, the PTX formalization harness do) | agx_runtime unit test (exactness over 400+ divisors, edge dividends); agx_libs simulator suites pass (sparse's atomic SpMV on multi-die G13 fails as before, simulator followups item 8); PTX formalization 408 pass, direct and AIR, as before |
| A31 | **Launch divisors: extended and guarded arguments** (L12-E2, extends A30): the low word of a sign- or zero-extended i32 argument (the 32-bit fast path of a 64-bit index division in agx_libs' 64-bit builds), and a PTX front end's guarded divisor `umax(d, 1)` (kinds 6-8: the launch computes the values of max(d, 1); no zero check, the divisor is never 0) | the front end's launch-divisors switch off | `lr` set: conv im2col (64-bit index) -13.6% cycles, conv bwd_data (64-bit) -8.7% / -14.5% tput; lb set (zero inputs) im2col_f16 -22% / -28% tput, 22 better, 4 tput-worse (+0.9% max, bn_bwd_params); PTX: permute4d -8.6% cycles / -7.3% tput (rw and co). The AIR path gets the same (permute4d 1552 cycles on both) | as A30 | lit launch-divisors.ll; agx_runtime unit test (kinds 6-8); agx_libs conv / dnn / blas / fft 64-bit suites pass; PTX formalization (see commit) |
| A32 | **Straight-line strength reduction and n-ary reassociation** (L13-E1, `-agx-straight-line-sr=1`, default; NVPTX's address passes: SLSR, EarlyCSE, NaryReassociate, EarlyCSE after kernel argument promotion, before the load/store vectorizer; Wu et al., LLVM Dev 2015, "Optimizing LLVM for GPGPU"): addresses of unrolled tiles become a neighbour plus a difference, sums are reassociated so common parts are computed once | -agx-straight-line-sr=0 | `lr` set: tile GEMMs tput -2..-12% (sgemm_tile64 -3.7%, _u -5.5%, hgemm cf32 -3.7% g14c, -11.7% g13g; sgemm_tile64_u g13g +4.8% tput at -4.9% cycles), geomean -0.46% tput / -0.78% cycles; lb set -5.9% total tput (hgemm _u tiles -41..-49% on zero inputs), rw / co: reduce -30% cycles, rounding -48% (floor/ceil chains fold), index_add g13g +8.2% tput at fewer GPRs and cycles (more resident groups in the model). GVN in place of the last EarlyCSE: identical. Compile time within noise (hgemm 1.01 -> 1.03 s) | none | AGX lit (fp.ll's rounding test now keeps each rounding apart); agx_libs simulator suites and PTX formalization (see commit / next row) |
| A33 | **Signed bytes convert with s8_to_f** (L13-E2): `sitofp` of a sign-extended byte (sext_inreg, or the shl / sra pair it expands to, from 16- or 32-bit registers, to f32 or f16) is one `convert s8_to_f`, which reads the low eight bits as signed (G14C testsuite convert_s8_to_f_*); also u8_to_f from 32-bit registers. Was shl + asr + s16_to_f | revert | q8_matvec (int8 weights) -24% cycles / -43% tput; nothing else changes (fp.ll's rounding row moved with its new input, A32) | none | lit convert-s8.ll, i64.ll |
| A34 | **Branches on the xor of two booleans compare them** (L13-E3, extends L6-E3): IF / LOOP on a ^ b is on a != b, and on (a ^ b) == 0 / != 0 on a == b / a != b: one if_icmp of the two instead of an xor and a compare with zero (53 such pairs over the suites, the most frequent dependent instruction pair) | revert | bitonic_smem -2.5% cycles / -2.9% tput; the rest are off hot paths | none | lit boolean-branch.ll |
| A35 | **Constants around ctlz / cttz fold into the subtraction** (L13-E4): ctlz(x) + 32 (the low half of a 64-bit count) is 63 - ffs(x), cttz likewise, and 31 - ctlz(x) / ctlz(x) ^ 31 is ffs(x); was 31 - ffs(x) and an add (ctlz was selected late, after DAG combines) | revert | ddot -1.8% cycles, software-f64 kernels (co) 26 better / 4 worse within +-1%; one instruction per 64-bit count | none | lit fp.ll, i64.ll |
| A36 | **Loop exit flags as 32-bit phis** (L13-E5, `-agx-widen-boolean-phis`, default): the structurizer's boolean phis that a loop's back edge takes (llvm.agx.loop), and the boolean phis they are made of, become i32 phis (incoming compares zero-extended, uses compared with zero), so the flag is not set in 16 bits and widened by a copy every iteration | -agx-widen-boolean-phis=0 | mandelbrot -1.9% cycles / -3.8% tput; nothing worse on any set. Widening every boolean phi (or every branch-feeding one) also removed if/else flow copies (vote_all -9.4% tput) but cost the software-f64 kernels 2 GPRs (14 lb kernels up to +3.4% tput) and moved else-flow-hoist.ll's compare | none | lit control-flow.ll (widening copies gone) |
| A37 | **Addressing modes for LSR** (L14-E2, `-agx-addressing-modes=1`, default; isLegalAddressingMode was the generic default: any register + any immediate): device accesses take a base plus a 16-bit offset in elements, threadgroup / private ones a base plus a 16-bit offset; no global bases, no scaled register (mode 2 adds base + extended index x element size x 1 / 2 / 4, which LSR cannot see the extension of) | -agx-addressing-modes=0 | attn_decode -9.0% cycles / -5.6% tput; nothing worse on rw, co, lb or the lr set. Mode 2: lb +5.6% tput (tile GEMMs up to +129% cycles) | none | AGX lit unchanged |
| A38 | **Special register bounds before instruction selection** (L14-E3, `-agx-sr-ranges`, default; follows the rejected L14-E1): the thread / threadgroup index reads get range return attributes after loop strength reduction (AGXSpecialRegisterRanges, first in addPreISel), which become AssertZext nodes, so sign fix-ups of indices fold and index extensions are unsigned; the IR optimizers and LSR never see them, so no 64-bit IVs | -agx-sr-ranges=0 | `lr` set tput geomean -0.24% (embedding -6.3%, rmsnorm -1.9%, softmax_online / layernorm -1.6%), worst +0.6% (sgemm_tile64_u), GPRs unchanged; rw / co small gains, lb 37 better / 25 worse tput within -6..+8% (zero inputs). (sgemm_reg4x4's -7.4% of L14-E1 needed the bounds in opt) | none | lit memory.ll, sink-addresses.ll (index extension now unsigned) |

## Cumulative (model): session start (41842c90f) to A16 (ab8a3ff4c)

The same IR through the `llc` of 41842c90f (before A1-A16 and the earlier precise-math, wait and soft-f64 changes)
and the current one, G13G and G14C, agx-bench. Geomean / total over kernel x chip; negative is faster.

| Set | Metric | `llc` default | PTX front end (+ `-agx-uniform-constants`) |
|---|---|---|---|
| CUDA suite (36 kernels) | cycles | -5.0% / -10.7% | -5.5% / -10.7% |
| | tput | -0.2% / -6.5% | -2.6% / -12.9% |
| | GPRs | -0.4% | -4.3% |
| PTX tests (200 files) | cycles | -1.2% / -1.2% | -1.7% / -2.3% |
| | tput | +0.7% / +0.3% | -1.3% / -2.3% |
| | GPRs | +1.1% | -2.1% |
| agx_libs (198 kernels) | cycles | -4.0% / -18.0% | -4.1% / -18.4% |
| | tput | -0.7% / -3.3% | -1.4% / -4.0% |
| | GPRs | -2.0% | -5.8% |

Largest gains (G14C cycles): hgemm tile64 variants -45..-50%, vector_operand -43%, gelu_f16 -37%, blur_rgba8 -37%,
attn_decode -34%; tput: sgemm_reg4x4 -39%, layernorm_f16 -26%, match_sync -24%. Slower by design: `transcendental`
+279% and softmax_row +23% (precise sin / cos / exp / log instead of the hardware approximations), div_ftz +19% and
min_nan_f16 +33% (IEEE results). Slower as a scheduling trade-off (A3): stencil +29%, reduce +28%, bitonic_smem +12%.

## Measured and rejected (model)

| # | Change | Model result | Why rejected / note for hardware |
|---|---|---|---|
| R1 | Threadgroup-load clustering (`-agx-mem-cluster-kinds=15`) | cycles: sgemm_reg4x4 -16%, nbody -9%; tput +12.8% (sgemm +57%: 70 -> 77 GPRs drops resident groups); ddot +33% | occupancy. **Worth checking on hardware**: the occupancy formula decides it |
| R2 | Generic post-RA scheduling without clustering (`-misched-postra -enable-post-misched`, legacy `-post-RA-scheduler`) | +1.0..+3.0% | spreads stores apart (see A1) |
| R3 | Scheduler knobs: `-enable-misched=false` +43%, pre-RA top-down +17.7%, bottom-up +0.05%, `-misched-regpressure=false` +16.9%; `-misched-cluster`, `-misched-cyclicpath`, `-enable-aa-sched-mi` no change | | default bidirectional pre-RA stays |
| R4 | bfeil for every low mask (also masks with other uses), bfeil for fabs | co -0.37% but 30 kernels up to +2.5% | a shared mov_imm + and beats several bfeil |
| R5 | Load clustering with weak (cluster-only) edges | ~0% | edges must constrain to matter |
| R6 | Runs of 4 or 6 loads | +0.2..+0.7% | attention loop needs 8 loads together |
| R7 | SimplifyCFG sink / hoist common instructions, GVNSink (DARM-like melding of divergent branches) on top of -O3 | 0 kernels changed | -O3 already does it; no similar-but-different branch pairs in these kernels |
| R8 | 64-bit division bypass (addBypassSlowDiv) | no-op | already in AGXCodeGenPrepare |
| R9 | 4x8 matrices per SIMD-group tile GEMM | 13% fewer cycles per element but 128 GPRs, spills, 448 -> 384 threads | **check on hardware**: spill and occupancy costs are uncertain in the model |
| R10 | Software-pipelined tile GEMM loop; SIMD-group-per-row CSR SpMV | +19..125%; scalar kernel wins at all row lengths tried | |
| R11 | Speculating divergent branches harder: SpeculativeExecution cost limit 40 (default 7, already in -O3 for divergent targets), two-entry PHI folding 12 (default 4) | rw geomean -0.31% cycles (jacobi2d -7.4%), lb -0.08% tput (one conv kernel -12.9%), nothing worse | small; only a late run after -O3 finds these (the limits inside zoc's -O3 pipeline change none of 450 PTX-test or 124 CUDA kernel x chip results), so adopting needs a late SpeculativeExecution + SimplifyCFG in the AGX IR pipeline and a global cost option. **Hardware check**: jacobi2d, agx_conv2d_im2col_f16 with `opt -spec-exec-max-speculation-cost=40 -two-entry-phi-node-folding-threshold=12` |
| R12 | Unrolling single-block loops with device loads x2/x4/x8 (runtime trip counts) for memory-level parallelism | no suite kernel faster; lb tput +0.6..+1.1% geomean (more GPRs), worst +85% | |
| R13 | Occupancy-targeted scheduling: register-pressure limits scaled to 104 / 83 / 64 / 48 GPRs | mixed, +-20% on tile GEMMs; kernels that gained resident groups gained little tput (mma_m16n8k32_s32_s8: 72 -> 34 GPRs, 22 -> 48 groups, tput -1.9%; conv3x3 32 -> 40 groups, tput +5%) | the model's latency hiding saturates near 20-30 groups. **Hardware check**: mma_m16n8k32_s32_s8_s8_s32 (PTX test) with `llc` at the default vs a register-limited schedule: if occupancy matters more on the M2 than in the model, revisit |
| R14 | EarlyCSE or GVN after AGXCodeGenPrepare's division expansion (share the reciprocal work of divisions by the same divisor: permute4d's 12 div / rem pairs by 3 runtime sizes) | permute4d 2096 -> 2087 instructions, +0.6% cycles; elsewhere +-noise (G13/G14 tile GEMMs both ways) | the zero-divisor guards (select / umax per element, in separate structured blocks) keep the divisors distinct; not a backend problem |
| R15 | G13: register-blocked scalar SGEMM (4x4 / 8x4 / 4x8 / 8x8 per thread) instead of the tile GEMM with emulated SIMD-group matrices | 0.155-0.171 cycles per element-k per group (8 groups) against 0.072 for agx_sgemm_tile64 on G13G (FMA peak 0.031); 8x8 spills | the emulated-matrix tile kernel is already 2x better; the earlier "10x slower than G14" compared a 4-group makespan |
| R17 | Same A-late order for sgemm `_tb` / `_tatb`, B-early order for `_tb` | sgemm _tb +11% G14G, +17..21% G13G | |
| R18 | TTI arithmetic costs at expansion sizes (f64 add / mul / div 180 / 190 / 280, 32-bit division by a variable 30, 64-bit 120), through the real -O3 pipelines (zoc for the CUDA suite and PTX tests, opt -O3 for agx_libs) | CUDA IR unchanged; agx_libs IR changed in blas / solver only, 0 instructions or cycles different | the libraries emulate f64 in MLIR before LLVM; no cost-driven decision flips |
| R19 | Other codegen options (task #14), all three sets: no machine LICM (cycles +2%, tput -3.5%, sgemm_reg4x4 tput -28% from fewer GPRs: mixed), no machine sink (lb geomean +10.5% / tput +19.5%), no branch folding (~0), no early tail duplication, no machine CSE (lb -1% but +18% tput on index_add), split-spill-mode=size, no post-RA machine LICM (~0) | mixed or worse | machine LICM is a latency-for-registers trade the model splits by metric: **hardware check** `-disable-machine-licm` on sgemm_reg4x4 / conv3x3 |
| R20 | R12 again with runtime unrolling allowed expensive trip counts (strided `i += blockDim` loops), x2 / x4 | spmv_csr -21% / -32% but q8_matvec +71%; lb tput geomean +2.7% / +3.2% | a generic unroll loses; the targeted rule (single-block loops, 1-2 device loads, <= 24 instructions) changes no suite kernel and costs lb tput +0.3% geomean (dnn_bn_stats +73%) |
| R21 | **Launch-time specialization in the runtime** (existing, opt-in: scalar arguments and block size as constants), emulated on the CUDA suite (G14C) | permute4d -85% cycles / -94% tput (2096 -> 46 instructions), attn_decode -24% / -13%; but rmsnorm +81% tput, softmax_online +20%, conv3x3 +16%: constants too wide for immediates land in GPRs (14 -> 21 GPRs, fewer resident groups); not unrolling (an unroll limit changes nothing) | worth enabling only with large constants kept out of GPRs: a launcher-filled constant table in uniform registers (the open R1-E6 direction). **Hardware check**: permute-style and rmsnorm-style kernels with the runtime's specialization on |
| R22 | SimplifyCFG branch folding with more bonus instructions before llc (`simplifycfg<bonus-inst-threshold=2/4>`: argmax's `v > best \|\| (v == best && j < bi)` steps become selects, 195 -> 164 instructions) (L6-E2) | only argmax changes: cycles +1.8%, tput -2.3% (the reductions are not its hot loop) | too little to add a pass |
| R23 | **Off, hardware-gated: element-aligned 8- / 16-bit vector accesses** (L8-E2, `-agx-element-aligned-vectors`): the vectorizer merges half / byte device loads at their element's alignment into one i16 / i8 format access (B33 measured only 32-bit elements at 4 bytes) | both paths: q8_matvec -54.1% cycles, attn_decode -13.8% tput (12 -> 3 loads), q4_matvec 25 -> 13 loads but +5.7% tput, f16 tile GEMMs up to -16.7% tput; rw geomean -3.45% cycles / -0.27% tput, lb -0.77% / -0.35% | **check on hardware** (AGX-Performance-Simulator follow-up task list, item 15): the largest model gain left; wrong results if the hardware needs more alignment |
| R16 | **Atomic optimizer** (`-agx-atomic-optimizer`, off by default; AGXCodeGenPrepare, after AMDGPUAtomicOptimizer): 32-bit int atomics to a uniform address -> one atomic by the first active thread (count x operand when the operand is uniform, else simd reduce), results rebuilt with an exclusive scan (add / sub) and a shuffle | **worse in the model**: cuda_compact 2530 -> 3576 cycles (1 group), 3710 -> 9069 (48 groups); PTX atom_add +68% | agx_cost does not serialize same-address atomics, which real GPUs do (the reason AMDGPU and CUDA compilers aggregate). **Key hardware check**: `kernels/cuda_compact.ll` (counter with result), `reduce_shfl`-style sums, PTX `atom_add`, with and without the option, at full occupancy. Correct on the simulator (PTX-formalization, agx_libs). Float add excluded (a reduction rounds and flushes denormals unlike the atomic) |
| R22 | 64-bit constants (REG_SEQUENCE of two mov_imm) as aligned uniform pairs under A15, so AGXFoldOperands folds them into 64-bit sources | (reverted) | G14C cycles -0.01% geomean, tput -0.1% (spmv_csr -3.2%); G13G +0.57% (spilling tile GEMMs at 128 GPRs swing -15..+62%) | not worth the code; 64-bit constants are rare once A14 handles 32-bit adds |
| R23 | **Preamble (upper bound only)**: share of simulated cycles in instructions whose sources are only uniforms (or values computed from them), which a Mesa-style per-dispatch preamble could compute once | (not implemented) | CUDA suite 2.2% of cycles (philox 58%, permute4d 18.5%, rope 17%, mandelbrot 16%, ddot 5%, nbody 3%; most kernels < 1%) | too small on average for a new dispatch path (preshader USC words, unknown hardware behaviour); philox-style kernels benefit from the runtime's specialization instead. **Hardware check**: none needed yet |

## Experiment: PTX -> upstream AIR -> AGX

A separate path (llvm/docs/AIR/AIRHints.rst): `agx-raise-to-air` writes the PTX front end's AGX IR as upstream AIR with
hints (operations AIR lacks, each with an AIR emulation; soft-f64 above AIR), AIR's portable passes run, and
`agx-lower-air` restores the AGX operations; the same AIR goes to the Metal pipeline. The direct path is
unchanged (asm identical, bench identical, d3 = d4).

| # | Change (llvm commit) | Model result, AIR path vs direct (same `llc` flags) | Correctness |
|---|---|---|---|
| X1 | Raise + hints + soft-f64 above AIR, by-value parameters restored (727052a5d and before) | cycles geomean rw -0.01%, co -0.00%, lb -0.01%; no kernel > 2% slower | PTX-formalization 408 pass = direct |
| X2 | Directed rounding / FP mode / exact min-max emulations (bb72df692, f534ac876) | AGX code unchanged (asm identical on the strict-FP tests) | every hint emulated: 381 -> 399 pass; left: f64 atomics, hardware approximations |
| X3 | System values read where the kernel extracts them (e4934c6dd); scalar ones stay at the entry (reading them where their uses meet: G14C +0.02%, rejected) | rw 72/72 identical (was 64), co 474/476, lb 434/468 (tile GEMMs reading the SIMD lane); G14C geomean -0.007% | 408 pass |
| X5 | Scalar system values (SIMD lane, SIMD-group index, thread index) through a `+value` hint at the read, whose emulation is AIR's parameter; AGX reads the register at the hint | **every kernel identical to direct**: rw 72/72, co 476/476, lb 468/468 | 407 pass native (tex-mip: the debug zoc's 121 s vs the checker's 120 s, both paths); emulated 404; Metal 246/248 |
| X6 | **Portable AIR**: every hint but the matrix FMA becomes its AIR emulation, inlined and folded at the use (`-agx-raise-hints`, default the matrix FMA); f64 atomics as 64-bit cmpxchg loops; `strictfp` dropped. AGX recovers what it can from plain AIR (`recoverAGXOperations`: afn 1/x, 1/sqrt, sin(x pi/2) as rcp / rsqrt / quadrant sin; the fmin-with-NaN-check idiom as minimum, in bfloat / half; `.ftz` flushes where the f32 ALU flushes). Measured first per kind (`-agx-air-emulate-hints`): system values +-0.01%, f64 -0.25% (faster), approximations rw +0.9% (nbody +36% tput), min/max up to +72%, FP mode co +0.9% (add_ftz +310%), matrix G14 GEMMs +15% (kept) | cycles geomean rw +0.29% (ddot +11%, its tput -1.2%), co -0.01% (call_rnd +73%, cvt_f64_f32 -36%), lb -0.01%; 2 of 248 modules keep a hint | 408 pass, with the matrix hint native and emulated; Metal 246/248 |
| L1-E1 | **Generic pointers as device pointers in AIR** when nothing in the module makes a generic pointer to other memory (cvta.shared / .local / .param) or calls through a pointer: AGX treats exactly those as device pointers (AGXLowerGenericAddresses), so the AIR is portable and Metal-correct, and AGX's alias analysis and load/store vectorizer now see device accesses (the direct path does not vectorize generic ones). 241 of 245 modules drop the generic hint. Store width limits tried (8 / 4 bytes, `-agx-max-store-vector-bytes`): lb +1.5% at 4, kept 16 | vs direct: co cycles -4.19% / tput -2.50% geomean (130 better, div_ftz -38%); rw / lb unchanged; mma_m16n8k32_s32_s8_s8_s32_interleave +300% (16 stores merged into 4 extend live ranges to 128 GPRs: open) | 408 pass; Metal 246/248 |
| L1-E2 | **Directed rounding of float add / sub / mul by error-free transformations** (TwoSum, the fma product error; Graillat, Lamotte, Nguyen Hong; Rump, Ogita, Oishi): the nearest result, the sign of its exact error, one unit in the direction; operands and result in [2^-70, 2^127) or zero (so the error is normal on a flushing ALU), exact zero sums by the IEEE sign rule; the rest soft-f64. The branch tests `ok or rounding is nearest`, so it folds away where the mode is known | call_rnd -60% cycles / -77% tput (305 cycles: below the direct path's 590, AGX's integer directed rounding); nothing else changes | 408 pass; host check (`tools/check_directed_eft.c`): 0 failures in 11.7M fast-path cases |
| L1-E4 | soft_f64.c add: denormal operands as exponent 1 without the implicit bit instead of normalized (two clz and shifts fewer; round_pack normalizes the sum) | fadd 195 -> ~171 instructions (378 cycles, direct 392), ddot +11% -> +3.4% against direct; rw geomean -0.19%, co -0.08% | soft_f64 host check 2M cases, f64 / testfloat PTX tests pass |
| L3-E1 | Store vectorization limited by register pressure in AGX TTI (A18; both paths) | co -5.18% cycles / -3.30% tput geomean vs direct (was -4.19% / -2.50%), interleave +300% -> -75% vs its L1-E1 form | 408 pass (tex-mip alone: under -j 4 the debug zoc passes the 120 s limit, as before) |
| L4-E1 | **Vector accesses shaped for AGX** (AGXVectorAccesses, around the load/store vectorizer): a vector load of which only some lanes are read (PTX `ld.v4` into `_`) loads only those lanes, so the vectorizer can merge it with its neighbours; a device vector store whose lanes are lanes of loads in another order (a swap, two loads) is split into the runs that need no register copy (a store right after the copy waits ~34 cycles). Threadgroup stores keep the vector: their split needs a second address register (bitonic sort +3.9%) | AIR path: ld_st_offset -36.8% cycles, sink -22.2% tput, co geomean -0.37% cycles / -0.19% tput, nothing worse; direct path unchanged (its generic accesses are not vectorized) | 408 pass on both paths (`-agx-narrow-vector-loads=0`, `-agx-split-permuted-stores=0` switch it off) |
| L4-E2 | **Rejected (flag, off): AIR's software f64 calls as AGX's double operations** (`-agx-air-native-f64`: agx-lower-air turns round-to-nearest `__air_f64_*` calls into fadd / fmul / fma / fpext / ... for AGXSoftF64). The portable library is as fast: ddot -3.3% cycles but +6.2% tput (52 -> 58 GPRs), fmul / fdiv faster, fadd / sqrt / fma tput / f64 atomics / cvt_f64_f32 slower (+4% .. +107%); co geomean +0.31% cycles / +0.50% tput | the AIR soft-f64 stays | -- |
| L4-E3 | **Adopted: short paths for double precision arithmetic rounded to nearest** (AGXSoftF64; agx-lower-air recovers `__air_f64_{add,sub,mul,fma,div,sqrt}` by default, `-agx-air-native-f64-arith`). Normal or zero operands with a normal (or exactly zero) result: the product's top bit at one of two known places (no normalization), one 128-bit alignment and one normalization for fma, the quotient / root without normalizing, rounding without the denormal, overflow and special cases; everything else in a conditional block with the general code. The L4-E2 trade-off reversed for these six; comparisons, conversions and rounding stay the library's (AGX's are slower). AGX Performance Simulator, busiest-core cycles over 32,768 threads of normal operands: dmul -30.2%, dfma -22.2%, dadd -11.1%, ddiv -16.5%, rcp -12.3%, dsqrt -4.6%; others unchanged (without the zero cases: -32.6 / -26.6 / -17.5%, but every zero operand took the general code too: agx-bench ddot, whose memory reads as zero, was +56% cycles; now -8.3% cycles, -8.7% tput at 70 GPRs instead of 55). The slow paths are marked unlikely. At most 256 operations per function get a short path (`-agx-soft-f64-fast-paths-max`): strict mode's exact texture sampler (~2000 in one kernel) took 764 MB to compile with them, 544 MB before; with the cap 545 MB, and 24 s instead of 42 s (the recovered double operations compile faster than the library calls). All 413 test modules: 342 -> 319 s, peak 544 -> 545 MB. Exact against Python on 12,288 add/sub/mul, 12,288 fma and 12,288 div/sqrt cases each (denormals, overflow, cancellation, signed zeros); PTX suites strict 872 pass, default unchanged | adopted | -- |
| L15-E1 | **Adopted: 64-bit division and remainder by Newton-Raphson** (AGXCodeGenPrepare, `-agx-newton-div64`). The 64-iteration shift-subtract loop became an f32 reciprocal estimate kept below 2^64 / y, two Newton steps in integers, a multiply-high and two corrections without control flow, a division and remainder of the same operands sharing one (a loop would be rewritten into a division by LSR); constant divisors use floor(2^64 / y). At most 4 expansions per function (`-agx-newton-div64-max`): each is ~200 bytes more than the loop whether or not its 64-bit case runs, and permute4d (6 divisions whose 32-bit case is the one that runs) grew past 16 KB of code, +24% tput; it keeps the loop. Simulator, one SIMD group: div.u64 3435 -> 946 cycles, rem.s64 3421 -> 973 | adopted | -- |
| L15-E2 | **Adopted: 64-bit bit reversal on the halves** (`bitrev` of each half, swapped) instead of the generic shift-and-mask expansion: 45 -> 2 instructions | adopted | -- |
| L15-E3 | **Adopted: float fma with directed rounding from the hardware fma** (AGXStrictFP fastFma): RN(a x + y) corrected by the sign of its exact error, from Boldo and Muller's ErrFma (TwoProd and two TwoSums; IEEE TC 2011), the integer computation in an unlikely block where the operands could make an intermediate denormal or overflow. Simulator, one SIMD group: fma.rp.f32 899 -> 705 cycles (192 -> 61 instructions); exact against Python on 6144 cases x rz/rp/rm, strict | adopted | -- |
| L15-E4 | **Adopted: wide atomics of one address per SIMD group take the lock once** (AGXExpandWideAtomics, `-agx-wide-atomics-batch`): the lowest thread takes the lock and reads, every thread applies the group's operations in lane order (a serialization, exact for f64 and cmpxchg too), the lowest writes and frees it; other groups keep the per-thread loop. agx-lower-air compiles air-emulate-f64's cmpxchg loop for f64 atomic add / sub back into an atomicrmw. Simulator, 32 threads on one address: atom.add.f64 329,453 -> 10,749 cycles, add.u64 18,569 -> 3,583, cas.b64 19,497 -> 4,009, min.s64 19,721 -> 3,736, exch.b64 17,232 -> 3,352; distinct addresses: add.f64 3,196 -> 2,530, add.u64 / cas.b64 +4% (the uniformity check) | adopted | -- |
| L15-E5 | **Rejected: the same batch for 32-bit atomics AtomicExpand makes compare-and-swap loops of** (f16x2 / bf16 adds, f32 min / max, f32 adds on threadgroup memory: the lowest thread reads, every thread applies the group's operations in lane order, one compare-and-swap of the result). Exact, but contended f16x2 add 7,802 -> 3,234 cycles while contended f32 max 1,192 -> 2,662 (the loop's compare-and-swaps mostly succeed at once for a max) and an uncontended f16x2 add 797 -> 1,280 (+60%: the uniformity check, two shuffles and two ballots before the atomic, costs more than such a short operation) | rejected | -- |
| L15-E6 | **Adopted: short paths for double conversions** (AGXSoftF64; agx-lower-air recovers `__air_f64_{from_f32,to_f32,to_i64,to_u64,from_i64,from_u64}`): float to double for normal or zero floats, double to float rounded to nearest for normal results, truncation to integers that fit, integers below 2^53 exactly (32-bit ones always, no branch). Kept as library calls: float conversions in functions that flush float denormals (the library keeps them bit-exact; the subnormal-preserving shared float atomic is emulated with them), and every conversion in a function with more than 256 soft-f64 calls (`-agx-air-native-f64-conversions-max`: AGX's general conversions are larger than the library's; tex-mip 545 -> 564 MB without the cap). Simulator, busiest core, 32,768 threads: f64 -> s64 -> f64 2,152 -> 1,677 (-22%), f64 -> f32 -> f64 1,744 -> 1,522 (-12.7%), f64 -> s32 -> f64 2,611 -> 2,394 (-8.3%) | adopted | -- |
| L15-E8 | **Adopted: 64-bit multiply-high from four multiply-adds**: UMUL_LOHI / SMUL_LOHI i32 as one 32 x 32 -> 64-bit imadd (the i64 expansions took each low half from a second, 32-bit imadd); MULHU i64 as t = a1 b0 + hi(a0 b0), u = a0 b1 + lo(t), a1 b1 + hi(t) + hi(u) (no sum overflows, so the sums ride in imadd's 64-bit addend: new add(mul_wide, c) patterns) instead of the generic expansion's carry compares; MULHS i64 as mulhu - (a < 0 ? b : 0) - (b < 0 ? a : 0). mul.hi.u64 19 -> 11 instructions, mul.hi.s64 23 -> 18. AGXSoftF64's 64 x 64 -> 128 products and the 64-bit division use them: simulator (32,768 threads, busiest core) mul.hi.u64 1,129 -> 922 (-18%), div.u64 3,688 -> 3,065 (-17%), rem.s64 3,930 -> 3,348 (-15%), sqrt.rn.f64 7,035 -> 6,218 (-11.6%), rcp.rn.f64 -10%, div.rn.f64 -9.5%, mul.f64 -4.7%, fma.f64 -1.9%. agx-bench unchanged (A/B against the previous llc: no kernel differs). New exactness test ptx/tools/agx_emulation_tests/mulhi64.ptx (512 pairs, mul.hi.u64 / s64, mul.lo.u64) | adopted | -- |
| L15-E9 | **Baseline refresh, regressions attributed** (baseline.json had not been updated since 2f8fa0dcd6): every cycle / tput regression against it (gelu_f16 +16% cycles, softmax_row +8%, rope +4.7%, layernorm_f16 / rmsnorm tput +2-4%) goes away with `fdiv arcp` in the kernels, i.e. it is the correctly rounded float division of 397c1c785d (PTX div.rn; the hardware rcp is off by one ulp for 19% of significands per the simulator's recorded table, so the Newton step stays). The rest are GPR increases with unchanged cycles and tput (q8_matvec 40 -> 55, permute4d 46 -> 60, ddot 58 -> 70, q4_matvec 46 -> 47): the machine scheduler's latency hiding; `-enable-misched=false` takes q8_matvec to 18 GPRs but 3,929 -> 11,028 cycles (q4_matvec 2.6x, attn_decode 2.4x, spmv_csr +74%) | baseline updated | -- |
| L15-E10 | **Rejected: float conversions recovered in flushing functions too**, lowered to AGXSoftF64's integer code at once (so that no fold can narrow them to flushing float arithmetic, the reason they are skipped there): correct (atom-f32-ftz, atom-noftz-f32 pass in both modes) but slower on the kernels that have them: atom-noftz-f32 4,398 -> 5,230 cycles (subnormal values, the slow path), 2,707 -> 2,855 with normal values (+5%). Also measured: cvt.rn.ftz.f32.f64 in a micro kernel looked 2.2x the plain conversion only because random 64-bit inputs overflow / underflow float (the short path's misses); the front end emits the .ftz flush explicitly | rejected | -- |
| L15-E11 | **Adopted: 32-bit atomic increment / decrement with wrap of one address take one compare-and-swap per SIMD group** (AGXExpandWideAtomics, `-agx-inc-dec-batch`; CUDA's atomicInc / atomicDec are uinc_wrap / udec_wrap, compare-and-swap loops on AGX): where the group's active threads all access one address, the lowest reads, every thread applies the group's operations in lane order (each with its own limit), the lowest compare-and-swaps the result and the group retries together on failure; other groups keep the loop; monotonic only. Simulator, 32,768 threads: one address atom.inc.u32 756,272 -> 6,512 cycles (116x), atom.dec.u32 756,274 -> 7,116; distinct addresses 1,176 -> 1,719 (+46%, the uniformity check). Unlike L15-E5's general 32-bit batch, a contended counter is the usual use of these two. New exactness test agx_emulation_tests/atomic-incdec.ptx. (Correction to L15-E7: the default-mode failure set was unchanged; the two tests reported there as newly passing were outside that run's file set) | adopted | -- |
| L15-E12 | **Adopted: 8- and 16-bit division and remainder from the float reciprocal** (AGXCodeGenPrepare, `-agx-narrow-divisions`): they went to LLVM's generic expansion, a bit-at-a-time loop. The magnitudes (at most 2^16) are exact floats, so trunc(x rcp(y)) is within 2^-6 of x / y and so within one of the quotient; one correction of the remainder either way makes it exact (host check of every unsigned 16-bit pair, rcp at -1 / 0 / +1 ulp of the rounded reciprocal: no difference). Zero divisor: all ones (remainder x), as the 32-bit expansion. Simulator, 32,768 threads: rem.u16 2,214 -> 895 cycles (-60%), div.s16 2,012 -> 984 (-51%). New exactness test agx_emulation_tests/div16.ptx (s8 / u8 / s16 / u16, 1,024 pairs each) | adopted | -- |
| L15-E13 | **Adopted: L15-E11's batch for every 32-bit compare-and-swap-loop atomic** (`-agx-cas-batch`, was `-agx-inc-dec-batch`; not in agx-strict mode): f16x2 / bf16x2 additions, float min / max, inc / dec, any 32-bit atomicrmw AtomicExpand makes a loop of. L15-E5 rejected this measuring 32 threads; at 32,768 threads on one address (simulator, busiest core): atom.add.noftz.f16x2 479,133 -> 11,048 cycles (43x), atom.add.noftz.bf16x2 147,787 -> 14,928 (10x), atom.max.f32 80,979 -> 6,239 (13x), atom.inc.u32 6,512 as before; distinct addresses +40-46% (f16x2 1,272 -> 1,828, max.f32 1,176 -> 1,719: the uniformity check, about 16 instructions). Tried: one compare-and-swap first and batching only the threads that failed (`-agx-cas-batch-try-first`): no check when uncontended (inc 1,297), but on one address each group's 32 tries serialize (inc 97,472, f16x2 101,609): off. Also tried: the uniformity check by min / max reductions of the address words instead of a shuffle and ballots: no faster. 16-bit atomics (atom.add.f16, atom.cas.b16, partword loops) not covered | adopted | -- |
| L15-E14 | **Adopted: the batch for 16-bit atomics; 8- / 16-bit atomic loads fixed**: a 16-bit atomicrmw (atom.add.f16 / bf16, 16-bit integer ops: AtomicExpand's partword loops) batches on its aligned word, each thread's operation on its half (simulator, 32,768 threads on one address: atom.add.noftz.f16 479,139 -> 10,417 cycles, 46x; own addresses 1,286 -> 1,753). And 8- / 16-bit atomic loads (ld.volatile / ld.relaxed / ld.acquire .b8 / .b16) failed instruction selection: AtomicExpand makes atomic loads compare-and-swaps and does not widen the narrow ones it makes; they now read their aligned word atomically. New exactness test agx_emulation_tests/atomic-16bit.ptx (f16 adds and cas.b16 counters on both halves of a word) | adopted | -- |
| L15-E15 | **Adopted: no NaN test when rounding a float arithmetic result to bfloat** (AGXISelLowering lowerFP_TO_BF16): the f32 add / sub / mul / fma's NaN is 0x7fc00000 (the simulator's fp32 semantics), which the rounding addition keeps a NaN, so bf16 / bf16x2 arithmetic (computed in f32) skips the test and select; not with the canonical-nan feature (strict mode's 0x7fffffff would carry out). Simulator (32,768 threads): add.rn.bf16x2 1,066 -> 971 cycles, fma.rn.bf16x2 1,218 -> 1,116, fma.rn.bf16 811 -> 759. Default-mode failure messages identical | adopted | -- |
| L15-E16 | **Adopted: 8- / 16-bit, fp8 / fp6 / fp4 and approx-function work (PTX front end and AGX)**: 8- / 16-bit div / rem from the float reciprocal (L15-E12); the narrow-format conversions round once with a bias addition and decode subnormals through the f32 of the integer mantissa (e4m3 round trip 1,665 -> 1,347 cycles, e2m1 1,665 -> 1,343, e5m2 1,222 -> 1,119; exhaustive host checks); tanh.approx divides by the reciprocal (f16x2 1,341 -> 971); rcp / rsqrt.approx.ftz.f64 from 21-bit integers (4,402 -> 1,470, 9,986 -> 1,706) | adopted | -- |
| L15-E17 | **Adopted: the batch for 16-bit compare-and-swaps** (atom.cas.b16, a partword loop): in lane order each thread's half is compared with what it expects and replaced on a match; the old half and the success per thread. Simulator, 32,768 threads on one address: 46,706 -> 10,127 cycles; own addresses 1,277 -> 1,761 | adopted | -- |
| L15-E18 | **Adopted (PTX front end): SIMD video add / sub / average on the whole word (SWAR)**: vadd2/4, vsub2/4 (wrapping, unsigned or signed saturated) and unsigned vavrg2/4 with the lanes in place: vsub4.s32.sat 1,250 -> 816 cycles, vavrg4.u32 993 -> 679, vadd4.u32.sat 930 -> 799. Rejected: minimum / maximum / absolute difference and the .add (lane sum) forms by SWAR: vabsdiff4.add 935 -> 1,032 (the borrow masks cost more than a lane at a time) | adopted | -- |
| L15-E19 | **Adopted: division by constants through multiply-high for 8- / 16- and 64-bit**: 8- / 16-bit div / rem by a constant become the 32-bit ones of the extended operands (a multiply-high: u16 / 10 18 -> 8 instructions, s8 / 7 21 -> 11; div.s16 by -7 856 -> 783 cycles, rem.u16 by 10 757 -> 738), and 64-bit ones are left to instruction selection's multiply-high now that the 64-bit high product is four multiply-adds (L15-E8) instead of the Newton expansion's constant path (`-agx-div64-const-mulhi`): u64 / 10 35 -> 16 instructions, s64 / 7 43 -> 22, u64 % 1000 35 -> 25; div.u64 by 10 1,329 -> 954 cycles, rem.s64 by -7 1,424 -> 1,317. New exactness tests div16c.ptx (7 constants) and div64k.ptx (15 constants: small, large, 2^32 + 1, 2^64 - 1, 2^63 + 1, -1, ...) | adopted | -- |
| L15-E20 | **Adopted: soft-f64 short paths' checks and the double-to-float conversion in 32 bits**: the normal-exponent tests (operands and the rounded result) took the 64-bit field (64-bit subtract and compare); the field is in the high word. The short path of double -> float did everything in 64 bits for a 32-bit result; now the exponent from the high word, the 23 kept bits as a funnel of both, the rest from the low word. Simulator (32,768 threads): mul.f64 2,058 -> 1,918, fma.f64 4,903 -> 4,632, add.f64 2,375 -> 2,242, div.f64 4,397 -> 4,215, rcp.f64 3,836 -> 3,703, sqrt.f64 6,218 -> 6,139, f64 -> f32 -> f64 1,522 -> 1,355, and with float -> double built from 32-bit words too (extFast: 23 -> 19 instructions) 1,255 | adopted | -- |
| L15-E21 | **Adopted: directed float -> half by the hardware's conversion plus a unit, and no bfloat rounding of bfloat-exact values**: cvt.rz / rm / rp.f16.f32 (strict mode) took the integer conversion of the unpacked float; now the hardware's round-to-nearest half, widened back and compared with the float (exact: a half is a float), moved one unit toward the direction when it went past (sign kept; overflow to infinity becomes the largest finite half toward zero), and a float denormal (flushed by the hardware) becomes a signed zero or the smallest half denormal away from zero: cvt.rz.f16.f32 1,005 -> 727 cycles, rm 1,343 -> 777, rp 1,353 -> 788. Rounding a float to bfloat is skipped when the float is already a bfloat (a widened bfloat, a constant, a min / max / select / sign operation of such): max.bf16x2 1,138 -> 962, fma.rn.relu.bf16x2 1,433 -> 1,185. Checked: f16_directed.ptx (4,096 floats in four directions, exact rationals) in both modes, strict 870 PASS, default failures unchanged (AGXStrictFP.cpp, AGXISelLowering.cpp; strict-f16-directed.ll, bf16-round-arith.ll) | adopted | -- |
| L15-E22 | **Adopted: the clamp to [0, 1] as the hardware's .sat, and .relu as two compare-selects (PTX front end)**: minimumnum(maximumnum(x, +0), 1), which is PTX .sat (a NaN and -0 become +0), was two hardware min / max with their signed-zero fixes (4 compare-selects per lane); it is now the .sat output modifier of the fma / add / mul that produced x, or an add of -0 (halves always; floats only outside ieee-fp32, as .sat flushes float denormals): fma.sat.f16x2 850 -> 745 cycles, the same as a plain fma. The PTX front end's .relu of f16 / bf16 / f32 on AIR is x > 0 ? x : +0 and the canonical NaN of x, instead of a NaN-propagating maximum (its own NaN test and signed-zero fix) and then the canonical NaN: fma.rn.relu.bf16x2 1,185 -> 1,144, fma.rn.relu.f16x2 838 -> 787 (f64 keeps the maximum: its NaN payloads are kept, which a first build got wrong, 10 strict cvt tests). Rejected: min / max.NaN as minimumnum and one NaN test of the operands: min.NaN.f16x2 799 -> 801. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E23 | **Adopted: float -> bfloat's NaN test as a float compare, and one shift**: the rounding (x + 0x7fff + lsb) >> 16 and the quieted NaN (x >> 16) | 0x40 were selected by |x| > 0x7f800000 (a magnitude and an integer compare); now x unordered with itself (one compare-select), selecting between the 32-bit words x | 0x400000 and x + 0x7fff + lsb, then one shift: 8 -> 6 instructions a value. cvt.rn.bf16x2.f32 835 -> 778 cycles, ex2.approx.ftz.bf16x2 1,074 -> 978. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E24 | **Adopted: directed float add / mul / fma fast paths with fewer checks and a 3-instruction step**: add / sub's validity (operands zero or in [2^-102, 2^126)) tested each operand (magnitude, range, zero) and the sum's finiteness, which the operand bound implies (all values below 1.5 * 2^127); now both operands at once, the smaller magnitude less one (a zero wraps to the largest) and the larger one (umin / umax). The step one unit in the direction took |S|, a select of the error's sign, both neighbors and the sign put back; now the error with S's sign xored in, on S's bits, and for a known up / down direction a +-1 chosen by S's sign where the error lies beyond S: add.rz.f32 950 -> 867 cycles, mul.rm.f32 1,094 -> 991, fma.rp.f32 1,254 -> 1,137 (plain add 569). Checked: new generated f32_addmul_directed (PTX front end, 3 x 2,048 pairs, add / sub / mul in three directions, exact rationals) and fma-directed in both modes. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E25 | **Adopted: 32-bit unsigned division by a PTX front end's umax(d, 1) without the maximum**: the front end divides by umax(d, 1) and selects its own result where d = 0; the expansion took the maximum and then tested its never-zero value for zero. Now the reciprocal is of d, the result where d = 0 is that of dividing by one (x, or 0 for a remainder), and where the only use is a select on d = 0 that select stands alone: div.u32 22 -> 20 instructions, 946 -> 923 cycles. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E26 | **Adopted: no signed-zero fix for minimumnum / maximumnum with a nonzero constant**: the hardware min / max leave equal operands as they are, and the fix (an and / or of the bits where they compare equal) is for zeros of two signs; with a nonzero constant operand (a clamp) equal operands have the same bits. The saturating conversion to s8 clamps with one compare-select per bound: cvt.rni.sat.s8.f32 13 -> 9 instructions, 694 -> 652 cycles. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E27 | **Adopted (fixes L15-E13's cost): the compare-and-swap batch only for a uniform address outside divergent branches, with no check**: the batch needs every active thread on one address, which in practice is a uniform one; its runtime check (a shuffle of the address, two ballots) cost every divergent-address atomic +40-46% and, on agx-g13g (no native float atomic add), agx-bench's reduce +36% cycles, reduce_shfl +22%, index_add +33% (L15-E13 had measured agx-bench on g14c only). Now a divergent address, or an atomic under a divergent branch (dominated by a block with a divergent terminator it does not post-dominate: a reduction's if (tid == 0)), keeps AtomicExpand's loop, and a uniform one batches without the check. Distinct addresses: atom.add.noftz.f16x2 1,828 -> 1,272 cycles, atom.max.f32 1,719 -> 1,176 (as before L15-E13); one address: f16x2 11,048 -> 10,869, max.f32 6,239 -> 6,103; agx-bench back to its baseline. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E28 | **Adopted: signed bytes on the ALUs**: sext_inreg i8 (bfi + asr) and sra(shl(x, 24 - p), 24) (two shifts) run on the unit that integer multiplies, bit-field operations, shifts and conversions share (one per scheduler half in the simulator's model); now the byte (an and, or one bfeil at p > 0) and (f ^ 128) - 128 on the ALUs, and a 16-bit field its shift and a sign-extending addition. dp4a.s32.s32 1,266 -> 1,049 cycles, dp2a 895 -> 811; agx-bench unchanged. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E29 | **Adopted: 16-bit halves packed into a 32-bit register as its halves**: or(zext lo, zext hi << 16) (the result of every f16x2 / bf16x2 / 16-bit SIMD operation and of 16-bit values in 32-bit lanes) took two extensions, a shift and an or (two of them on the shared multiply / bit-field unit); it is now a REG_SEQUENCE of the two 16-bit registers, as are (x & 0xffff) | (y << 16), (x & 0xffff) | (y & 0xffff0000), (x >> 16) | (y & 0xffff0000) and (x >> 16) | (y << 16) of 32-bit values (their 16-bit halves; a 16-bit move where the register allocator cannot place them). mul.f16x2 688 -> 632 cycles, min.f16x2 750 -> 694, fma.rn.f16x2 745 -> 700, cvt.rn.f16x2.f32 694 -> 640, cvt.rn.bf16x2.f32 778 -> 741, add.bf16x2 971 -> 936, fma.rn.relu.bf16x2 1,144 -> 1,103, add.s16 649 -> 624, vmax2 643 -> 596; agx-bench unchanged. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E30 | **Adopted (PTX front end): fp8 e4m3 / fp6 / fp4 to f16 without normalizing subnormals**: the element's fields placed in the f16's with the exponent rebiased give a normal element's f16; a subnormal element (exponent 0) is then f = 2^-bias (1 + m) and its value 2^(1 - bias) m = 2 f - 2^(1 - bias), one exact f16 fma, in place of the field arithmetic and the integer-to-float normalization of the subnormal mantissa. e4m3 round trip (encode + decode) 1,347 -> 1,007 cycles, e2m1 1,343 -> 1,087. Tried first: the fields in place times 2^(15 - bias) in f16, which takes f16 subnormals as operands: the f16 multiply flushed them (e2m1 0.5 became 0). Not done: encoding through the hardware's f32 -> f16 conversion (rounding twice to nearest, wrong for 24-bit inputs near a midpoint) or by adding an exponent-dependent constant (as many instructions). | adopted | -- |
| L15-E31 | **Rejected: a bfloat widened to float as a zero low half under its 16 bits (REG_SEQUENCE) instead of a shift**: bf16 -> f32 is x << 16, one bfi on the shared multiply / bit-field unit; as the register's halves it is a 16-bit move and a zero (often a copy of it). Packed operations gain where the shared unit is the bottleneck (fma.rn.bf16x2 1,074 -> 963 cycles, fma.rn.relu.bf16x2 1,103 -> 990, max.bf16x2 935 -> 867, though 36 -> 41 instructions), but scalar ones lose (fma.rn.bf16 721 -> 751, setp.lt.bf16 642 -> 675), and both take their bfloats from halves of one loaded word, so the two cannot be told apart at lowering. Generic x << 16 the same way: the same split. | rejected | -- |
| L15-E32 | **Adopted: bytes packed into a word as bit-field inserts**: or(q, x << s) whose possibly-set bits are one byte-aligned field of q's known zeros becomes one bfi of x into q (AGXISD::BFI_FIELD, with known bits for chains), and a 16-bit value under such an assembled low part the register's high half (AGXISD::PACK16): cvt.pack.sat.s8.s32.b32's packing 6 -> 3 instructions, 784 -> 745 cycles; prmt with a variable selector 1,233 -> 1,222. Only byte-aligned fields and not funnel shifts ((y >> (32 - s)) | (x << s), y perhaps a 64-bit shift's low word, one extr): every field at first cost soft-f64 1-2% (dmul 1,918 -> 1,951, rsqrt.f64 9,061 -> 9,256: extr and the free shift of iadd replaced). agx-bench unchanged. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E33 | **Adopted: x << s, s at most 4, as iadd 0, x, lsl s**: a shift is a bfi on the shared multiply / bit-field unit; the addition's free shift of its second source does it on the ALU. Soft-f64 1-1.5% (dmul 1,918 -> 1,896 cycles, ddiv 4,215 -> 4,175, dsqrt 6,139 -> 6,046, rsqrt.f64 9,061 -> 8,991), vadd4 799 -> 791; rcp.approx.f64 1,470 -> 1,475, cas.b16 on one address 9,857 -> 9,905; agx-bench unchanged. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E34 | **Rejected: bytes and 16-bit integers to float on the ALUs**: x in the mantissa of 1.5 * 2^23 (signed) or 2^23 (unsigned) then that constant subtracted, exact, instead of a convert on the shared unit. A byte is sign-extended in its 16-bit register first (bfi + asr), and with the integer and float additions the conversion becomes 3-5 instructions: q8_matvec 3,929 -> 4,570 cycles, blur_rgba8 and q4_matvec more registers. The conversion stays. | rejected | -- |
| L15-E35 | **Adopted: masked parts of one shifted value merged in an or tree**: a byte permute with a constant selector (prmt / __byte_perm through the generic helper) folds to one masked part per byte, or-ed in a chain where parts of one value and shift are not adjacent, so InstCombine leaves them apart; AGXCodeGenPrepare now flattens single-use 32-bit or trees and merges (x op c) & m1 and (x op c) & m2 into (x op c) & (m1 | m2), after which selection sees halves of registers and bit-field inserts: prmt.b32 0x5410 (the low halves of a and b) 5 instructions -> 1 (a 16-bit move). Selectors with a different shift per byte (0x6420, 0x7531) are unchanged. agx-bench unchanged. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E36 | **Adopted (PTX front end): lop3 with a constant table synthesized**: the sum of the eight table-masked minterms, which InstCombine does not minimize (0x96, a ^ b ^ c, eight instructions), becomes a two-input operation where the function has at most two inputs, T(g(u, v), w) (two) where one exists over all input orders, else f0 ^ ((f0 ^ f1) & w) (four). All 256 tables on 128 triples (lop3.ptx): 541 -> 467 instructions, 26,382 -> 8,277 cycles. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E37 | **Adopted: fshl / rotl as one extr**: they were expanded to two shifts and an or (shf.l.wrap 5 instructions); extr by 32 - (s mod 32), whose amount of 32 gives the high word as s = 0 needs, is 3 (1 for a constant amount). shf.ptx (every amount 0..63, both modes) passes; its kernel 638 -> 627 cycles; agx-bench unchanged. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E38 | **Adopted: L15-E25 for signed division**: the front end guards a signed divisor as d + 1 <u 2 ? 1 : d (d = -1 ? 1 : d where d is known nonzero) and selects its own results for d = -1 and d = 0; the expansion now takes d, and with those selects in place (through two single-use selects) no select of its own, else the result of dividing by one where the guard took 1. div.s32 47 -> 44 instructions, rem.s32 42 -> 39; s32div 1,059 -> 1,040 cycles. New div32.ptx (PTX front end: div / rem .u32 / .s32 of all pairs of edge values and random ones) passes before and after in both modes. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E39 | **Adopted: a scheduled region that costs resident threads gets its original order back**: the generic scheduler sees register pressure only against the whole file (256 halves), not the occupancy steps below it (all 1,024 threads up to 52 registers, then 896, 832, ...); on large straight-line blocks its latency ordering kept dozens more values live (spc: 43 registers unscheduled, 70 scheduled). AGXScheduleDAGMILive measures the region's peak GPR16 pressure before and after; where the new order needs more halves and allows fewer threads (AGXSubtarget::maxThreadsForHalves), it schedules the region again in its original order (a top-down strategy by original position, without the clustering mutations, whose edges need not hold there), as AMDGPU reverts a region that lowered occupancy. A first version that moved the instructions back by hand broke subregister liveness (handleMove asserted); one that kept the mutations did not reproduce the order (tc-pack16 76 -> 93 registers). Across the 1,472 PTX-formalization kernels (strict mode) none loses occupancy and 39 gain: spc 70 -> 43 registers (704 -> 1,024 threads), tc-pack16 76 -> 51 (640 -> 1,024), mma.m8n8k4 f16f32 122-126 -> 102-104 (384 -> 512), mma-5 122 -> 116; agx-bench and the micro benchmarks unchanged. `-agx-sched-keep-occupancy`. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E40 | **Adopted (occupancy): L15-E28's signed bytes on the ALUs only for the low byte**: the field forms (an extract, an xor, a subtraction for bytes 1-3) raised the int8 matrix kernels' registers enough to cost threads (wmma-2 78 -> 84, 640 -> 576 threads; mma-4 96 -> 105, 512 -> 448); the low byte alone (an and in place of a bfi) keeps every kernel's occupancy (wmma-2 78 -> 80, the same 640): dp4a 1,266 -> 1,167 cycles instead of 1,049, dp2a 895 -> 832. `-agx-signed-bytes-alu` (0 / 1 / 2). Also: PTX bfi with a constant field (base masked by exactly the field's complement) is one bfi at any offset (20 -> 17 instructions; soft-f64 df32ftz / f16f64 +0.2%). | adopted | -- |
| L15-E41 | **Adopted: forms that trade registers for speed where a function has register headroom**: agxEstimatePressure (L3-E1's IR liveness estimate, now shared and cached per function in AGXMachineFunctionInfo) at most -agx-headroom-gprs (24) lets a function take the all-bytes ALU sign extension (L15-E28); others keep the low-byte form (L15-E40). dp4a 1,167 -> 1,049 cycles, dp2a 832 -> 811 again, while the int8 matrix kernels (estimates far above 24) keep their registers; no PTX-formalization kernel changes occupancy. `-agx-signed-bytes-alu=3` (the default). The same gate can carry other register-for-speed choices. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E42 | **Adopted: strict bfloat fma in float where that is exact**: --strict-fp's bfloat fma was always the exact software fma (straight-line, about 600 instructions): fma.rn.bf16x2 9,278 cycles against 1,074 in the default mode, whatever the inputs. Now the product of normal bfloats (exact in float), TwoSum's error of the sum with the addend (all zero or in [2^-102, 2^126)), the sum rounded to odd, then to nearest bfloat (float keeps 16 bits more) behind an unlikely branch to the software fma. Realistic inputs (|x| in 2^-10..2^10): 9,278 -> 2,298 cycles; the random-bit micro benchmark, where nearly every SIMD group has a lane out of range and so takes both paths: 9,278 -> 10,790. New strict/bf16_fma.ptx (PTX front end: 4,096 triples with subnormals, edge exponents and cancellations, exact rationals) passes before and after; registers 32-38 -> 34-36 (no occupancy change). `-agx-strict-bf16-fast-fma`. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E43 | **Adopted: strict canonical NaNs made on the bits as one fcmpsel; no software path for a strict f32 addition of a large constant**: the front end's --strict-fp canonicalizes NaN results on the bits (Metal may take a float self-comparison for true), `select((bits & 0x7fff) > 0x7c00, 0x7fff, bits)` or InstCombine's `select(bits(fabs x) > inf, NaN, x)`: an and and an icmpsel with a constant per lane. AGXStrictFP now makes it `select(fcmp uno x, x)` (AGX's comparisons see NaNs: one fcmpsel), or nothing where the backend canonicalizes the value itself (canonical-nan). Strict micro benchmarks: mul.f16x2 694 -> 663 cycles, add.f16 674 -> 646, tanh.f16x2 1,025 -> 964, fma.relu.f16x2 776 -> 744, min.f16x2 739 -> 724, ex2.f16x2 691 -> 679. Also: a strict f32 fadd / fsub with a constant operand of at least 2^-101 takes no software path (the slow path is only for both operands below 2^-101), and an nnan result is not canonicalized. lit strict-bits-canonical-nan.ll, strict-fadd-const.ll; no PTX-formalization kernel changes occupancy. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E44 | **Adopted: 4-byte aligned stores of 16-bit pairs as one 32-bit store each**: v2i16 / v2f16 / v4i16 / v4f16 are illegal types, so type legalization split their stores into a 16-bit store per element (plus a 64-bit address add for the second), even where the elements were just computed into one register's halves; loads already had the paired form. Now lowerVectorStore makes a word per pair (PACK16 -> REG_SEQUENCE of the 16-bit values, a new pattern taking them without any-extend copies). PTX `mul.rn.f16x2` loaded and stored: 12 -> 6 instructions. agx-bench (against `-agx-paired-half-stores=false`, which reproduces the baseline exactly): gelu_f16 cycles -3.6% and 28 -> 25 GPRs, permute4d -2.8% (60 -> 58 GPRs), rope -2.5%, layernorm_f16 tput -7.4%, ddot 70 -> 66 GPRs, radix_hist tput -5.8%, blur_rgba8 bytes -2.8%; nothing worse. `-agx-paired-half-stores` (on). | adopted | -- |
| L15-E45 | **Adopted: `!air.flush.harmless`, a front end's mark for an operation whose result a flush of subnormal operands or results does not change**: AIR's and AGXCodeGenPrepare's half flushes (functions with denormal_fpenv(preservesign), the PTX .ftz mode the front end gives a kernel without other half arithmetic) put |x| < 2^-14 selects around every half operation, AIR's IEEE f32 emulation (air.ieee-fp32) a software path behind a denormal check, and AGXStrictFP its IEEE lowering; all three now leave a marked operation alone, and agx-lower-air keeps the mark on the operation it makes of an AIR math function. The front end marks the magic-number addition of its fp8 / fp6 / fp4 encoding and the f16 / f32 fma of the decoding, whose operands and results are normals or zero: decoding e2m1x2 to f16x2 in a flushing kernel 31 -> 25 instructions. lit AIR/half-flush-harmless.ll, AGX/half-denormals.ll, AGX/air/flush-harmless.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E46 | **Adopted with L15-E47 (subregister liveness), `-agx-byte-pair-chains` on: neighbouring 2-byte aligned device bytes as one byte-format access**; adopted with it, harmless by themselves: (a) AGXVectorAccesses raises an access's alignment to what an access of the same base proves where both run (a block's accesses up to an instruction that may not return: the bytes after llama.cpp q4_0 / q8_0 blocks' 2-byte aligned f16 scale are 2-byte aligned at even offsets), (b) the byte-format loads' results have their top byte known zero, (c) `srl (zext i16 x), c` is a 16-bit shift (no copy into a 32-bit register). With pairs: q8_matvec cycles 3,929 -> 2,800 (-29%), tput 267.4 -> 236.8 (-11.4%); q4_matvec cycles 2,832 -> 4,582, tput 313.8 -> 319.8 (+1.9%): the pairs' halves share a 32-bit register live until the later byte's last use (no subregister liveness), its loop needs 107 GPR16 halves where 104 keep 1,024 threads, and -agx-sched-keep-occupancy then reverts it to the original order (13 waits; without the revert cycles 2,257, tput 319.0). Rejected on the way: 16-bit loads of byte pairs (q8 tput 278.9, q4 338.4: extraction on the imadd/bitfield unit), a pressure-limited generic reschedule before the original-order revert (its greedy choices reached 106-109 halves; no bench kernel changed), an agxEstimatePressure gate (estimates 22 and 20 for kernels that allocate 46 and 55). Without the flag no agx-bench kernel changes. lit neighbour-alignment.ll, neighbour-alignment-call.ll, byte-to-float.ll.  Re-measured with subregister liveness on: q8_matvec cycles 4,835 -> 2,786, tput 258.3 -> 233.6 (-9.6%); q4_matvec tput within 1%, cycles 2,988 -> 4,491 (the original-order revert, a single wave only; GPRs 45 -> 24); PTX-formalization occupancy unchanged (1472 kernels); strict suite 881 PASS. Baseline refreshed with q4's cycles. | adopted | subregister liveness for GPR32 halves (q4's +22 halves) |
| L15-E47 | **Adopted: subregister liveness for the register allocator (`-agx-subreg-liveness`, on), without the scheduler's lane-mask pressure (`-agx-sched-lane-masks`, off)**. The allocator's liveness of 16-bit halves apart gives every agx-bench gain: GPRs down in 14 kernels (nbody 47 -> 40, permute4d 60 -> 54, swiglu 21 -> 17), tput ddot -11%, matrixMul -12%, nbody -4.5%, spmv -3.6%, q8_matvec -3.4%; worse: q8_matvec cycles +23%, q4_matvec cycles +5.5% (one wave: the tighter assignment reuses registers sooner), swiglu tput +3% (the baseline refreshed with them). The scheduler's lane masks changed no bench kernel but made the mma emulation kernels allocate 102-116 -> 124-128 GPRs (448-512 -> 384 threads); without them mma-0 110 -> 108. PTX-formalization occupancy (1472 kernels): 0 worse, 40 better in both modes (half-cmp-pk2 55 -> 45 GPRs, 896 -> 1024 threads; multimem 92 -> 76). Strict suite 881 PASS, defaults unchanged; compile time 447 -> 439 s, peak 539 -> 553 MB over 413 strict modules. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E48 | **Rejected: loads a window earlier in the original-order revert** (the revert's order with each load up to W instructions earlier, W tried 48 / 24 / 12 while the occupancy holds): q4_matvec, whose loop reverts, W=48: waits 13 -> 6 but cycles 4,491 -> 5,640, tput 308 -> 316; W=16: cycles -2%, GPRs 24 -> 31; W=8: cycles +23%. The simulator's cost does not follow the waits here; no other kernel reverts. | rejected | what q4's cycles depend on in the reverted order |
| L15-E49 | **Adopted: a load's destination lanes allocated until its first use (AGXLoadLanesLive, `-agx-keep-load-lanes`)**: a load writes its destination when the memory answers; with L15-E47's subregister liveness the allocator gave an unread lane of one load's destination (the high word of a 64-bit load) to the next load, which then waited for the first, serializing their latencies. The first instruction reading the loaded value (through lane copies and REG_SEQUENCEs) now gets an implicit use of the whole destination. Micro benchmarks (cycles): add.f16 and add.s16 1,094 -> 624, mul.lo.s16 1,125 -> 655, prmt 1,634 -> 1,229, vmax2 619 -> 596 (the values before L15-E47); no agx-bench kernel changes; PTX-formalization occupancy unchanged (1472 kernels, both modes); strict suite 881 PASS. lit load-lanes-live.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E50 | **Measured: what subregister liveness still costs** (with L15-E46 byte pairs and L15-E49 on, against `-agx-subreg-liveness=false`): q8_matvec cycles 2,800 -> 2,786, tput 236.8 -> 233.6 (its earlier +23% was the code before byte pairs); q4_matvec cycles 4,442 -> 4,491 (+1%; its single-wave cost is L15-E46's original-order revert), tput 310.9 -> 308.2; swiglu cycles 422 -> 426, tput 32.6 -> 33.6 (17 GPRs instead of 21, far below an occupancy step: the tighter assignment reuses registers that are still being read, write-after-read dependences that hold back the post-RA order). Breaking anti-dependences after allocation (renaming into registers below the function's count) would recover it; not done. | measured | post-RA renaming of anti-dependences |
| L15-E51 | **Rejected (wrong results): renaming write-after-read definitions after allocation** (a 32-bit definition into a register read within the 6 instructions before it, moved to a register free over its life among those the function uses): agx-bench attn_decode cycles -7.2%, q8_matvec -5.5%, lit only register numbers, but the PTX-formalization suite failed 30 more default-mode files and 47 strict ones (mma, tensor-core, tanh-half). A register that liveness shows free can still be the destination of an earlier load whose write has not landed (a load whose result is unused, or one in another block), and the renamed value is then overwritten. A safe version needs the outstanding loads' destinations (as AGXInsertWaits tracks them) across blocks; the simple safe one (no register any load writes as a new name, loads not renamed) changes no agx-bench kernel. | rejected | renaming that respects outstanding load writes |
| L15-E52 | **Rejected: other scheduling directions before the original-order revert** (the generic scheduler again top-down, then both ways, keeping one that holds the occupancy): q4_matvec's loop needs 107 GPR16 halves in every direction (104 keep 1,024 threads); its eight 16-byte f32 loads and the nibble pairs issued early are what take them, so no schedule that issues its loads early fits, and its single-wave cost (L15-E46) is the price of the occupancy. | rejected | -- |
| L15-E53 | **Adopted: soft-f64 division and square root residuals from 64-bit high products**: the Newton steps' residuals (2^114 - N Y^2 for the root, 2^115 - D Z for the quotient) were 128-bit products and differences of which only the top bits are used; each is now the high word of (N << 10) Y^2 or (D << 11) Z subtracted from a 64-bit constant, off by less than 2^54 (root) or 2^53 (quotient, taken one unit low and clamped at 0 so that the reciprocal stays below), inside what the steps tolerate. A Python model of the integer algorithm with the reciprocal estimates 4 ulps either way: roots at most 1 unit off before the remainder correction (201,200 radicands), quotients never above and at most 1 below (753,600 pairs). Cycles: div.rn.f64 4,042 -> 3,595 (-11%), rcp.rn.f64 3,671 -> 3,227 (-12%), sqrt.rn.f64 5,756 -> 5,087 (-12%), the same in --strict-fp; the generated f64_divsqrt sets pass; strict suite 881 PASS; no agx-bench kernel changes. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E54 | **Rejected: soft-f64 fused multiply-add normalization on 64-bit words** (the 128-bit product-sum's leading-zero count and shift as a select between the high word's count and the low word's, with a funnel shift, instead of a 128-bit ctlz and shl): fma.rn.f64 4,368 -> 4,429 cycles (+1.4%); the 128-bit forms already legalize to about the same word operations and the selects add to them. | rejected | -- |
