| L15-E55 | **Adopted: 8- and 16-bit signed division without the front end's guards** (AGXCodeGenPrepare expandDivRem16): the front end divides by d = -1 ? 1 : d (or d + 1 <u 2 ? 1 : d) and selects -x / all ones (remainder 0 / x) itself; the float-reciprocal expansion gives exactly those values when it divides by d (x - q 0 = x, x - (-x)(-1) = 0, -2^15 / -1 wrapping), so where every use of the division is such a select chain (or DivRemPairs' x - q d' ending in one), it divides by d and the selects and guard go; the conversions read the 8- / 16-bit values (s8_to_f / s16_to_f, no extension), the remainder is computed in the narrow type (its quotient frozen where d may be 0), no select by zero where d is known nonzero. Also an AGX DAG combine: trunc (sext_inreg i64 Y, i16) -> sext_inreg (trunc Y) (zext (sext i16 -> i32) -> i64 took four instructions, now two). Dynamic instructions (1,024 threads): div.s16 22,528 -> 16,384, s8 33,792 -> 24,576, rem.s16 25,600 -> 18,432, div.u16 18,432 -> 16,384, rem.u16 18,432 -> 17,408; cycles lower in all five. Checked: all 65,536 s8 pairs and every s16 dividend by -1, -2, 1, 0 (div and rem, both modes), the emulation tests, PTX-formalization suites unchanged (default failures the same 33, strict 881 PASS); agx-bench: nbody -0.8% cycles, nothing else changes; occupancy over 1,472 kernels: none worse, one better (tcsp-3 98 -> 92 GPRs, 512 -> 576 threads). | adopted | -- |
| L15-E56 | **Adopted: byte permutes from 16-bit halves** (AGXCodeGenPrepare permuteBytes, `-agx-byte-permutes`): a 32-bit or tree whose bytes are bytes of other values, zeros or sign fills of a byte (through whole-byte masks and shifts, ashr, InstCombine's select of a sign fill, zero extensions) is built half by half: each 16-bit half at most one bfi of one byte into the other byte's half (plus a shift or a sign fill), the halves the register's, where the tree costs more. With DAG combines for 16-bit and offset-0 byte inserts ((x & m) \| (y & ~m) and (x << s) \| q as one bfi) and PACK16 of one extended 16-bit half. Constant prmt.b32 (instructions after the loads): 0x6420 10 -> 2, 0x7531 9 -> 4, 0x8880 9 -> 3, 0x9990 9 -> 2, 0x0000 6 -> 3, 0x2301 10 -> 4, 0x5140 8 -> 3, 0x3120 7 -> 3; a kernel of 320 prmt on separate operands 2,609 -> 2,103 executed. agx-bench: blur_rgba8 159 -> 157 instructions, nothing else changes. New test agx_emulation_tests/prmt_const.ptx (320 selectors, expected from the ISA's definition), both modes. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E57 | **Adopted: a 32-bit value widened to 64 bits is a REG_SEQUENCE of the value and a zero (zext) or undefined (anyext) high half**: the generic expansion moved the value into the low half of a fresh pair and cleared the high half with a separate copy, which kept the pair live and added moves; now the high half is one mov_imm 0 the allocator can drop where only the low half is read (64-bit address arithmetic of 32-bit indices). MLIR tile2d (mlir/utils/agx-kernels/bench) 153.0 -> 145.9 cycles; agx-bench permute4d cycles 1,644.1 -> 1,595.8, tput 319.6 -> 302.7, nothing worse (baseline re-recorded). Validated with agx-tv (alive2 scripts/agx-tv: the lifted machine code refines the IR). lit zext-dead-high.ll, i64.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E58 | **Adopted (verify on hardware): the workgroup fence after a threadgroup barrier dropped where one precedes it** (AGXLowerControlFlow, `-agx-merge-barrier-fences`, on): AIR's threadgroup_barrier(mem_threadgroup) and the PTX front end's / MLIR's fence-barrier-fence sequences emitted `memory_barrier 1, 2, 9; threadgroup_barrier; memory_barrier 1, 2, 9`; every workgroup fence is the same memory_barrier, and the one before the barrier already orders the thread's accesses before the others pass it, so a second one orders nothing more (the front end's __syncthreads is the fence before the barrier only). A fence after a barrier with none before it stays. MLIR block_reduce 507.2 -> 396.8 cycles (1 group), 967 -> 857 (32 groups); agx-bench unchanged; AGX / AIR lit 277 pass. alive2 cannot decide it (its sequential model treats a fence as a clobber; with the fences kept the query times out), so the evidence is the hardware's documented fence semantics: check on an M-series GPU with the A/B switch. lit barrier-fences.ll. | adopted | hardware check of the dropped fence |
| L15-E59 | **Adopted: a comparison that only branches read is each branch's own** (AGXFoldOperands, `-agx-fold-shared-branch-compares`, on): instruction selection makes every branch condition an icmpsel / fcmpsel of 1 or 0 that AGXFoldOperands folds into the if / loop when it is the branch's single use; after MachineCSE merges equal conditions of several blocks (a reduction's lane == 0 before and after a barrier, a switch lowered twice on one value) the select stayed and each branch tested it for nonzero. Now, where every use is a branch and the comparison reads at most one general register (the others immediates or uniforms, so no register pressure moves), each branch takes the comparison. MLIR block_reduce at -O3 398.5 -> 396.8 cycles, 31 -> 30 instructions, 6 -> 5 GPRs; agx-bench scan_blelloch tput 158.2 -> 137.5 (-13%, 28 -> 20 GPRs, bytes 1,594 -> 1,526), radix_hist 12 -> 11 GPRs, softmax_online 20 -> 19, nothing worse; AGX / AIR lit 279 pass. agx-tv (alive2) proves the machine code of branch-compare-shared.ll. lit branch-compare-shared.ll, else-flow-hoist.ll (FOLD). | adopted | -- |
| L15-E60 | **Adopted: a 64-bit loop counter that steps by one to a loop-invariant bound counted in 32 bits** (AGXNarrowInductions, `-agx-split-wide-counters`, on): an MLIR index loop to a dynamic bound (`scf.for` over `index`, i64) keeps a 64-bit counter that loop strength reduction makes a count down to zero, `isub r_r, r_r, 1` (a 64-bit add: 4 issue cycles in the model) and an `or` of its halves per iteration. Where the counter's only other uses are truncations to 32 bits or fewer (and its value after the loop, the bound), the loop counts the low half and exits when it reaches the bound's low half; a new enclosing loop runs it again for each further 2^32 steps ((D - 1) >> 32 of them, D = (End - Start) * Step, 2^64 for 0), and does not run where scalar evolution bounds the trip count by 2^32 (an `i32` count widened by IndVarSimplify), so the outer loop is not built there. An i32 phi that loop strength reduction already counts the low half with is reused. Exhaustive model of the scheme at 8 bits (4-bit halves, every start, end and step +-1: same trip count and low halves) and agx-tv (alive2) of the lifted machine code for both forms (one iteration; two time out with or without the change). MLIR matmul 5,747.5 -> 5,609.3 cycles, 10 -> 9 GPRs, 96 groups 241.9 -> 179.8 cycles per group (-26%); agx-bench and the other MLIR kernels unchanged; AGX / AIR lit 281 pass. lit split-wide-counters.ll. | adopted | -- |

Instructions that touch no memory, have no side effects and change no execution mask may now come between; with no memory access between the two fences the second orders nothing more. (A one-instruction iadd of a 16-bit source into 64 bits encodes, but no recorded hardware case uses that form, so it is not used.) agx-tv proves zext-i16-i64.ll's store64 (the full value); index64 times out. lit zext-i16-i64.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E71 | **Adopted: no r + 0.0 in the precise e^x** (AGXMathExpansion: exp passed a constant +0.0 as the low part that exp2 / exp10 have, so every exp added it to the reduced argument): +0.0 only turns r = -0 into +0, and e^r = fma(P(r), r r, r) + 1 is 1 for both; an exhaustive check over all 2^32 floats r (x86 fmaf, IEEE and with FTZ/DAZ as AGX flushes) finds no input whose result differs (NaNs aside). MLIR bench silu_f16x4 640.4 -> 636.5 cycles, 246 -> 242 instructions, 36 -> 34 GPRs; softmax_rows 3,338.3 -> 3,306.3 (-1%), 163 -> 161; agx-bench within tolerance. lit exp-no-zero-add.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E72 | **Adopted: no +inf / +0 selects after e^x's clamps** (AGXMathExpansion, exp only): x is clamped to [-104, 89] and then x > 89 selected +inf, x < -104 +0; but at the clamps the result overflows (e^89 > FLT_MAX by 30%) and rounds to +0 (e^-104 is below half the least denormal; flushed anyway) by itself. An exact C model of the expansion (fmaf, nearbyintf, the two-step ldexp) for every non-NaN float, IEEE and with FTZ/DAZ: no result differs. The NaN select stays (fptosi of a NaN n is poison in the IR); exp2 / exp10 / pow, with a low part, keep the range selects. agx-bench softmax_row tput 27.2 -> 25.1 (-8%), GPRs 10 -> 9, 444 -> 422 bytes; MLIR bench silu_f16x4 636.5 -> 577.6 cycles (-9%), 242 -> 234 instructions, 34 -> 32 GPRs; softmax_rows 3,306.3 -> 3,232.6 (-2.2%), 161 -> 154, 29 -> 27 GPRs; nothing worse. lit exp-no-zero-add.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E73 | **Adopted: no NaN select at the end of e^x, 2^x, 10^x and pow's exponential** (AGXMathExpansion): the select gave a NaN argument back because the scale's fptosi of a NaN n is poison; with that conversion frozen (no instruction), a NaN hi is a NaN reduced argument and the polynomial and scaling make the result a NaN by themselves (any NaN, as LLVM allows; the C model: all 16,777,214 NaN inputs give a NaN, IEEE and flushing, whatever the conversion returns). MLIR bench silu_f16x4 577.6 -> 534.5 cycles (-7.5%), 32 -> 30 GPRs; softmax_rows 3,232.6 -> 3,199.0, 27 -> 26 GPRs; agx-bench within tolerance. lit exp-no-zero-add.ll, fp.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E74 | **Rejected: the exponentials' ldexp split in integers** (n converted once, i >> 1 and i - (i >> 1), instead of trunc(n / 2) and n - trunc(n / 2) in floats and two conversions; bit-identical for every float, IEEE and flushing, as v 2^(i >> 1) is exact): 4 instructions fewer per exp, softmax_rows 3,199.0 -> 3,149.4 cycles, but silu_f16x4 534.5 -> 556.6 (+4%): the asr and the conversion share the unit integer multiplies and bit-field operations use, which silu's four exponentials per thread keep busy, while the floating-point steps they replace did not. | rejected | -- |
| L15-E75 | **Rejected (not better everywhere): 2^x and 10^x clamping x instead of x ln b** (x to [-151, 130] and [-46, 40], so that no infinity makes the low part a NaN, and no clamp of hi nor +inf / +0 selects after; bit-identical for every float, IEEE and flushing): a lone exp2 59 -> 55 instructions, 176.6 -> 172.1 cycles, but fp.ll's transcendental (exp2 beside pow, which keeps the 89 / -104 constants) 514.7 -> 518.4 cycles at the same instruction count: the new constants take their own registers. | rejected | constants shared with pow's exponential |
| L15-E76 | **Adopted, gated on register headroom: 64-bit ctlz / cttz select the half first, then one ffs** (lowerCTLZ_CTTZ64, `-agx-select-count-half`): 31 - ffs(hi) or 63 - ffs(lo) as off - ffs(hi == 0 ? lo : hi) with off = hi == 0 ? 63 : 31 (64 for zero: ffs(0) = -1, the M2 Max coverage case ffs_r14_0), instead of both halves' counts and a select: ctlz 6 -> 5 instructions with one ffs, cttz 8 -> 6 with one bitrev and one ffs (both on the unit integer multiplies share). Ungated, ddot cycles -2.5% and tput -3.5% but 64 -> 66 GPRs (832 -> 768 threads per threadgroup: both halves stay live to the select), so only where agxEstimatePressure is within -agx-headroom-gprs; agx-bench then unchanged; i64.ll bitcount -3 instructions, no register count changes. agx-tv proves ctlz64-select-half.ll's lz and tz (and rejects them with 63 / 31 swapped). | adopted | -- |
| L15-E77 | **Adopted: minimum / maximum as unordered compare-selects** (lowerFMinimumMaximum, `-agx-select-minimum-maximum`; and +0.0 select arms as the immediate 0): a ugt b ? a : b (ult for minimum) takes a NaN a, one more select a NaN b (an operand NaN is a NaN result LLVM allows: no default-NaN constant), equal operands the and / or of their bits; a constant goes second, a non-NaN one needs no NaN select, a nonzero one no zeros, and maximum(x, +0) / minimum(x, -0) need neither: relu (maximum(x, +0)) 5 -> 1 instruction (`fcmpsel lte, r, x, 0.0, 0, x`), maximum(x, 1.0) 1, two variables 6 -> 5. agx-tv found two old bugs on the way, fixed here: a zero constant's equal operand took a | +0 (minimum(+denormal, +0) gave the denormal; now a & sign), and two denormals the comparison flushes took the and / or of their bits (a third denormal; now canonicalized where f32 flushes, fmul 1.0). agx-tv proves maximum / minimum with +0, -0, 1.0 and f16, and the two-variable and constant-first forms for every non-subnormal input; with subnormal inputs alive2 flushes the source's result while the target's select passes the operand's bits (allowed: denormal outputs may be flushed), as the old maxnum-based lowering did. agx-bench unchanged (no min / max in it); lit minmaxnum.ll. | adopted | maximumnum / minimumnum keep the old zero handling (two equal denormals: a third one) |
| L15-E78 | **Adopted: copysign of a variable magnitude as one bfi** (lowerFCOPYSIGN, `-agx-copysign-bfi`): the magnitude's low 31 (15) bits inserted into the sign's value, instead of a sign mask in a register, a bfeil of the magnitude and an or: f32 4 -> 1 instruction, f16 5 -> 1; the same unit count (the bfeil was on the bfi's unit). A constant magnitude keeps the ALU masks (copysign(0, x) is one and; the half-denormal flushes use it). agx-bench gelu_f16 tput 234.4 -> 226.8 (-3.2%), GPRs 25 -> 24, 624 -> 592 bytes (tanh's copysign); round 11 -> 9 instructions; nothing worse. Encodings as the simulator's applegpu-compatible assembler gives them; agx-tv proves copysign f32 / f16 / copysign(1.0, x) and round (and rejects copysign with base and source swapped). | adopted | -- |
| L15-E79 | **Rejected (not better everywhere): 64-bit widening by a register pair for ctpop.i64 and sext i32 -> i64** (the 32-bit value and a zero / an asr 31, instead of a 64-bit iadd of zero on the wide issue slot, as L15-E57 / E70 did for zext): sext made conv3x3, index_add, nbody, permute4d, q4 / q8_matvec and sgemm_reg4x4 0.02-0.6% faster but rope 283.2 -> 284.0 cycles (the asr on the unit integer multiplies share); ctpop.i64 changed no bench kernel but i64.ll's bitcount 7 -> 8 GPRs (the zero scheduled first). | rejected | -- |
| L15-E80 | **Adopted (net win, revisiting L15-E79): sext i32 -> i64 and ctpop.i64 as register pairs** (the value and an asr 31 / a zero, instead of a 64-bit iadd of zero on the wide issue slot). One group: conv3x3 -0.02%, index_add -0.12%, nbody -0.01%, permute4d -0.54%, q4 / q8_matvec -0.04 / -0.07%, sgemm_reg4x4 -0.04%, rope +0.28% (geomean of the changed -0.07%). 64 groups: rope -1.25%, q4 -0.67%, q8 -0.59%, nbody -0.14%, conv3x3 -0.11%, sgemm -0.08%, index_add +0.03%, permute4d +0.73% (geomean about -0.26%); GPRs unchanged in all of them; i64.ll's bitcount one instruction fewer, 7 -> 8 GPRs; MLIR bench unchanged. agx-tv proves the sign extension (a stored 64-bit sext); ctpop.i64 on the simulator's functional model: 4,096 inputs (random and edge values) give popcount. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E81 | **Adopted (net win, revisiting L15-E75): 2^x and 10^x clamp x instead of x ln b** (bit-identical for every float, IEEE and flushing): with throughput and registers in the count it wins where L15-E75 saw a single-group loss. An exp2 attention-row loop (p = exp2(s scale), summed): 196.2 -> 190.7 cycles at one group, 1,617.6 -> 1,493.0 at 64 (-7.7%), 25 -> 23 GPRs; a lone exp2 / exp10 175.5 -> 172.1, 1,232.1 -> 1,147.9 at 64 groups, 10 -> 7 GPRs; fp.ll's transcendental (exp2 beside pow) 514.5 -> 516.2 at one group (+0.3%) but 7,555.2 -> 7,436.1 at 64 (-1.6%), 34 -> 31 GPRs. agx-bench and MLIR bench have no exp2 / exp10 (unchanged). lit exp2-bounded.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E82 | **Adopted (net win, revisiting L15-E74): the exponentials' ldexp split in integers** (one conversion, i >> 1 and i - (i >> 1); bit-identical for every float, IEEE and flushing). With throughput and registers counted: silu_f16x4 534.5 -> 556.6 cycles at one group (+4%) but 6,606.5 -> 6,474.4 at 64 (-2.0%), 30 GPRs both; softmax_rows 3,199.0 -> 3,149.4 at one group, 16,041.9 -> 15,219.6 at 64 (-5.1%), 26 -> 25 GPRs; agx-bench softmax_row 253.1 -> 249.9, 414 -> 406 bytes, the rest unchanged. lit fp.ll regenerated. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E83 | **Adopted: round(x) as trunc(x + copysign(b, x))**, b the float just below 0.5 (0x3effffff, half 0x37ff): a mov_imm, a bfi, an add and a trunc instead of trunc, a subtraction, a compare-select against 0.5, a copysign of 1 and a final copysign (9 instructions -> 4, 2 GPRs). A round kernel 22 -> 17 instructions, 121.4 -> 115.0 cycles (f32), 120.4 -> 115.0 (f16). Exact: every float (x86 model, flushing and not), every half (exact float64 sums), and on the simulator's functional model all 65,536 halves and 442,624 floats (every exponent's edges, halves of integers and their neighbours, random). CUDA's roundf reaches it (the PTX front end makes llvm.round of it). agx-bench and MLIR bench have no round (unchanged). `-agx-round-bias`; lit float-misc.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E84 | **Adopted: a float's finiteness / infinity bit tests as float compares** ((bits << 1) <u 0xff000000 and its complement, (bits & 0x7fffffff) == 0x7f800000 and its complement, as is.fpclass's expansion and hand-written code write them; half alike): |x| against +inf with fcmpsel's .abs modifier, instead of the shift or mask (on the shared unit) and an icmpsel. Over every is.fpclass test mask selecting between two values: 19 masks one or two instructions fewer for f32 and for f16 (isfinite, isinf and their complements 3 -> 2, half's isinf 4 -> 2), none worse, f32 total 10,707 -> 10,683 instructions. A first try that lowered IS_FPCLASS itself (Custom) left the other masks to be expanded after type legalization, 254 of them an instruction worse, and crashed on an i16 setcc of i32 bits; matching the bit forms keeps the builder's expansion. agx-tv proves the 19 masks' selects and 6 class tests for both types. agx-bench and MLIR bench have none (unchanged). `-agx-fpclass-compare`; lit fpclass-compare.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E85 | **Adopted: sign tests against an immediate 0** (x >= 0 is x > -1 after canonicalization, and icmpsel takes 0 as an immediate but -1 only in a register): a select on x > -1 becomes one on x < 0 with the arms swapped (mov_imm + icmpsel -> icmpsel, i32, i16 and float arms); zext(x >= 0), srl(not x, 31), one icmpsel instead of a not and a bfeil; a branch on x >= 0 or x < 0 one if_icmp against 0 (AGXFoldOperands folds the icmpsel) instead of the not, the bfeil and an if_icmp of the bit (3 -> 1). agx-bench: conv3x3 47,417.0 -> 47,406.4 cycles at one group, tput 2,531.3 -> 2,533.1 (+0.07%, a rescheduling), the rest unchanged; MLIR bench unchanged. agx-tv proves the four select / branch / truth-value kernels. `-agx-sign-select`; lit sign-test.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E86 | **Adopted: the signed bytes of one word through one xor of the word** (where two or more of its bytes are sign-extended, as sext <4 x i8> does: x ^ 0x80808080 once, each byte of which is the byte ^ 128, then per byte the field and - 128, instead of ((field) ^ 128) - 128 per byte; the constant opaque so that demanded bits do not narrow it per byte again; the top byte stays an asr): int8_dp4a_gemv's loop 6 xors -> 2 (the constant hoisted), 1,741.0 -> 1,714.1 cycles, tput 125.9 -> 122.7 (-2.5%), bytes 418 -> 400, GPRs 17 -> 18 (occupancy unchanged); the rest of agx-bench unchanged. Exact (byte p of x ^ 0x80808080 is byte p ^ 128); on the simulator's functional model a sext-sum and a dp4a word kernel agree with numpy on 262,144 inputs (every byte pair in every lane, random elsewhere); agx-tv times out on both (as before the change). `-agx-shared-byte-bias`; lit signed-bytes-shared.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E87 | **Adopted: saturating float -> 8-bit conversions as the saturating 16-bit convert and an integer clamp** (the convert saturates and gives 0 for NaN, so clamping its result is the 8-bit saturation): fptoui.sat.i8 convert + one icmpsel against 255 (6 -> 2 instructions), fptosi.sat.i8 convert + a mov_imm and two icmpsels (7-8 -> 4), instead of the float clamped against both bounds, a NaN test and the convert; a <4 x i8> fptosi.sat of four floats packed in a word 31 -> 25. agx-tv proves u8 / s8 from f32 and f16, stored as i8 and extended to i32 (12 kernels). agx-bench and MLIR bench have none (unchanged). `-agx-sat-convert-clamp`; lit fptoi-sat.ll, clamp-const.ll (its s8 clamp now written with maximumnum / minimumnum). | adopted | -- |
| L15-E88 | **Adopted: x & 0xffff as a move of the low 16 bits** (bitop from the 16-bit half, zero-extending), instead of a mov_imm of 0xffff and an and: a 16-bit multiply-add of two masked words 4 -> 3 instructions. A 16-bit-immediate form for other wide masks was tried and dropped for the same reason. agx-tv proves the masks (and16: 0xfff, 0xff00). agx-bench unchanged. lit and-wide-mask.ll (with an object emission). | adopted | -- |
| L15-E89 | **Adopted: a select on a select of truth values as two nested selects** (after legalization, select(select(c, 1, d), x, y) -> select(c, x, select(d, x, y)) and select(select(c, d, 0), x, y) -> select(c, select(d, x, y), y), d a compare): a select on fcmp one / ueq, which legalization expands to the or of olt and ogt, 3 -> 2 instructions (two fcmpsels instead of a truth value, a second compare selecting it and an icmpsel). | L15-E90 | **Adopted: -\|x\| as an or of the sign bit** (f32 and f16): a mov_imm and an or instead of the and with 0x7fffffff and the xor with 0x80000000, each with its own mov_imm (4 -> 2 instructions). agx-tv proves both. agx-bench unchanged. lit float-misc.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E91 | **Adopted (net win): the sign as a small mask, and signed remainders by 2^k without -2^k**: and(sra(x, 31), m) for an 8-bit m and srl(sra(x, 31), 32 - k) for k <= 8 as icmpsel slt x, 0, m, 0 (one ALU instruction with immediates instead of an asr and a bfeil / and), and x - ((x + b) & -2^k) as ((x + b) & (2^k - 1)) - b (8-bit mask, no mov_imm, one subtraction fewer; TableGen patterns, since a DAG combine to a select was folded straight back into the and of the asr). sdiv by 4..256 4 -> 3 instructions, srem by 2..256 5-6 -> 4. agx-bench: sgemm_reg4x4 22,506.4 -> 22,384.0 cycles, tput 2,744.8 -> 2,657.6 (-3.2%), 66 -> 64 GPRs; swiglu 425.8 -> 422.9, tput 33.6 -> 32.5 (-3.3%); embedding 726.9 -> 722.9; q8_matvec 2,783.7 -> 2,780.5; q4_matvec 4,489.5 -> 4,486.3 cycles but tput 306.2 -> 309.2 (+1.0%, one prologue instruction rescheduled); permute4d 54 -> 56 GPRs, 48 bytes more, cycles and tput unchanged. Restricting to a single-use asr removed permute4d's registers but also sgemm's gain (tput +0.4% instead of -3.2%), so the patterns are unrestricted. MLIR bench unchanged. agx-tv proves sdiv / srem by 2, 4, 16, 256, 512 and both patterns on their own (12 kernels). lit sdiv-srem-pow2.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E92 | **Adopted: min / max with +0.0 (relu) as one fcmpsel with immediates** (f32 and f16: the compare's operand the float immediate 0.0, the arm the integer immediate 0, +0.0's bits), instead of a mov_imm of 0 that the generic pattern shares between the compare and the arm (an instruction, and in a loop a register held across it). agx-bench bilinear_resize 226.4 -> 225.3 cycles, 6 bytes smaller, the rest unchanged; MLIR bench unchanged. agx-tv proves maxnum / minnum with 0 for f32 and f16. lit minmax-zero.ll. | adopted | -- |

| L15-E95 | **Adopted (correctness fix, at a cost): minimumnum / maximumnum of f32 operands canonicalize the zeros fix's bits** where compares flush (no ieee-fp32): two different denormals compare equal, and the or / and of their bits was a third denormal, neither result nor its flush (as minimum / maximum already did in L15-E66's lowering). On the simulator's functional model, 65,536 operand pairs drawn from denormals of both signs, zeros, infinities, NaNs and normals: before, 65,019 (min) / 64,829 (max) results were neither the exact result nor that of flushed operands flushed; after, none. | L15-E98 | **Adopted: the ballot of a comparison as one icmp_ballot / fcmp_ballot** (new FCMP_BALLOT pseudo; simd and, for integers, quad ballots; inverted conditions with bit 3, immediates as the compare-selects take them), instead of the comparison's 0 / 1 and an icmp_ballot of it against 0: ballot(a < b) and its popcount (__popc(__ballot_sync(..., a < b))) 3 -> 2 instructions, any / all / first-lane forms one fewer each.
