| L15-E99 | **Adopted: float -> integer conversions rounding to nearest even in the convert, and roundings moved out of integral clamps** (an int8 quantization kernel, new: q = clamp(round(x / s), -127, 127) of a float4, packed). (1) fptosi / fptoui (and .sat at the result width) of rint / roundeven / nearbyint, the rounding with no other use: one convert. The round field (bit 26) is the direction and mode bit 1 the rounding (the simulator's convert, exact against the G14C over 80 forms, AGX Performance Simulator tasks/safe-01), so the round-to-nearest-even float -> integer form is the mode the backend names for the opposite direction with the field clear (printed "s32_to_f ..., rtz"); CUDA's __float2int_rn 2 -> 1 instruction. With other uses (the exponentials' rint, converted and multiplied) the fused convert only rescheduled: silu_f16x4 +1.8% at one group, so those keep rint + convert. (2) fptosi(clamp(rnd(x))) with integral bounds -> fptosi(rnd(clamp(x))) (a monotone rounding keeping integers commutes with min / max against integers, NaN included), and fptosi(trunc(y)) -> fptosi(y): the quantization kernel 53 -> 49 instructions, 163.8 -> 155.6 cycles, 19.3 -> 17.9 per group at 64 (rint instead of round: 44 -> 40, 150.3 -> 142.3, 14.2 -> 11.5). Functional model: the conversions identical to rint + convert on 620,000 inputs (ties, specials, out of range, denormals, random) and to numpy; both quantization kernels identical before / after on 50,944 packed words at four scales. agx-tv (lifter taught the convert's direction bit) proves s32 / u16 rounding conversions and both clamp forms. agx-bench and MLIR bench unchanged. `-agx-round-out-of-clamp`; lit convert-rne.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E100 | **Adopted: log / log2 / log10 / pow without the denormal scaling where f32 flushes** (the 2^23 scale and exponent correction for x < 2^-126 only under ieee-fp32; also skipped for widened halves, never f32 denormals): on the hardware the path was dead, its compare and multiply flushing the denormal to the 0 for which x == 0 gives -inf: bit-identical on the simulator's functional model for log, log2 and log10 over 623,616 inputs (every exponent's edges and the sqrt(2) split, 20,000 denormals, random words). log 72 -> 66 instructions, 129.4 -> 122.7 cycles, 22.3 -> 20.2 per group at 64; pow 135 -> 129, 235.5 -> 226.7, 47.6 -> 45.6. agx-bench and MLIR bench have none (fp.ll regenerated). lit log-flush.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E101 | **Adopted: tanh's exponential without the argument clamp** (e^(2 min(\|x\|, 9)): the argument is in [0, 18] or a NaN, which the exponential keeps, so the bounded form of L15-E81 instead of exp's clamp to [-104, 89]): 78 -> 74 instructions, 146.7 -> 139.1 cycles, 25.2 -> 23.9 per group at 64; bit-identical on the simulator's functional model over 702,208 inputs (every exponent's edges of both signs, 400,000 points in [-20, 20], random words, the 0.625 and 9 switch points). sinh / cosh keep their clamp (e^a for any a >= 0). agx-bench and MLIR bench have no tanh. lit tanh-bounded-exp.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E102 | **Adopted: tanh's 2 / (e^2a + 1) as twice the correctly rounded reciprocal** (d in [2, e^18 + 1] or a NaN: y0 = rcp(d), y = y0 + y0 (1 - d y0), lowerFDIV32's reciprocal, which scales with d's exponent there; 2 / d is then 2 y exactly), without the general division's exponent factors and NaN selects: tanh 74 -> 65 instructions (78 before L15-E101); bit-identical to the previous code on the simulator's functional model for every positive float in [0.5, 16) (41,943,040; tanh is constant beyond 9) and 2,432,512 random words and negatives. lit tanh-bounded-exp.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E103 | **Adopted: sinh / cosh's 0.25 / h as a quarter of the correctly rounded reciprocal** (h = e^a / 2 >= 0.5: L15-E102's reciprocal while h <= 2^124, else 0, the flushed quotient, an infinite h included, whose reciprocal step would be a NaN): sinh 101 -> 94 instructions, 242.0 -> 227.4 cycles, 38.6 -> 35.9 per group at 64; cosh 90 -> 84, 227.1 -> 216.5, 33.8 -> 32.1. Bit-identical to the previous code on the simulator's functional model for every positive float in [0.5, 128) (h's whole range to overflow) and 2,000,000 random words, specials and negatives. agx-bench and MLIR bench have none. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E104 | **Adopted: atan's argument reduction with one division** (the operands chosen first, -1 / a above tan(3 pi / 8) and (a - 1) / (a + 1) above tan(pi / 8), instead of both divisions computed and one selected): atan 66 -> 59 instructions, 192.2 -> 186.8 cycles, 23.4 -> 20.7 per group at 64, 13 -> 12 GPRs; atan2 101 -> 94, 313.2 -> 305.0, 38.2 -> 35.6, 18 -> 16 GPRs. Bit-identical to the previous code on the simulator's functional model: every positive float in [0.25, 8) (both switch points), 3,000,000 random words, negatives and specials for atan; 4,194,304 random / special / proportional pairs for atan2. agx-bench and MLIR bench have none. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E105 | **Adopted: asinh / acosh with one log** (log(arg) + (a > 1500 ? ln 2 : 0), the argument a or a + sqrt(a^2 + 1) picked first, instead of both logs computed and one selected; AIR's asinh / acosh, built by AGXLowerAIR): asinh 153 -> 103 instructions, 310.9 -> 244.2 cycles, 58.2 -> 34.6 per group at 64 (-41%), 28 -> 16 GPRs; acosh 161 -> 111, 332.8 -> 250.5, 60.8 -> 38.5 (-37%), 29 -> 18 GPRs. Bit-identical to the previous code (NaN payloads aside) on the simulator's functional model: every float in [0.25, 4) and [1024, 2048) for asinh, [1, 4) and [1024, 2048) for acosh (the 1500 switch), 3,500,000 random words, negatives and specials. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E106 | **Adopted: sinh / cosh's e^a / 2 with one exponential** ((a < 88 ? 0.5 : 1) * e^(a < 88 ? a : a - ln 2), instead of both exponentials computed and one selected): sinh 94 -> 73 instructions (101 before L15-E103), 227.4 -> 195.8 cycles, 35.9 -> 24.2 per group at 64; cosh 84 -> 63, 216.5 -> 186.2, 32.1 -> 20.4. Bit-identical to the code before L15-E103 (NaN payloads aside) on the simulator's functional model over every positive float in [0.5, 128) and 2,000,000 random words, specials and negatives. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E107 | **Adopted: asin / acos with one polynomial** (of sqrt((1 - a) / 2) or a, picked first, instead of both evaluated and one selected): asin 53 -> 49 instructions, 172.6 -> 170.6 cycles, 17.7 -> 15.4 per group at 64, 12 -> 8 GPRs; acos 56 -> 52, 177.9 -> 174.8, 18.7 -> 16.4, 11 -> 8 GPRs. Bit-identical (NaN payloads aside) on the simulator's functional model: every float in [0.25, 2) and 4,800,000 random words, small arguments, negatives and specials. (tan's sin and cos already share one reduction.) | adopted | -- |
| L15-E108 | **Adopted: (x ^ c) << s as (x << s) ^ (c << s)** (c << s an 8-bit immediate; a DAG combine so the shifted x is shared): a threadgroup index tg[t ^ 1] beside tg[t] is one xor of t's byte offset instead of an xor and a shift (an added or or-ed constant already folded into iadd's shifted form); memory.ll's kernel one instruction and one GPR fewer. agx-tv proves the shift form (a kernel with both shifts); the threadgroup kernel times out (exact identity on 32-bit words). agx-bench and MLIR bench unchanged. `-agx-xor-shift-commute`; lit memory.ll regenerated. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E109 | **Adopted (correctness fix): half division without arcp / afn correctly rounded.** It was rcp(b) * a in half, which LLVM's fdiv does not allow: on the simulator's functional model 432,237 of 2,097,152 random pairs (20.6%) were an ulp off. Now in float: the correctly rounded reciprocal y = y0 + y0 (1 - b y0) and one correction q = q1 + (a - b q1) y of q1 = a y (lowerFDIV32's steps; halves are normal floats and their quotients far from float's flushed and infinite ranges, so no scaling), rounded to half (float's 24 bits are the 2 * 11 + 2 for which that double rounding is exact); zeros, infinities and NaNs take a y0 (a NaN q, or a zero a whose correction can lose the sign). 2 -> 12 instructions. Functional model against float64 division rounded to half: 4,194,304 random pairs, every a against 64 b (16 specials: zeros, infinities, NaN, denormals, powers of two) and 64 a against every b: none wrong. arcp keeps rcp and the product. agx-bench and MLIR bench have no such division (unchanged). `-agx-precise-half-div`; lit fdiv-half.ll, fp.ll regenerated. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E110 | **Adopted (correctness fix): half sqrt without afn correctly rounded.** x rsqrt_special(x) in half was an ulp off for 8,520 of the 65,536 halves (simulator). Now lowerFSQRT's float steps (s0 = x y, s = s0 + (x - s0^2) y / 2, a NaN s replaced by s0 for 0, inf, negatives and NaNs) without its scaling below 2^-80 (no half is there), rounded to half (exact double rounding at 24 >= 2 * 11 + 2 bits): every one of the 65,536 halves now gives numpy's correctly rounded square root (-0 stays -0). agx-bench and MLIR bench unchanged. lit fdiv-half.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E111 | **Audit, no change: correct rounding of the other arithmetic on the simulator's functional model** (after L15-E109 / E110 found half division and sqrt an ulp off): f32 fdiv, 1 / x, sqrt, frem, uitofp / sitofp i32 (2,097,152 mixed random / normal / special pairs, flushed operands or results accepted); f64 fadd, fmul, fdiv, sqrt (262,144), fma (60,000 against exact rational arithmetic), fptrunc f64 -> f32, sitofp i64 -> f64, uitofp u64 -> f32; i32 and i64 udiv / sdiv / urem / srem (262,144, shifts of random words, small divisors); f32 -> f16 truncation; bf16 add, div, sqrt (mul / fma differ only where an f32 product below 2^-126 flushes before rounding to the bf16 minimum normal, the flushing contract); half frem, fma, <2 x half> division: none wrong. | audit | -- |
| L15-E112 | **Adopted (accuracy fix): sinh / cosh near the overflow** e^(a - ln 2) for a >= 88 took a rounded a - ln 2: up to 24.45 ulp off (simulator, every third float in [0.5, 90)). Now the exponential's hi / lo argument, (a - 0.693359375) exact plus 2.12194440e-4: at most 1.62 ulp (sinh) / 1.47 (cosh) there, the overflow to inf exactly where the correctly rounded result overflows (every float in [89.3, 89.5]), specials unchanged; +4 instructions (sinh 77, cosh 67). Also measured, not changed: ulp errors of the float transcendentals over 2^20 inputs: exp / exp2 / exp10 / log / log2 / log10 / tanh / acos <= 1.3, asin 2.4, atan 2.7 (Cephes); sin / cos <= 1.6 up to 10^6 but up to 52 / 11 in [10^6, 2^22) (rint(x 2 / pi) at a 0.125-0.25 spacing can pick the neighbouring quadrant, leaving |r| up to about 0.98 for polynomials made for pi / 4; a fix-up of j costs ~6 instructions on every sin / cos) and the hardware's reduction beyond 2^22 (afn by design). | adopted | -- |
| L15-E113 | **Adopted (accuracy fix): half sin / cos with an exact reduction to turns** (without afn). They took one rounded x / 2pi in float, up to 2^-24 t turns off: over every half on the simulator sin was up to 1,517 ulp off (6,357 halves beyond 1 ulp), cos 4,800 (4,425). Now x times c_hi = 0.159149169921875 (13 bits: exact for a half's 11), its fraction (exact), + x * 5.773169959866209e-6 by an fma, the fraction again: sin at most 6.48 ulp (the small results near the zero crossings of large x, the remaining fma rounding), cos 4.61 (7 halves beyond 1 ulp); specials as before. +4 instructions (2 of them hoistable mov_imm). The other half functions measured over every half: exp, exp2, log, log2, sqrt, tanh at most 0.51 ulp. agx-bench and MLIR bench have no half sin / cos (unchanged). `-agx-precise-half-trig`; lit sin-half.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E114 | **Adopted (accuracy fix): bfloat sin / cos without afn by the float expansion.** Promoted to float they took the hardware's reduction (afn's): over every bfloat on the simulator sin was up to 256 ulp off already below 16 (14,399 of 33,280, small results lost), and up to 6,219 between 65,536 and 2^22. Now AGXMathExpansion's float sin / cos (Cody-Waite reduction, Cephes polynomials) rounded to bfloat: at most 0.5 ulp for every bfloat below 2^22 (beyond, both use the hardware's reduction, as float does: a Payne-Hanek reduction would be needed). Measured too: bfloat exp, log, log2, sqrt, tanh at most 0.5 ulp; exp2 one 128-ulp result at x = -126 (the result at the flushed boundary). agx-bench and MLIR bench have no bfloat sin / cos. lit sin-half.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E115 | **Adopted (net win): the exponentials' 2^n scale as an exponent addition where f32 flushes** (e^r in [0.7, 1.42]: its bits plus n << 23, +inf at or past the infinite exponent, the flushing multiplications' +0 below the normal range, a NaN e^r itself; instead of the two-factor ldexp's asr, sub, two shifts, two adds and two multiplications; 2^x / 10^x clamp to 128 / 38.6 instead of 130 / 40, still past their overflow, so n <= 128 and the sum stays below 2^31 — the first try returned 0 for 2^x above 128). Bit-identical to the previous code on the simulator's functional model: exp, exp2, exp10, tanh for every float with \|x\| in [16, 256) (both signs: every overflow / underflow edge) and 4,000,000 random words; pow on 4,194,304 pairs. In loops 5 instructions instead of 8 per exponential (the 4 constants hoisted); standalone even (44). agx-bench softmax_row 249.9 -> 244.1 cycles; MLIR bench softmax_rows 3,147.3 -> 3,122.4 cycles, 246.5 -> 236.4 per group at 32 (-4.1%), 25 -> 27 GPRs; silu_f16x4 230 -> 219 instructions, 102.8 -> 102.1 per group at 32 but 556.6 -> 573.2 at one group (+3%), 30 -> 31 GPRs; a cross-entropy row kernel (log-sum-exp) 14,014.7 -> 13,244.1 per group at 64 (-5.5%); pow 235.5 -> 225.1 cycles. ieee-fp32 keeps the multiplications (denormal results). `-agx-exp-integer-scale`; lit exp2-bounded.ll, exp-no-zero-add.ll, fp.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E116 | **Adopted: a float select on an integer comparison takes +0.0 as the immediate 0** (as the float-comparison selects already did; L15-E115's underflow select was the first user): no mov_imm of 0 and its register. exp 44 -> 43 instructions; MLIR bench silu_f16x4 31 -> 30 GPRs, 102.1 -> 100.2 per group at 32; softmax_rows 27 -> 26 GPRs, 236.4 -> 235.8; agx-bench cross_entropy 25 -> 24 GPRs, tput 863.9 -> 853.6, softmax_row 6 bytes smaller. Exponentials bit-identical to before (3,000,000 random words and the overflow / underflow edges). lit fp.ll regenerated. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E117 | **Adopted: a byte to half as the byte convert** (u8_to_f with a 16-bit destination, for the masked i16 / i32 a promoted u8 becomes, as to float already): 2 -> 1 instruction. agx-tv proves both forms; on the functional model every byte converts to its half. (Bytes of a vector load arrive zero-extended and already took one convert.) agx-bench unchanged. Also measured, already one instruction: s8 / u16 / s16 / u32 / s32 to half and float, half / float to 8-, 16- and 32-bit integers. lit u8-to-half.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E118 | **Adopted: a 16-bit store of x >> 16 as a store of x's high half register** (device and threadgroup memory, logical or arithmetic shift; TableGen patterns on the truncating store, since a DAG combine to a store of the truncation was folded back into the truncating store and llc did not terminate): no bfeil. A float rounded to bfloat and stored 8 -> 7 instructions; a byte store of x >> 16 likewise stores the high half's low byte (agx-tv); bit-identical on the functional model (4,194,304 floats with the specials and the rounding ties); agx-tv proves the srl / sra forms. agx-bench unchanged. lit store-high-half.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E119 | **Adopted: e^-y clamps y to the mirrored bounds and negates the clamp** (sigmoid / silu's exp(fneg x)): -clamp(y, -89, 104) is clamp(-y, -104, 89) exactly (a NaN stays one), and the negation folds into the multiplication by log2 e and the fma's addend modifier, instead of an xor before the selects (whose operands take no modifier). An exp(-x) + silu kernel 70 -> 66 instructions, bit-identical to the previous code on the simulator's functional model (every float with \|x\| in [16, 256), 4,000,000 random words, specials); MLIR bench silu_f16x4 219 -> 212 instructions, 575.4 -> 560.0 cycles, 100.2 -> 95.5 per group at 32 (-4.7%), 30 -> 27 GPRs; agx-bench unchanged. 2^-y and 10^-y likewise (their clamp of the argument itself): an exp2(-x) / exp10(-x) kernel 76 -> 72 instructions, bit-identical (every float with \|x\| in [16, 256), 3,000,000 random words). `-agx-exp-negated-clamp`; lit exp-neg-clamp.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E120 | **Adopted: 16-bit shifts by up to 4 as the 16-bit iadd / isub's shift** (alone, into an addition or a subtraction, as the 32-bit patterns already did; an i16 shift amount, which the 32-bit leaf did not match): 2 -> 1 instruction for x << s + y, and the shift off the unit integer multiplies and bit fields share. agx-tv proves the three forms; on the functional model (x << 3) + y and y - (x << 4) on 1,048,576 random pairs are right. agx-bench and MLIR bench unchanged. lit shift-add16.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E121 | **Adopted: L15-E91 on 16 bits** (and(sra(x, 15), m) and srl(sra(x, 15), 16 - k) as icmpsel slt x, 0, m, 0; x - ((x + b) & -2^k) as ((x + b) & (2^k - 1)) - b; i16 immediates): 16-bit sdiv by 4..256 4 -> 3 instructions, srem by 8..256 6 -> 4 (srem by 2 5 -> 4). agx-tv proves sdiv / srem by 2, 4, 8, 16, 256, 512 and the mask form (10 kernels). agx-bench unchanged. lit sdiv-srem-pow2-i16.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E122 | **Audit, no change: half and bfloat tan, asin, acos, atan, sinh, cosh over every value** (simulator's functional model, through the float expansions): at most 0.5 ulp for all six in both types (below 2^22). Also re-examined, no change: saxpy_vec4 / bsearch_while's uniform base copies inside the loop come from MachineSink's cycle sinking (A13's `sinkInstsIntoCyclesToAvoidSpills`, whose first stage sinks copies regardless of pressure); keeping them out costs GPRs (L15-E63); lower_bound_while's cost is its 64-bit (MLIR index) arithmetic and compare. | audit | -- |
| L15-E123 | **Adopted: (x >> 16) & m, m < 256, as the ALU and of the high half** (`and r, xh, m` instead of `bfeil r, 0, x, 16, mask m` on the shared imadbf unit; the third byte of a packed word): agx-tv proves m = 255, 7, 170, a uniform and a loaded source (5 kernels). int8_dp4a_gemv 1714.1 -> 1699.7 cycles, tput 122.7 -> 110.5, 400 -> 396 bytes; the rest of agx-bench and the MLIR bench unchanged. lit and-high-half. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E124 | **Adopted: 16-bit saturation as iadd.sat / isub.sat** (the saturation is to the destination size, as the simulator's functional model says): i16 sadd / uadd / ssub / usub.sat legal (8, 3, 8, 2 instructions -> 1 each), and a 32-bit value clamped to [-32768, 32767] (smin / smax either order) or umin 65535, then truncated, stored as 16 bits or packed into a half (PACK16), is `iadd.sat dst16, x(.sx), 0` (2 mov_imm + 2 icmpsel -> 1). agx-tv proves 12 kernels (scalar, vector, packed s/s, s/u, s/plain, device and threadgroup stores); the functional model matches every int16 sum and edge clamp. New kernel pcm_mix (int16 audio mixing, sat16(a + b) and sat16((a * gain) >> 8)): 37 -> 18 instructions, 207.9 -> 181.3 cycles, tput 15.0 -> 9.3; the rest of agx-bench and the MLIR bench unchanged. lit sat16. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E125 | **Adopted: 16-bit sources of 32-bit iadd / isub / imadd** (a sign- or zero-extended 16-bit value, i.e. sext / zext of an i16, sext_inreg 16, x >> 16 arithmetic or logical, x & 0xffff, is its 16-bit register, .sx for the sign; the functional model extends each source by its own flag): the extension (iadd .sx, asr, bfeil or bitop, often on the shared imadbf unit) goes, 2 -> 1 instructions per operand, 3 -> 1 for both. Also x & 0xffff with one use is the ALU move of the low half (E119), not the bfeil low-mask form. agx-tv proves the add / sub forms (6 kernels; the 32-bit multiplies time out and match the lifted IR by inspection), the functional model matches all 11 forms over random and edge words. pcm_mix 181.3 -> 177.3 cycles, 122 -> 106 bytes; lit and-wide-mask (3 -> 1), kernel-args (2 -> 1); the rest of agx-bench and the MLIR bench unchanged. lit ext16-src. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E126 | **Adopted: signed byte 1 of a word as one asr of its low half by 8** (asr sign-extends its source from the source size, so `asr r, x.l, 8` is sext(x << 16 >> 24)); also sra(sext_inreg(x, 16), s) and sra(sext i16, s) as one asr of the 16-bit register. Byte 1 was bfeil + isub 128 (with the shared x ^ 0x80808080, E86) or bfeil + xor + isub: the same one shift on the shared unit, without the ALU subtraction. agx-tv proves 4 kernels (dp4a_word times out on its multiplies; the functional model matches it over 8192 words with edge bytes). int8_dp4a_gemv 1699.7 -> 1682.1 cycles, tput 110.5 -> 109.0, 396 -> 380 bytes; the rest of agx-bench and the MLIR bench unchanged. lit asr-half, signed-bytes-shared. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E127 | **Adopted: 64-bit iadd / isub with a 16-bit source** (i64 +/- sext / zext of an i16, or of a 32-bit sign / zero-extended 16-bit value of the same kind, E125): the 16-bit register, .sx for the sign, instead of a mov_imm of the high word, a move of the low and the add (3 -> 1). agx-tv proves 3 kernels with argument operands (6 with 64-bit loads time out, the unchanged add_bool among them; the functional model matches all 6 over random and edge values). cuda_ddot (the soft f64 fma adds flags zero-extended to 64 bits) 32562 -> 32400 bytes, tput 2574.0 -> 2572.8; the rest of agx-bench and the MLIR bench unchanged. lit add64-ext16. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E128 | **Adopted (net): a 64-bit operand that is a pair with a zero high word** (BUILD_PAIR(x, 0), from 64-bit logic on the halves, e.g. and with 0xffffffff, 0xffff or lshr by 48) as the zero-extended 32-bit (or, for a 16-bit x, E127 16-bit) source of a 64-bit iadd / isub; also isub of zext / sext i32 as one isub with a 32-bit source. agx-tv proves 6 kernels. agx-bench: cuda_ddot 32400 -> 32294 bytes, 64 -> 63 GPRs; q4_matvec 4486.3 -> 4472.8 cycles; q8_matvec 2780.5 -> 2777.5, tput 233.6 -> 231.2; nothing worse. MLIR bench mixed (one instruction fewer each, scheduling moves): gemm_simd_matrix 1012.5 -> 1027.5 cycles, tput 115.9 -> 117.3; gemm_simdgroup_matrix 138.9 -> 147.2, tput 22.7 -> 22.5; silu 560.0 -> 564.9, tput 95.5 -> 95.2; softmax_rows 3120.2 -> 3121.3, tput 235.8 -> 232.1 (the new stalls are consumers placed right after their producer: the and -> iadd64 latency is 4 cycles in the simulator, the scheduling model says 2). lit add64-ext16. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E129 | **Not adopted, examined (E123 - E128 rounds)**: (1) compare operands as extended 16-bit sources (icmpsel / if_icmp compare each source extended from its own size, signed or not by the condition): in the corpus (226 lit, agx-bench and MLIR kernels) the extended halves feeding an icmpsel are select arms, which must match the destination size, not compare operands; nothing to gain, not built. (2) fmadd with a lane-shifted A source (the simulator's FMAddLane): the functional model does not execute it (a TODO) and only some lane offsets and modes are measured, with a round-to-odd intermediate; not sure-fire. (3) signed bytes 0 and 2 as two 16-bit shifts (shl 8, asr 8 of a half): one ALU op fewer than the and / isub 128 of E86 but two shifts on the shared unit instead of none (E40, E86 chose the ALU); only byte 1 (E126) is a strict win. (4) op survey leftovers, not pursued: cttz.i16 (or 0x10000, bitrev, ffs, isub: 5; a 16-bit bitrev / ffs might make it 3), variable rotl.i16 (6; xx-replicated shl could make it 3), llvm.ldexp.f32 (26, the generic scaled multiplies; an integer exponent add under flushing needs the e' = 0 rounding-up edge), frexp.f32 (21). Rare in kernels. (5) blur_rgba8 / q4_matvec / q8_matvec loops: byte 1 / 3 extracts before u16_to_f converts, nibble isub 8, an fadd of 0.0 for the first sum (needs nsz) and 2-byte-aligned byte-pair loads of 34 / 18-byte blocks are inherent. (6) agx-tv: a <4 x i16> vector load of partly poison memory fails (lifter loads whole i32 words, so one poison lane makes its neighbour poison; the old llc fails the same way); !noundef loads verify. 64-bit adds with 64-bit loads time out where the same forms with argument operands prove. (7) the scheduling model gives ALU results 2 cycles; the simulator stalls an and -> iadd64 consumer 4 (E128's MLIR moves). Not changed: it would move every kernel. | audit | -- |
| L15-E130 | **Adopted: vectors of bfloats loaded and stored as words** (AGXCodeGenPrepare, `-agx-word-bfloat-loads`): bfloat is not a register type, so a <2k x bfloat> load extended to floats became a 16-bit load per element and a shift each, and a store a 16-bit store per element. Now a load whose users all extend it (as a vector, or the scalarizer's elements) is a <2k x i16> load, each element zext and shl 16 (the DAG loads it as k words; the high element's float is the word and 0xffff0000 on the ALU, the low one the word shifted), and an aligned store stores the elements' bits as <2k x i16> (one store of words, each pair packed with one 16-bit move). A first version loaded <k x i32> words: alive-tv showed it more poisonous than the source (a poison bfloat makes its neighbour's word poison), so the integers are 16-bit, per element; alive-tv proves the load (4 elements) and the store (2) rewrites at the IR level; agx-tv proves the machine code with !noundef loads (with partly poison memory the lifter's word loads cannot, E129); the functional model gives bit-identical results to the old code for gemv_bf16 (infinities, denormals) and axpy_bf16 (every special pair, four scales). New kernels: gemv_bf16 (gemv_f16 in bf16) 57 -> 50 instructions, 5967.3 -> 5526.8 cycles, tput 315.9 -> 191.7 (-39%); axpy_bf16 55 -> 43, 268.2 -> 200.0 cycles, tput 20.9 -> 13.8 (-34%); the rest of agx-bench and the MLIR bench unchanged. lit bfloat-words. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E131 | **Audit, no change**: (1) the scheduling model against the simulator's instruction bench (results/2026-10-11-instruction-bench): ALU 2 (and / iadd / fadd32 / fmul32 / fmadd32 1.99), WriteALU64 5 / 4 (iadd64 4.85 / 3.89), WriteIMul 3 / 2 (imadd, bfi, bfeil, asr, convert 2.94 / 2.0), WriteSFU 5 / 2 (rcp 4.76, exp2 4.76), WriteSFUSlow 6 / 4 (rsqrt 5.71 / 3.81), reductions 24 (22.9), matrix 17 / 16 (16.2 / 15.5): they agree; E128's stalls are local placement, not a wrong latency. (2) register cache hints (SRC_MOD_CACHE): never emitted; the bench charges a cached source 2 issue cycles (fadd32_cache 2.03 vs 1.04), so none is right. (3) fmadd32 / icmpsel / fcmpsel reading three distinct 32-bit GPRs issue in 2 cycles (uniforms, immediates and 16-bit operands not counted): gemv's accumulating fmadd32 is that form and stays. (4) e^x / 2^x keep a clamp, two integer selects on the scaled word and the NaN select: an overflowed word (exponent 255, any mantissa) and a NaN input's word overlap, so only x tells them apart. (5) bfloat rounding (and, two iadds per element): no shorter form; the NaN test is already skipped after float arithmetic (also needed after a simd_fadd, once per kernel). (6) llvm.ldexp / llvm.frexp (26 / 21 instructions) occur in none of the lit, agx-bench or MLIR kernels: not pursued. | audit | -- |
| L15-E132 | **Adopted: 4- and 8-byte device accesses at a 16-bit-unit index in the 16-bit format** (SelectDeviceAddr32Via16, `-agx-via16-addresses`): an i32 / f32 / i64 / f64 / two-word access at base + 2 * i (a half or bfloat vector at an element offset, e.g. W + row * K with K at run time) did not fold i into the 32-bit format's index (its unit 4 bytes), so the 64-bit address was computed (two uniform copies and an iadd64, 3.9 issue cycles); the 16-bit format with xy / xyzw fills the same register halves with the index folded. Only where the 32-bit form would not fold. agx-tv proves the 4-byte load and store (the 8-byte ones time out on 64-bit memory); the functional model gives the old code's results for 8-byte loads, i64 loads and 8-byte stores at aligned addresses (misaligned ones, undefined for the IR's alignment, differ: the 32-bit format rounds the address down to 4) and for gemv_f16_rowptr. New kernel gemv_f16_rowptr (gemv_f16 over a const half * row): 43 -> 40 instructions, 3776.3 -> 2890.6 cycles, tput 141.9 -> 102.6 (-28%); the bf16 dot loop of bfloat-words 3 instructions fewer per load; the rest of agx-bench and the MLIR bench unchanged. lit device-addr-via16. Hardware: an i16-format access with several elements is not measured (B33 measured the i32 format); these are only at the alignment the IR states (4 / 8 bytes), as the i8-format byte-vector loads on by default are, while R23 gates only the sub-4-byte case; their cost against an i32-format access is the model's assumption (follow-up item 15). | adopted | -- |
| L15-E133 | **Adopted (net): flow-block code hoisted above an if whose condition is its comparison** (AGXHoistFromElseFlow): the pass moves the side-effect-free code of an if / else's flow block, which runs for the then side only before the else, into the dominating if block, but only recognized an if block ending in AGX_IF, not the AGX_IF_ICMP / FCMP / ICMP16 that AGXFoldOperands makes of an if on a comparison (E59). Those flow blocks kept their code, and AGXLowerControlFlow ran it twice, before the else for the then side and after it again for the else side. Found as identical instructions on both sides of an else_fcmp (51 in the corpus: layernorm's 7 xor / and / iadd lane indices, sgemm_reg4x4's accumulator zeroing, jacobi2d's 3 index computations). The functional model gives the old code's results for else-flow-hoist's kernel (divergent switch values, four mixes; agx-tv times out on it). agx-bench: attn_decode 2268 -> 2256 bytes, tput 2745.9 -> 2731.8; conv3x3 tput 2533.1 -> 2530.0; jacobi2d, sgemm_reg4x4 smaller; MLIR: layernorm_rows 507 -> 500 instructions, tput 145.3 -> 144.1; layernorm_allreduce / ln 520 -> 513, cycles 2239.2 -> 2238.1, tput 147.4 -> 148.3 (+0.6%, the one worse number). lit else-flow-hoist (FOLD). | adopted | -- |
| L15-E134 | **Adopted: E133 for flow-block code reading uniform registers** (AGXHoistFromElseFlow): an instruction with a physical register operand stayed, so jacobi2d's imadd of the width argument (u4) and icmpsel against u5 still ran on both sides of the else; a physical register that MachineRegisterInfo::isConstantPhysReg says nothing in the function writes (the preloaded argument uniforms) reads the same anywhere. jacobi2d 600 -> 584 bytes, 842.9 -> 839.8 cycles; the rest of agx-bench and the MLIR bench unchanged. The duplicates left around an else_fcmp (18 in the corpus) are mov_imm 0 of phi copies, one per side by construction. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E135 | **Rejected: no jump over short if bodies with loads or stores** (`isShortALURun` taking up to 4 instructions including memory accesses, which touch nothing with an empty execution mask). agx-tv proves it (guarded device store, guarded load into a phi, threadgroup store), and the simulator calls it better everywhere: conv3x3 tput 2530.0 -> 2088.7, bitonic_smem 481.1 -> 446.2, saxpy 4.9 -> 3.9, scan_blelloch, rmsnorm, layernorm_f16, jacobi2d, the layernorm MLIR kernels, nothing worse. But the hardware measurement B31 (AGX-Performance-Simulator tasks/ptx-re-blockers.md, 2026-10-20) found a masked device_load + wait ~13-20 ns and a masked device_store ~9-12 ns, more than an active store, against ~1-4 ns for masked ALU instructions: its rule is to drop the jump over ALU-only bodies only, which -agx-max-unskipped-instrs does. The simulator's empty-mask memory cost disagrees with that measurement (a simulator follow-up, not a backend change). | rejected | -- |
| L15-E136 | **Adopted: a shuffle's lane index computed straight into r0h** (AGXLowerControlFlow lowerShuffleIndex, `-agx-shuffle-index-into-r0h`): a register shuffle index is moved to r0h before the shuffle (bitop 0101, r0h, xl); when the index is the low half of the result of an iadd / isub / bitop (or their 16-bit forms) at most 16 instructions back in the block, read by nothing else before the shuffle and dead after it (or rewritten by the shuffle), and its sources are not rewritten in between, that instruction becomes its 16-bit form writing r0h right before the shuffle (the low 16 bits of these results depend only on the sources' low 16 bits), and the move goes. The divergent shuffles' quad gathers (index | 1, | 2, | 3) are the case: 426 moves to r0h in the corpus, 294 after such an instruction. MLIR: layernorm_rows 500 -> 452 instructions, 2427.4 -> 2401.6 cycles, tput 144.1 -> 139.5; layernorm_allreduce / ln 513 -> 465, 2238.1 -> 2212.3, tput 148.3 -> 141.1; shuffle-divergent 192 -> 180 and shuffle-identity 73 -> 67 instructions; agx-bench unchanged. agx-tv cannot lift r0h shuffle indices; the functional model gives the old code's results for all 10 kernels of shuffle-divergent / shuffle-identity (three inputs each), the two of shuffle-index-r0h, and layernorm_rows (bit-identical, within 1e-6 of numpy). lit shuffle-index-r0h. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E137 | **Adopted: || and && of comparisons in their own blocks merged before structurization** (AGXCodeGenPrepare foldConditionChains, `-agx-fold-condition-chains=2`: SimplifyCFG's foldBranchToCommonDest on every conditional branch, up to 2 speculated instructions; then a SimplifyCFG without branch simplification before LowerSwitch, `-agx-preisel-simplifycfg=2`, makes the phis of the empty side selects). Every branch is an if, a pop and often a jump; argmax's steps (larger value, or equal with a smaller index: two branches into an empty block, three instructions the default bonus threshold of 1 would not speculate, and the PTX front end's .opt.ll keeps them) became 4 icmpsel / fcmpsel per step. A first version ran the whole SimplifyCFG: its branch simplification also makes switches of comparison chains, and indirect-calls' promoted call (a chain ending in a trap) then lowered to a tree the structurizer left unstructured (llc failed); only the one fold runs now. agx-tv proves skip-jumps, trap-exit, control-flow (6 kernels) and a shuffle-free cond-chain with the new default (branch-widened-cond times out; its new code is lt ? 1 : eq by inspection, NaN false as in the IR); the functional model gives the old code's argmax results and numpy's for five inputs (random, all ties, repeated maxima, NaNs, rounded). argmax 171 -> 150 instructions, 1038 -> 950 bytes, tput 666.6 -> 649.1; layernorm_f16 tput 130.3 -> 129.0; the rest of agx-bench and the MLIR bench unchanged. The CFG-lowering tests (branch-widened-cond, control-flow, experimental-call-masked, skip-jumps, trap-exit) run with both off to keep their branches. lit cond-chain. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E138 | **Adopted: a 64-bit operand that is a 32-bit value and its sign as a .sx source** (agx_sext_pair: BUILD_PAIR(x, x >> 31), or BUILD_PAIR(s, s) for s with 32 sign bits): the 64-bit (x ^ s) - s of an absolute value (abs.i64, a signed 64-bit division's operands, s64 -> float) paired the sign mask with itself by a move for the isub; now isub / iadd with s.sx (2 -> 1). agx-tv proves abs64, a sub of the (x, x >> 31) pair and an add of ashr(y, 63) (3 kernels). permute4d 13516 -> 13372 bytes; fp.ll's s64 -> f32 one move fewer; the rest of agx-bench and the MLIR bench unchanged. lit add64-ext16. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E139 | **Adopted: correctly rounded division by a constant folds the divisor's part** (lowerFDIV32, `-agx-fdiv-const-divisor`): for a normal constant b, d (b's significand in [2, 4) with its sign), y = RN(1 / d) (APFloat; the rcp + Newton step gives the rounded reciprocal for every significand, so the same y) and pb = 2^(128 - eb) are constants, and b needs no NaN test; for pb >= 2^-100 (|b| below about 2^101) q * pb is exact, so pb first is right for every a and the test of a's size with its two selects goes (pb = 1 for b in [2, 4) is no multiply at all). a / 3.0 17 -> 10 instructions (7 without the constants a loop hoists). The functional model gives the old code's bits for 12 divisors (3, 10, 0.1, 7, -3, 2.5, 0.75, 1e30, 1e-30, 2^-126, 3e38, 6e-20; the last four keep the generic path or a pb < 1 multiply) over 4M random words plus every exponent with six significands and both signs. agx-bench and the MLIR bench have no constant division (unchanged). lit fdiv-const. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E140 | **Adopted: a constant +-2^k dividend's quotient is the rounded reciprocal** (lowerFDIV32): a1 = +-1, so q0 = +-y is already RN(a1 / d) and Markstein's correction (two fmas) changes nothing (it was skipped for 1 / b only). 2 / x 12 -> 10 instructions. The functional model gives the old code's bits for dividends 2, -1, 0.5, 4, -8, 2^-120, 2^-126, 2^127 (the last three through the size test) over 4M random divisors plus every exponent with six significands and both signs. agx-bench and the MLIR bench unchanged. lit fdiv-const (two_over). | adopted | -- |
| L15-E141 | **Adopted: E139 for the correctly rounded half division** (lowerFDIV's f16 path, `-agx-fdiv-const-divisor`): a normal constant half b gives y = RN32(1 / b) at compile time (the Newton step's value, halves being far from float's flushed and infinite ranges), which also serves as the y0 of a's zeros, infinities and NaNs (exact for them, with b's sign); a y0 equal to y makes the fallback product the quotient q1 itself. x / 3.0h 10 -> 7 instructions. Every half dividend (65536) gives the old code's bits, and numpy's correctly rounded quotient, for b = 3, 0.1, -7, 65504, 2^-14, -1, 0.333, 255. lit fdiv-const (half_by3). | adopted | -- |
| L15-E142 | **Adopted: a multiply-high and a multiply of the same operands as one widening multiply** (DAG combine on MULHU / MULHS, `-agx-mul-lohi-pairs`: a MUL of the same two operands, either order, exists -> UMUL_LOHI / SMUL_LOHI, E8's one 32 x 32 -> 64-bit imadd, whose low word is the product in both signednesses). Philox's rounds compute mul.hi and mul.lo of each counter word by the same constant: they were a 64-bit imadd and a 32-bit imadd each (39 -> 20 imadds). philox 832 -> 680 bytes, 257.2 -> 227.2 cycles, tput 69.1 -> 50.9 (-26%); the rest of agx-bench and the MLIR bench unchanged. agx-tv times out on the multiplies; the functional model gives the exact lo ^ hi and signed-constant lo + hi for 300 operand pairs (edge values included) and philox's old output for 65536 numbers and two seeds. lit mul-lohi. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E143 | **Adopted: a float saturated to an 8-bit integer is the convert's 8-bit mode** (AGXISD::CVT_F_TO_U8 / S8, `-agx-convert8`; mode 0 / 1, 2 / 3 rounding to nearest even for a rint / roundeven / nearbyint with no other use): B27's hardware data (1.37 billion inputs) has every float-to-integer mode saturate to its type with NaN 0 and 8- and 16-bit results zero-extended, so fptoui.sat.i8 is the convert (2 -> 1) and fptosi.sat.i8 the convert and a sign extension that a byte store drops (4 -> 1: it was the convert to 16 bits, a mov_imm of -128 and two icmpsel). quantization's round-then-saturate to int8 4 -> 1. agx-tv proves f32 -> s8, f16 -> u8 / s8, s8 -> half and a sign-extended use (5 kernels; the roundeven one times out) after fixing the lifter: it read every integer-to-float-named mode with the float-to-integer direction as rounding to nearest, true of the 16 / 32-bit names but not of u8_to_f / s8_to_f (modes 0 / 1 truncate); it now decodes the mode number as the simulator does. The functional model matches numpy for 4M random floats and edge values (f32 -> s8, the sign-extended use, the roundeven form) and every half (f16 -> u8 / s8). agx-bench (quant_i8 clamps before a plain fptosi) and the MLIR bench unchanged. lit convert8, fptoi-sat. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E144 | **Adopted: a clamp at an 8- / 16-bit integer's maximum before its conversion is the saturating conversion** (AGXCodeGenPrepare satConvertClamps, `-agx-sat-convert-clamps`): fptosi / fptoui (minnum(y, h)) to i8 / i16 with h at least the type's maximum and y a maxnum / maximumnum with a non-NaN constant (never NaN) is fpto[su]i.sat(y) (above the maximum the conversion was poison or the maximum, and truncation commutes with the bound); with E143 the upper bound is the convert's own saturation. Also: the 8-bit convert takes a ftrunc operand (round's lowering), and a byte-vector store's values drop extensions of their byte (SimplifyMultipleUseDemandedBits on STORE_V2_I8 / V4_I8). alive-tv proves the rewrite for f32 -> s8 (40 min), f32 -> u8 with h = 300 and f16 -> s16; the functional model gives the old quant_i8's bits for 1M float4s (random words, normal values, rounding edges, NaN, infinities) at three scales. quant_i8 268 -> 230 bytes, 11 -> 10 GPRs, 155.6 -> 151.2 cycles, tput 17.5 -> 15.5 (-11%); unorm8 conversion (x * 255 clamped to [0, 255]) 4 -> 3; the rest of agx-bench and the MLIR bench unchanged. lit sat-clamp. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E145 | **Adopted: a saturating conversion's own bounds go** (satConvertClamps, second step): fptoui.sat (maxnum(m, l)) with l <= 0 is fptoui.sat (m) for every m (NaN gives l under maxnum, converted to 0, and 0 from the conversion); fptosi.sat (maxnum(m, l)) with l at most the type's minimum and fpto[su]i.sat (minnum(m, h)) with h at least its maximum are the conversion of m where m is a maxnum / minnum with a non-NaN constant. A unorm8 conversion (x * 255 clamped to [0, 255]) is the product and the convert: 4 -> 2 instructions (3 after E144). alive-tv proves the f32 -> u8 lower bound (l = 0, 50 min), f16 -> u8 with l = -5, f16 -> s8 with l = -200 below a minnum and f16 -> s16 with h = 32768 above a maxnum; the functional model matches numpy for the unorm8 kernel over 4M floats (random words and [-0.5, 1.5]). agx-bench and the MLIR bench unchanged. lit sat-clamp. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E146 | **Adopted: a device word loaded only to take its bytes apart is a byte vector load** (AGXCodeGenPrepare loadBytesApart, `-agx-byte-loads`): the bit ranges of the word each value holds are followed through trunc, lshr by whole bytes, low masks and zext; when every value used otherwise is one byte, zero-extended, the i32 load is a <4 x i8> load (the byte format, each byte zero-extended in a 16-bit half, E-era LOAD_V4_I8) and those values its elements: no bfeil / and / shift per byte (RGBA8 pixels). Per-element loads are no more poisonous than the word. A first version took sign-extended bytes too: asr-half and signed-bytes-shared got worse (zero-extended halves lose E86's one xor of the word for all its signed bytes), so a byte with a sext / sitofp user keeps the word. agx-tv proves two kernels (all four bytes through the trunc / lshr / and forms; byte 0 and byte 3 as i32); the functional model gives the old blur_rgba8's every pixel of a 64 x 48 image. blur_rgba8 164 -> 146 instructions, 1102 -> 958 bytes, 518.3 -> 492.0 cycles, tput 83.9 -> 65.7 (-22%); and-high-half's loaded kernel 3 -> 1; the rest of agx-bench and the MLIR bench unchanged. lit byte-loads. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E147 | **Adopted: a device word stored from four separate values' bytes is a byte vector store** (AGXCodeGenPrepare storeBytesTogether, `-agx-byte-stores`): a stored i32 made only of bytes (bytePieces: or, zext, shl by whole bytes, and 255, a value shifted into the top byte) of four values from different roots is a <4 x i8> store (STORE_V4_I8, the byte format from 16-bit halves: each value's low byte, no packing); per-byte poison is a refinement of the word's. Bytes of one word (byte-permute.ll's prmt forms) are skipped: their bit-field inserts build the halves already, and a byte store would only add moves of the uniforms. agx-tv proves two kernels (four 16-bit values through and / shl / or / zext; i32 and i8 pieces with a top-byte shl); the functional model gives the old blur_rgba8's pixels for 64 x 48 and 48 x 32 images. blur_rgba8 (after E146) 958 -> 930 bytes, 492.0 -> 484.8 cycles, tput 65.7 -> 63.8 (together with E146: 518.3 -> 484.8 cycles, tput 83.9 -> 63.8, -24%); the rest of agx-bench and the MLIR bench unchanged. lit byte-stores. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E148 | **Adopted: E144 / E147 for more forms, and a byte minus a constant from its half**: (1) the never-NaN test of satConvertClamps also asks ValueTracking (isKnownNeverNaN: a sum of products of converted bytes), so luma's minnum(y, 255) before its fptoui to i8 goes (the convert rounds and saturates); (2) bytePieces takes a constant or-ed in (its nonzero bytes, an alpha of 255) and a value known to fit in a byte (computeKnownBits: clamps to [0, 255]); (3) an extended 16-bit value plus a small negative constant (byte - 128) is ISUB_I32_A16 of the 16-bit register, ahead of agx_neg_imm8's 32-bit isub (AddedComplexity 11), which had needed a move. agx-tv proves a pack of two umin clamps, a masked argument and alpha, and two bytes minus 128 / 200; the functional model gives the previous rgba_gray (64K pixels with edge colours) and yuv2rgb (64K pixels) outputs. New kernels: rgba_gray 25 -> 23 instructions, 126.5 -> 123.3 cycles, tput 9.2 -> 8.8; yuv2rgb 46 -> 41, 209.8 -> 201.4, tput 17.7 -> 15.8 (-11%) (against E147; against E136: rgba_gray 26 -> 23, tput 10.2 -> 8.8); the rest of agx-bench and the MLIR bench unchanged. lit byte-stores-known. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E149 | **Adopted: E146 for 16-bit words** (loadBytesApart: an i16 device load, 2-byte aligned, taken apart into its two bytes is a <2 x i8> load, the byte format xy): a UV pair's and 255 / lshr 8 (a bfeil and an and) go, 4 -> 2 instructions in byte-loads' uv. agx-tv proves it. agx-bench and the MLIR bench unchanged (yuv2rgb loads <2 x i8> already). lit byte-loads (uv). | adopted | -- |
| L15-E150 | **Rejected: E147 for 16-bit words** (two bytes packed into an i16 store as a <2 x i8> store): the pack was one bfi of the bytes into a half, and the byte store needs the two values in a register's halves, one move: 2 instructions either way (a bfi on the shared unit against a move on the ALU, and an i16 x against an i8 xy store), not a sure gain. | rejected | -- |
| L15-E151 | **Adopted: a 64-bit stride multiply as one 32 x 32 -> 64-bit multiply and a shifted add**: (1) lowerMUL64 rewrites mul(shl(x, k), y) to shl(mul(x, y), k) when x and y are both sign- or both zero-extended 32-bit values (ComputeNumSignBits / computeKnownBits > 32), so a row offset sext(i) * (sext(n) << 2) is one imadd of the two 32-bit registers (.sx) and the shift goes into the iadd64's lsl; (2) sinkMulExtensions (AGXCodeGenPrepare) clones the operands of a mul i64 of two extensions of one kind (a sext / zext of an i32, or a shl of one by a constant) that come from another block into the mul's block, since the DAG works per block and a stride computed in the entry block had reached the loop as an opaque 64-bit value (three imadds and an asr per offset). A first version cloned any extended operand, whatever the other one: permute4d's tput went 297.0 -> 304.4 and sgemm_reg4x4's cycles up, so it only takes a multiply both of whose operands are extensions. index_add's inner loop goes from imadd / asr / imadd / imadd / iadd / iadd per row offset to imadd / iadd lsl 2: 740 -> 580 bytes, 28 -> 26 GPRs, 8482.8 -> 7894.0 cycles, tput 627.6 -> 421.6 (-33%); conv3x3 1210 -> 1204 bytes, 40 -> 39 GPRs; sgemm_reg4x4 has one imadd read u7.sx in place of its copy in r4 (same bytes, GPRs and cycles; tput 2661.6 -> 2682.6, +0.8%, from the resident groups' interleaving: the model charges nothing for a uniform source); the rest of agx-bench and the MLIR bench unchanged. The rewrite is (x * 2^k) * y = (x * y) * 2^k mod 2^64 for any x and y (the extensions only decide that it pays); agx-tv and alive-tv time out on the 64-bit multiplies (shared_stride, xblock, the IR identity for k = 2), so the functional model checked index_add against the E147 llc and a numpy reference for (n, dim, rows) = (64, 256, 32), (7, 100, 5), (13, 512, 13): identical. lit mul64-shl-stride. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E152 | **Adopted: a shuffle at select(c, i, thread index) as a select of the shuffle at i and the value** (shuffleSelectedLanes in lowerDivergentShuffles, `-agx-shuffle-selects`): a thread reads its own value at its own index, so shuffle(v, select(c, i, lane)) = select(c, shuffle(v, i), v), and a shuffle at lane ^ k (k < 32) is shuffle_xor(v, k), whose index is uniform. A mask of the index that keeps the thread index's bits (MLIR's and 65535) is looked through. The partial-SIMD-group butterflies of MLIR's subgroup reductions (the partner where it is in range, else the lane) were split into four shuffles and three selects each (the quad merge of the low index bits): layernorm_rows 452 -> 330 instructions, 26 -> 25 GPRs; layernorm_allreduce / ln 465 -> 343, 33 -> 32 GPRs. That path does not run in the costed launch (whole SIMD-groups): cycles are unchanged and the hot code differs only in register numbers (tput32 139.5 -> 139.2, 141.1 -> 142.2 from the groups' interleaving). agx-bench unchanged. The lifter has no shuffles and alive2 models a shuffle as an opaque read, so the functional model checked it: the E151 llc's four shuffles and the new shuffle_xor against a reference for k = 1, 2, 4, 8, 16, n in {-5, 0, 3, 13, 24, 31, 32, 100} and 7, 32, 48 or 64 threads (partial SIMD-groups, whose missing source lanes the select drops): 5909 lanes, all equal. lit shuffle-select-lane. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E153 | **Rejected: value ranges on the special-register intrinsics** (thread_index_in_simdgroup and simdgroup_index_in_threadgroup in [0, 32), thread_index_in_threadgroup and thread_position_in_threadgroup in [0, 1024), threads_per_threadgroup in [1, 1025), as AMDGPU's work-item ids): InstCombine drops masks such as and 65535 of the thread index, and MLIR's layernorm_allreduce went 2212.3 -> 2098.3 cycles, but a loop index known not negative lets LSR take a 64-bit pointer induction variable in place of the 32-bit signed register offset: gemv_f16 184 -> 218 bytes, 9 -> 11 GPRs, tput 87.0 -> 121.8; gemv_bf16, gemv_f16_rowptr, layernorm_f16 (tput 129.0 -> 200.4) and embedding as much worse, with the [0, 32) ranges alone too (gemv starts its strided loop at the lane): L14-E1 again, which is why AGXSpecialRegisterRanges gives them only after LSR. E152 checks the thread index's bits itself; E154 takes the gain. | rejected | -- |
| L15-E154 | **Adopted: the special register ranges during straight-line strength reduction, taken off before LSR** (AGXStraightLineSRPass, `-agx-early-sr-ranges`): E153's gain came from the passes before LSR, which with tid < 1024 see zext(tid + 256) as zext(tid) + 256, so the loads at tid + 256k share one address and take immediate offsets (device_load ..., 256) in place of an add and a constant move each. The ranges (AGX::addSpecialRegisterRanges, the bounds of AGXSpecialRegisterRanges) are given for that pipeline only and removed after it, so LSR sees none (L14-E1) and AGXSpecialRegisterRanges gives them again before instruction selection. (Giving them only before CodeGenPrepare, or keeping them through the vectorizer and AGXCodeGenPrepare up to LSR, changed nothing.) MLIR: layernorm_allreduce / ln 2212.3 -> 2098.3 cycles, 343 -> 336 instructions, tput32 142.2 -> 137.6; layernorm_rows 2401.6 -> 2266.9 cycles, tput32 139.2 -> 138.5, 25 -> 29 GPRs; agx-bench: scan_blelloch 1526 -> 1512 bytes, 20 -> 18 GPRs, 5148.0 -> 5109.0 cycles, tput 137.5 -> 135.9; the rest unchanged. The bounds are the hardware's (a threadgroup is 1 to 1024 threads, AGXUsage.rst); the functional model gives the E152 llc's sums for threadgroups of 1, 32, 100, 256 and 1024 threads (sr-range-offsets). lit sr-range-offsets. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E155 | **Adopted: a loop-carried variable's new value computed after the old value's last use in its block** (sinkLoopCarriedDefs in AGXAnnotateControlFlow, `-agx-sink-loop-carried-defs`): mandelbrot's z.re' = cr + (z.re^2 - z.im^2) came before 2 z.re (for z.im'), so both were live and the loop copied z.re' into z.re's register each iteration; instruction selection keeps the block's order there, so the new value (the header phi's, or what the structurizer's flow phi gives it from that block) moves after the last use when it does not touch memory and nothing between uses it. mandelbrot 352 -> 346 bytes, 14214.1 -> 13932.5 cycles (-2.0%), tput 1791.2 -> 1720.3 (-4.0%); the rest of agx-bench and the MLIR bench unchanged (control-flow's nested_loops: an add and a compare swap). The functional model gives the E154 llc's 1024 x 1024 mandelbrot over 384 columns (40 iterations, all 41 counts) and smaller grids, identical. (The structurizer's undefined flow-phi values of loop-carried variables, given the header phi's value instead, changed nothing: the remaining copy, of the counter, is there because its old value is read after an early exit.) lit loop-carried-order. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E156 | **Rejected: maximumnum / minimumnum's equal operands as the operand of the result's sign** (select on the first operand's sign bit, in place of the and / or of the bits and its flush where f32 compares flush): one instruction less per max (attn_decode 2256 -> 2218 bytes, -0.4% cycles; softmax_online 1498 -> 1454 bytes, -0.8%; maxbf 19 -> 17 instructions), and the functional model's results for all 576 pairs of 24 special values (zeros, denormals, normals, infinities, NaNs) are an operand of the right sign. But it returns a denormal operand where the flushed operands' answer is a zero, and agx-tv (alive2's preserve-sign model) rejects that: maximumnum(-0, -denormal) and maximumnum(+denormal, +0) verify with the E155 llc and not with this one. (maximumnum(+denormal, -1.0) fails with both: the unequal operands' fcmpsel gtn passes a denormal operand through, a gap of the current lowering that agx-tv shows and that flushing every result would close at an instruction per max.) | rejected | -- |
| L15-E157 | **Adopted: a loop's integer address ((inv + i) << c) + base as (base + (inv << c)) + (i << c), the first sum before the loop** (hoistInvariantAddressParts in AGXCodeGenPrepare, `-agx-hoist-address-parts`): PTX computes addresses in 64-bit integers, and argmax's row walk, ((row offset + sext(i)) << 2) + buffer, took two 64-bit adds each iteration; modulo 2^64 the shift distributes over the sum, so the invariant part goes to the preheader and the load takes the base and the 32-bit index (device_load ..., r6_r7, r8, signed). Loop strength reduction does not see it: i's add has no nsw, so its extension is not a recurrence. Only where the address is used by inttoptrs and the inner sum and shift have no other uses. argmax 14 -> 11 GPRs, 14630.2 -> 11393.9 cycles (-22%), tput 649.1 -> 330.8 (-49%), 950 -> 954 bytes (the preheader's add); the rest of agx-bench and the MLIR bench unchanged. alive-tv proves the identity for 16-bit values (sext i8 index, the same modular arithmetic as 64 bits, where it times out); the functional model gives the E155 llc's argmax and numpy's for (rows, n, threads) = (4, 1000, 64), (3, 37, 32), (2, 4096, 256), (5, 1, 64), (1, 300, 128), (6, 5000, 1024). lit loop-address-hoist. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E158 | **Adopted: the sign splat of an index known not negative as 0, while the special register bounds are on** (AGXStraightLineSRPass, `-agx-nonnegative-sign-splats`): a signed division or remainder by a power of two of l (sgemm_reg4x4's tile copy, l = ty * 16 + tx stepping by 256 nsw; l / 16, l % 16, l / 64, l % 64) keeps l >> 31 as its correction; with E154's bounds ValueTracking knows the recurrence is not negative (start a sum of thread positions, step positive without signed overflow), so x >> (bits - 1) is 0 there and the division and remainder select as a shift and a mask (the correction's select, add and subtract go: the test's loop 13 -> 10 instructions). sgemm_reg4x4 3336 -> 3284 bytes, 22384.0 -> 22182.4 cycles (-0.9%), tput 2682.6 -> 2618.8 (-2.4%); the rest of agx-bench and the MLIR bench unchanged. The functional model gives the E157 llc's C and numpy's A @ B for (M, N, K) = (128, 128, 64), (64, 192, 48), (64, 64, 16). lit nonneg-sign-splat. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E159 | **Adopted: a loop's compare of its index with a constant as a compare of the incremented index, where that cannot overflow** (postIncrementCompares in AGXAnnotateControlFlow, `-agx-post-increment-compares`): InstCombine writes l + 256 < 1024 as l < 768, so the exit test at the end of the iteration reads l next to l + 256 and the loop copies one into the other's register each iteration (sgemm_reg4x4's tile copy, E158's test). Where scalar evolution's signed range of l (with the special register bounds) shows that l + c and k + c do not overflow, the test is of l + c against k + c, the same for every l (the add need not carry nsw: LSR drops it), and E155 then orders the add after l's other uses. sgemm_reg4x4 3284 -> 3278 bytes, 22182.4 -> 22099.2 cycles (-0.4%), tput 2618.8 -> 2643.6 (+0.9%, the resident groups' interleaving: the change is the copy gone and the add later); the rest of agx-bench and the MLIR bench unchanged. The functional model gives the E158 llc's sgemm (and numpy's A @ B) for (128, 128, 64) and (64, 192, 48), and its tile test for 1, 100, 256 and 1024 threads. lit nonneg-sign-splat (10 -> 9 instructions in the loop). | adopted | -- |
| L15-E160 | **Adopted: a 64-bit index modulo a power of two in 32 bits, and a scaled field as one bfi** (narrowMaskedIndices in AGXCodeGenPrepare, `-agx-narrow-masked-indices`, and an agx_shl_field pattern): MLIR computes a broadcast operand's index (i * 4) % 4096 as (zext(i) << 2) & 4092 in 64 bits (a 64-bit shift-add, a mov_imm, an and, the base's copy to registers and a 64-bit add); the mask clears every bit the 32-bit shift loses, so it is zext((i << 2) & 4092) and the load takes the base and the 32-bit index; (x << s) & ((2^k - 1) << s) with a mask beyond the 8-bit immediates is then one bfi of x's low k bits at offset s (bfi r2, 0, r1, 2, mask 0x3FF) in place of a shift, a mov_imm and an and. The test's broadcast add 13 -> 8 instructions. MLIR silu / silu_f16x4 211 -> 209 instructions, 564.9 -> 554.6 cycles (-1.8%), tput32 95.2 -> 93.7; agx-bench unchanged. alive-tv proves the narrowing (masks 4092 and 0xFFFFFFFC), agx-tv the bfi for (x << 2) & 4092 and (x << 9) & 0x7FFFFE00; the functional model gives the E159 llc's silu (and numpy's) for 256 and 2048 threads. lit masked-index. | adopted | -- |

| L15-E174 | **Adopted: narrowing of a 64-bit induction whose signed range fits 32 bits** (AGXNarrowInductions narrow(), `-agx-signed-narrowing`): LSR counts rowcopy_vec4's loop from -4 ({-4, +, 4} to 508), outside narrow()'s [0, 2^31), so it stayed 64-bit: a 64-bit add and a 64-bit compare (three icmpsels) each iteration. Where scalar evolution's signed ranges of the phi, its increment and the step fit 32 bits, the phi is 32-bit with sign-extended uses (the add nsw only), and a compare of a sign-extended value with a constant C is of the 32-bit value (C in [0, 2^31) for unsigned predicates: a negative value is above C either way; signed ones with C in 32 bits). Where s / t is 4, 8 or 16 (32-bit elements, the index shift 0 to 2) and scalar evolution keeps j in [0, 2^31), p = p0 + (s / t)(j - j0): the accesses take p0 - (s / t) j0 and zext(j) scaled as their index (device_load ..., r5, unsigned, lsl 1), and p and its add go. The functional model gives the previous llc's sums bit for bit, and the correctly rounded fma chain's exactly. lit pointer-from-counter. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E176 | **Adopted: a narrowed induction's compare with a 64-bit loop bound known to fit 32 bits, in 32 bits** (AGXNarrowInductions narrow(), `-agx-narrow-bound-compares`): after narrowing, the 32-bit counter's zero extension is compared with an invariant 64-bit bound such as umin(n - i, 65536) or a zero-extended size, computed before the loop, which instruction selection sees only as a register: a 64-bit compare of three icmpsels each iteration. Where known bits (ValueTracking, across blocks) show the bound's high word zero, both are in [0, 2^32), where the signed and unsigned orders agree: the compare is of the 32-bit counter with the bound's low word, unsigned. New MLIR bench kernel stride_loop (a grid-stride loop to a 32-bit n): loop 10 -> 8 instructions, 9 -> 6 GPRs, 518.0 -> 507.0 cycles, tput 22.4 -> 18.7 (-16.5%); gemm_smem_tile 91 -> 87 instructions, 38 -> 35 GPRs, 5495.7 -> 5457.8 cycles, tput 184.5 -> 180.0; softmax_rows 144 -> 145 instructions, 3121.3 -> 3122.4 cycles, tput 225.9 -> 225.7; agx-bench unchanged. | L15-E177 | **Adopted: nuw on the narrowed i32 additions of a memref index of ids and constants** (narrowMemRefIndices in convert-gpu-to-air, nonNegativeTree / flagNarrowTree): narrow-indices computes an index of a static dimension of at most 2^32 in i32, but without flags, so LLVM keeps zext(i + c) whole and the backend adds each constant into its own index register. Where the index is a sum of products of values that are not negative (GPU ids and sizes, constants of at most 32 bits, index_castui of i32), each product of a constant of at least 1, and a static bound of the tree is below 2^64 (no partial sum or product wraps in index), each partial sum is at most the in-bounds index, below 2^32: its i32 additions get nuw. LLVM then takes zext(i + c) as zext(i) + c and the backend merges x[i], x[i + 1], x[i + 2] into one vector load. New MLIR bench kernel conv1d: 13 -> 8 instructions, 216.1 -> 111.8 cycles (-48.3%), tput 5.3 -> 3.4 (-35.8%); every other MLIR bench and newk kernel the same (cycles, instructions, GPRs, resident groups and tput); agx-bench does not go through it. The functional model gives the old mlir-opt's outputs bit for bit for conv1d (three random inputs, one with NaNs and denormals; and the fma reference exactly). Rejected on the way (all measured against the E176 mlir-opt): nuw on flatten's (linearize-indices) additions and stride multiplications too: conv3x3 (new kernel, 258-wide rows) -35.4% cycles, tput 18.0 -> 16.4; stencil5_2d -2.2% cycles but tput 9.3 -> 9.5; layernorm_rows -6.4% cycles but 19 -> 22 GPRs, 80 -> 72 resident groups, tput 56.7 -> 60.6; transpose_smem +4.3% (a power-of-two stride multiplication with nuw becomes shl nuw, through which LLVM moves the zext: 64-bit address arithmetic); without the stride multiplications' nuw, or with it only for strides not a power of two, the layernorm_rows and stencil5_2d throughput losses stay. nuw on lb + k of narrow-loops' counter k of a loop with a bound not constant: stride_loop +3.1%, grid_stride +7.7% (LLVM widens lb + k to 64 bits in the loop); excluded. lit gpu-to-air.mlir (@nuw). | adopted | -- |

Small either way. Also checked: shifts 5..7 of a register second source (the field is 3 bits): the hardware recordings iadd / isub r14, r6, r7, lsl 5..7 match the model, which drops the second source for them, so the backend's limit of 4 is right. | not adopted | -- |
| L15-E179 | **Adopted: (1) a 64-bit device address index whose high word is known zero as the unsigned index register of its low word; (2) byte loads of only the bytes used** (SelectDeviceAddr, `-agx-known-high-zero-indices`; AGXCodeGenPrepare loadBytesApart). (1) The address selection took only zext / sext of an i32 register as an index; an index the DAG built otherwise, such as a byte's i64 zero-extending load (and(load i32, 255) zero-extended) or x & 0xffffffff, became a 64-bit address: a copy of a uniform base to registers, a zero high word and a 64-bit add. Where known bits show the high word zero the index is the low word (sub0), unsigned (the hardware's unsigned index is zero-extended, as for zext). Loads, stores and (E169) atomics. (2) loadBytesApart made a word whose bytes are taken apart a <4 x i8> load even when only byte 0 (or bytes 0 and 1) is used, the other halves taking registers: now 1, 2 or 4 bytes from the word's address (one byte as an i8 load; the address and its folding unchanged). The functional model gives the E176 llc's outputs bit for bit, and NumPy's, for hist (random words, all -1, four buckets: np.bincount), a byte-0 kernel (the byte and byte 0 * byte 1) and x & 0xffffffff indices (x = 5 * 2^32 + 100, the high word all ones, 0x1234 * 2^32, 2^63). lit known-high-zero-index, byte-loads (byte0, bytes01). | adopted | -- |

| L15-E181 | **Adopted: -0.0, not +0.0, as the start of a vector.reduction <add> of floats without an accumulator** (VectorToLLVM, ReductionNeutralFPNegZero): the ordered llvm.vector.reduce.fadd started at +0.0, which is not the identity of a floating-point addition (-0.0 + +0.0 is +0.0: a sum of negative zeros came out +0.0), so the LLVM optimizer kept it: one fadd32 of 0.0 per reduction. -0.0 is the identity (-0.0 + x is x), which the optimizer removes. Unmasked reductions only: a masked one with every lane off is its start value, the empty sum, left +0.0. New MLIR bench kernel dot_vec4: 34 -> 33 instructions, 23895.0 -> 23893.0 cycles, tput 2296.6 -> 2303.0 (+0.3%: the 4-group interleaving, from one instruction fewer after the loop); every other MLIR bench and newk kernel the same; agx-bench does not go through it. The functional model gives the E177 mlir-opt's outputs bit for bit for dot_vec4 (random rows; and one of NaNs, one of -0.0) and the fma reference exactly. lit vector-reduction-to-llvm, vector-to-llvm-interface (the start constants); mlir/test Conversion, Dialect/Vector, Dialect/SparseTensor and Target/LLVMIR pass. | adopted | -- |

Now, for |x| < 1/2, Kahan's (u - 1) x / log(u), u = exp(x), and Goldberg's log(u) x / (u - 1), u = 1 + x (x where u is 1); from 1/2 u - 1 and log(u), which do not cancel there and whose products would overflow (a first version gave expm1(88) and log1p(3e38) infinite); f16 and bf16 in f32. Also measured, no change: half exp, exp2, log, log2, log10, rsqrt, atan, tanh at most 1 ulp over every input; half sin / cos up to 6 / 5 ulp near their zeros at large arguments (1120 / 5 inputs over 1 ulp: the hardware's sine). lit gpu-to-air.mlir (@accurate_m1). | adopted | -- |

Not upstream's expansions log(x + sqrt(x^2 +- 1)) and log((1 + x) / (1 - x)) / 2, which cancel for small x and overflow for large: asinh = log1p(a + a^2 / (1 + sqrt(1 + a^2))), from 2^12 log(a) + ln 2; acosh = log1p(t + sqrt(2 t + t^2)), t = x - 1, from 2^12 log(x) + ln 2, NaN below 1 (a first version gave -inf for large negative x); atanh = log1p(2 a / (1 - a)) / 2; cbrt = exp2(log2(a) / 3) and a Newton step (a first version gave NaN for a denormal: now a signed zero, the flushing mode's); a = |x|, the sign copied, through E204's log1p. The functional model: f32 at most 3 ulp over the 65536-value sample (E204's), the specials (+-0, +-inf, NaN, +-1, 1 + 2^-23, 4096 and below, 3e38) as NumPy / math.erf, denormal inputs flushed; f16 at most 1 ulp and bfloat correctly rounded over every input for all five (erf of f16 on the f16 polynomial was up to 3 ulp, 1524 inputs over 1: widened first). lit gpu-to-air.mlir (@inverse_hyperbolic). | adopted | -- |
| L15-E206 | **Adopted: a loop's exit on its index rather than on a down counter only the exit test uses** (mergeDownCounter in AGXNarrowInductions, `-agx-merge-down-counters`): LSR counts a loop down to zero beside the index it steps (c = n .. 1, exit when c - 1 is 0; i = i0 + s k), an isub each iteration. For s odd, c - 1 = 0 is i' = i0 + s n exactly mod 2^32 (s invertible, both at the same iteration); s = 2^m odd also where SCEV bounds the iterations below 2^(32 - m). The test on i' (the end computed before the loop), and c goes. agx-bench cross_entropy: both row loops one instruction shorter (the max loop 6 -> 5), tput 853.6 -> 821.6 (-3.7%), cycles 13726.2 -> 13728.2 (+0.01%, the one-group schedule); conv3x3 47406.0 -> 47401.4 cycles, tput 2530.1 -> 2518.6; everything else in agx-bench, the MLIR bench and newk unchanged. The functional model gives the E203 llc's outputs bit for bit for cross_entropy (n = 64, 7, 1, 0) and conv3x3 (64 x 56 x 56, two output channels; and a float64 convolution within 7e-5). lit merge-down-counter (an even step without a bound keeps the counter). Tried and dropped: counters stepped by -t, t > 1, through SCEV's exit count (i' = i0 + s (count + 1)): no kernel changed (attn_decode's -8 counter runs beside an i64 threadgroup address, index_add's -4 beside i64 device addresses, whose 64-bit equality would cost three icmpsels). | adopted | -- |
| L15-E207 | **Adopted: an ordered 64-bit comparison with a constant whose low half decides nothing, on the high halves alone** (lowerSETCC64, `-agx-high-half-compares`): x < c or x >= c with c's low half 0, x > c or x <= c with it all ones (signed or unsigned; the sign tests x < 0 and x > -1 among them) fix the low comparison to what the condition gives for equal values, so the result is the high comparison: one icmpsel instead of three (the high comparison, the low one and the select on equal high halves, two after folding). agx-bench cuda_ddot (soft-f64): 4154 -> 4093 instructions, 63 -> 62 GPRs, cycles 12457.1 -> 12259.6 (-1.6%), tput 2579.3 -> 2560.0; cuda_permute4d (64-bit division by 32-bit values): cycles 1587.2 -> 1552.0 (-2.2%), tput 297.0 -> 295.8; everything else in agx-bench, the MLIR bench and newk unchanged. agx-tv proves the six forms of setcc64-high-half.ll and the sign tests x <= -1, x < 0. The functional model gives the old llc's outputs bit for bit (and NumPy's) for cuda_ddot (n = 5000, 1023, 1, 0; doubles of exponents over the whole range) and cuda_permute4d (three shapes, random permutations of the strides). E208's fmod loop: 10 -> 8 instructions. lit setcc64-high-half, add64-ext16 (a sign test: 2 icmpsels fewer). | adopted | -- |

| L15-E209 | **No effect or not sure-fire (after E207)**: (1) a 64-bit comparison whose high halves are known equal (computeKnownBits) as the low halves' unsigned comparison: no agx-bench, MLIR bench or newk kernel changes (the DAG combiner narrows those first). (2) or with a run of up to 8 ones above bit 7 (x \| 0x10000) and and with a hole of zeros (x & ~0x7ff00000) as one bfi instead of a mov_imm and the and / or: bfi issues on the shared integer-multiply / bit-field unit (cost.rs imadbf), and in loops the mov_imm is hoisted, so the trade is one fast-ALU instruction against one of the shared unit and a register: not sure-fire. (3) signed comparisons of bytes as unsigned ones of x ^ 0x80 (a_maxsi_i8: 12 against i16's 7 instructions; the sign extension is a shift and an asr, both on the shared unit): no bench kernel compares signed bytes (int8_dp4a_gemv's extensions are single asr / isub forms already). (4) E199 sweep outliers that are semantics: bf16 rsqrt (45 instructions against f32's 27) is MathToLLVM's 1 / sqrt(x), rounded to bfloat twice, which the intermediate rounding must keep; f32 exp2 / log2 / sin / cos are the accurate expansions (AGXUsage.rst). Also looked at, tight: nbody's uniform inner loop (while_icmp and jmp_exec_any, no cheaper uniform form), loop-invariant constants (3 mov_imm in loops over all of agx-bench, none hoistable). | -- | -- |

Still failing: the f64 transcendental functions (exp, tan, asin, acos, atan2, sinh, cosh, powf, erfc of f64: no soft-f64 versions). No bench kernel uses these. lit emulate-f64-fpclass-powi, soft-f64-powi, gpu-to-air.mlir (@erfc_isinf_ipowi). | adopted | -- |

Now ieee-fp32's lowering in every mode: in float where exact (TwoSum, the sum rounded to odd, then to nearest bfloat), the exact software fma behind an unlikely branch otherwise (denormal operands, tiny or huge products, infinities, NaNs; it keeps denormal results). llvm.fmuladd of bfloat (which may round the product) is the bfloat multiplication and addition, each rounded once (the product exact in float). | L15-E220 | **Adopted: rarely entered if-bodies out of line** (AGXColdRegions, after AGXInsertWaits, `-agx-move-cold-regions`, `-agx-cold-regions-percent`=1): an if-body entered with an edge of at most 1% (the unlikely weights of withSlowPath's general code in software double precision, of the strict float and bfloat fma lowerings, of a Newton 64-bit division's long case) was skipped by a jmp_exec_none taken nearly always, which agx_cost charges like a taken loop branch (about 34 cycles; one not taken 1): in ddot's loop the fma's skip was 542 of 12198 cycles, 8.1% of the kernel with the reduction's adds. Such a body, entered from its if only and left to its join only, moves to the end of the function: the if enters it with jmp_exec_any (not taken in the common case) and it returns with a jmp_exec_any / jmp_exec_none pair to the join (unconditional whatever the mask). Every path runs the same instructions in the same order, so the waits stay right. agx-bench cuda_ddot: cycles 12259.6 -> 11462.1 (-6.5%), tput 2560.0 -> 2554.9, 31730 -> 31910 bytes; nothing else in agx-bench, the MLIR bench or newk changes. The functional model gives the old compiler's outputs bit for bit (ddot with denormal inputs, whose fma runs the moved code; permute4d; strict float add / mul / fma / div on 4096 denormal-heavy values, 8 regions moved) and the references of E208 / E210 / E211 (frem, powi, bfloat fma). agx-tv gives no verdict: it times out (500 to 600 s) on a small kernel with an unlikely store, on one whose rare body is seven ALU instructions before an unconditional store, and on a double multiply, whether the body is moved or not (it does not decide kernels with execution-mask branches at that size); the functional model gives the reference for that ALU kernel, moved, over 40960 threads (40 argument pairs, a third with groups split between the two sides). Not with ieee-fp32 (follow-up commit): its exact paths are marked unlikely but taken for zero operands (an addition of two values below 2^-101), common in real data and in agx_cost's traces, whose skipped loads give zeros; strict agx-bench with the pass against without was mixed (mandelbrot cycles -62.5%, gelu_f16 -37.1%, softmax_online tput -15.8%; philox cycles +36.1%, cross_entropy +20.0%, sgemm_reg4x4 tput +5.3%, layernorm_f16 +5.4%). Tried and dropped: unlikely weights on every 64-bit division's long case (not only Newton's): the bodies moved in permute4d are never reached in its trace (its taken skips are 50 / 50 if / else diamonds: 26% of its cycles, which nothing marks), +144 bytes for no cycle. lit cold-regions, ieee-fp32-flag. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E221 | **Adopted: the outermost "operands fit in 32 bits" test of a 64-bit division marked likely** (AGXCodeGenPrepare, `-agx-div64-fits-likely`): the PTX front end divides 64-bit values as (a \| b) < 2^32 ? a 32-bit division : the 64-bit one (which AGX bypasses again inside), with no branch weights, so E220 left the long case in line and each common-case pass skipped it with a taken jump: permute4d's 12 skips (three divisions, four unrolled steps) were 26% of its cycles. The outermost such test ((a \| b) < 2^32, or the bypass's (a \| b) & ~0xffffffff == 0) on a 64-bit division's chain of single predecessors is marked likely to fit; not an inner one (under an outer test that failed, the inner fast case is not the common one: E220's attempt that marked the inner bypass moved code never reached). agx-bench cuda_permute4d: cycles 1552.0 -> 1264.9 (-18.5%), tput 295.8 -> 291.0, 13062 -> 13206 bytes; nothing else in agx-bench, the MLIR bench or newk. Where the operands rarely fit, the cost is one more taken jump beside a 64-bit division of at least ~100 cycles. The functional model gives the old outputs bit for bit for permute4d and exact quotients and remainders for 2048 pairs of a front-end-style kernel with lanes on both sides (some fitting, some 62-bit, mixed groups). lit div64-fits-likely. | adopted | -- |

On the words: moved by 64 places where n >= 64 (one icmpsel compare-and-select each), by 32 where bit 5 is set, then each result word a funnel shift of two neighbours by n mod 32 (one extr), the edge word shifted (asr for ashr, with the sign as the fill): 20.
