| L15-E226 | **Adopted: a binary search's scf.while in i32** (convert-gpu-to-air narrow-loops, narrowBinarySearch; E195's lead): an scf.while over index (lo = 0, hi = n) continuing while lo < hi (unsigned), whose body loads a static 1-D memref of at most 2^30 elements at mid = (lo + hi) >> 1 before anything that could leave it, and yields only lo, hi, mid, mid + 1 or selects of them, keeps its values below 2^31 and its sums below 2^32: each yield is at most max(lo, hi, S), and the first iteration's mid = hi0 >> 1 is in bounds (or the load is undefined), so hi0 < 2 S; a hi0 of 2^32 or more makes that load out of bounds, or, truncated to 0, ends the i32 loop at once, which is then as good. So lo and hi are i32, mid indexes through index_castui. Not with a signed test (for a hi0 negative as an index the original ends at once returning hi0, not its truncation), nor a lo not starting at 0 (lo + hi may wrap). MLIR bench bsearch_while: 34 -> 22 instructions, 12 -> 8 GPRs, cycles 1757.0 -> 1508.6 (-14.1%), tput 136.5 -> 49.4 (-63.8%); every other MLIR bench and newk kernel unchanged. The functional model gives the old outputs and NumPy's searchsorted for n = 4096, 1, 0, 7, 1000, 65537 (1024 keys each, inside and outside the array). lit gpu-to-air.mlir (@binary_search: narrowed; a lo from an argument and a signed test not). | adopted | -- |
| L15-E227 | **Added: math.exp and math.log of f64** (convert-gpu-to-air rewriteExpF64 / rewriteLogF64): E210's open gap (the double transcendental functions did not compile: "no emulation of llvm.exp.f64"). Both are fdlibm's algorithms in double and integer operations, which the AIR emulation of doubles computes: exp (e_exp.c) by k = roundeven(x / ln 2), ln 2 split so that k ln2hi is exact, the degree-5 polynomial and rational correction, 2^k as two powers of two so a denormal result rounds once, x clamped to [-746, 710] by compares that keep a NaN; log (e_log.c) by x = 2^k (1 + f) with 1 + f in [sqrt(2)/2, sqrt(2)) (denormals scaled by 2^54), s = f / (2 + f) and the degree-7 polynomial, both of fdlibm's final forms computed and selected by its test, the specials last. The functional model: exp within 1 ulp of Python's (glibc) over 7680 values (uniform over [-745, 710], [-1, 1], 1e-6 around 0, denormal and near-overflow results, +-0, +-inf, NaN, the thresholds); log within 1 ulp over 6400 (e^[-700, 700], [0.5, 2], 1 +- 1e-6, all denormal bit patterns' range, 0, -0, -1, inf, NaN, the largest and smallest values). On llc directly too (follow-up commit: AGXSoftF64 expands llvm.exp / llvm.log of a double the same way before its lowering; the same accuracy on the same sets). Still missing: the other double transcendental functions (sin, cos, tan, the inverse and hyperbolic ones, pow, atan2, erfc). No bench kernel uses them. lit gpu-to-air.mlir (@exp_log_f64), soft-f64-exp-log. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E228 | **Added: math.expm1, log1p, exp2, log2, log10, cosh, sinh and tanh of f64** (convert-gpu-to-air: rewriteAccurateM1 extended to f64, new rewriteF64Function): E227's remaining gap for the exponential family; each is built from E227's f64 exp and log (rewritten in turn), in fdlibm's forms: expm1 / log1p as the f32 ones' exp(x) - 1 and log(1 + x) with the rounding error recovered, now in double; exp2 as exp(r ln 2) 2^k (k = roundeven(x), r ln 2 with its rounding error from an fma); log2 / log10 as k + log(m) / ln 2 and k log10(2) + log(m) log10(e), m in [sqrt(2)/2, sqrt(2)); cosh (e + 1/e) / 2, from 709 exp(\|x\| / 2)^2 / 2; sinh fdlibm's three ranges and exp(\|x\| / 2)^2 from 709; tanh 1 - 2 / (t + 2) from 1, t / (t + 2) below (t = expm1(2\|x\|), whose error passes on scaled by at most 1), a - a^3/3 + 2a^5/15 below 2^-9, 1 from 22. First tries, rejected by the accuracy test: cosh from 709 as exp(\|x\| - ln 2) (the subtraction rounds \|x\|'s last bits away: 494 ulp), tanh below 1 as fdlibm's -t / (t + 2), t = expm1(-2\|x\|) (scales expm1's error by up to 1.8: 3 ulp), and tanh without the series (3 ulp below 1e-6); and sinh from 22 as (t + 1) / 2 in place of exp(\|x\|) (no gain: expm1's expansion already computes exp(\|x\|), and CSE shared it). The functional model against Python (glibc), max ulp: expm1 2 (10240 values), log1p 2 (6400), exp2 1 (3328, with denormal and overflowing results), log2 1, log10 2 (2560 each, with all denormal inputs' range), cosh 2 (2560, up to 712), sinh 2 (3328), tanh 2 (4864), each with +-0, +-inf, NaN and the thresholds. agx_cost per G14C kernel of one call (256 threads): expm1 10721 cycles, log1p 6740, exp2 5933, log2 6016, log10 6447, cosh 9148, sinh 17051, tanh 12752 (exp 4222, log 5404). No bench kernel uses them (agx-bench, the MLIR bench and newk unchanged). Still missing: the f64 sin, cos, tan, the inverse functions, pow, atan2, erfc; and these on llc directly. lit gpu-to-air.mlir (@more_f64_functions). | adopted | -- |
| L15-E229 | **Adopted: the threadgroup's special registers bounded by agx-max-threads-per-threadgroup** (AGXSpecialRegisterRanges, `-agx-threadgroup-ranges`): MLIR's known_block_size reaches the AGX backend only as "agx-max-threads-per-threadgroup" (the range the MLIR lowering puts on its air.thread_position_in_threadgroup call is lost when the AIR target makes the call a kernel parameter), and the ranges pass gave the threadgroup registers the hardware's bounds (1024 threads, 32 SIMD-groups) whatever it said. The functional model gives the old compiler's outputs bit for bit for gemm_simd_matrix (K = 64, 16; 4 x 4 threadgroups), within 7.5e-6 of a float64 product. lit threadgroup-ranges. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E230 | **Rejected (no gain): device accesses at B[ext(X + C_k)] sharing one base B[ext(X)] with the C_k as immediate indices** (AGXSinkAddresses prototype, `-agx-shared-index-bases`; E188's lead 5): where at least two distinct C_k != 0 in a block and each X + C_k proven not to wrap (nuw / nsw, or ValueTracking's overflow test with the special register ranges), one 64-bit addition for the base replaces an addition (and, for C_k above 255, a mov_imm) per access. A cost test (indices removed against 3 instructions a uniform base) would apply it nowhere. stencil5_2d, the natural case (X + 1, X + 1026, X + 1028, X + 2053), shows these offsets only after the DAG reassociates the IR's sums, and its thread_position_in_grid is unbounded (no known grid), so no X + C_k there is provably free of wrapping. | L15-E231 | **Adopted: what narrowing a loop's inductions left dead deleted before the rewrites that count uses** (AGXNarrowInductions, `-agx-dead-after-narrowing`): MLIR's naive matmul after LSR has a 64-bit down counter (umin(n, 2^20)) beside its two 32-bit indices; narrow() makes it 32 bits but leaves the sign extensions its 64-bit uses had, dead, in the loop, and E206's mergeDownCounter (one use of the counter, only the exit test using its decrement) then saw two uses and kept it. | L15-E232 | **Rejected (mixed): E206 for an up counter, a loop's exit on its index rather than on a counter stepped alike that only the exit test uses** (AGXNarrowInductions prototype, `-agx-merge-up-counters`): a counter c stepped by s and tested against an invariant bound b (c' < b) beside an index i stepped by the same s has i' = c' + d (d = i0 - c0, mod 2^32), so the test can be i' < b + d with b + d computed before the loop, where x + d wraps for no c' or b (their SCEV ranges, and d's: from the branch into the loop where SCEV has none, as a grid-stride loop is entered only with its start below its end, zext(tid) < sext(n), tid then below 2^31; SCEV's loop guards and isLoopEntryGuardedByCond did not find that bound). Nothing else in agx-bench, the MLIR bench or newk changes. Reverted. | rejected | -- |

| L15-E234 | **Adopted: E187's device-load loops unrolled 16 times where all their device loads are of one or two bytes** (AGX TTI getUnrollingPreferences, `-agx-device-unroll-narrow`; found running opt's -O3 twice, which unrolls such loops 4 x 4 and helped exactly these kernels while it hurt the f32 ones, as E187's factor 8 did): a half or int8 row's iteration is a 2-byte load and an operation or two, so 4 iterations' loads in flight leave the loop waiting. 16 where it divides the constant trip count, else E187's 4; a loop with any wider device load keeps 4. Factor 8: all three better than 4 but less (tput 441.2, 1366.8, 339.6; dot_i8 cycles 20366.7, better than 16's); 32: rowsum_f16 cycles 12159.8 and gemv_f16 11085.1 (worse than 16), gemv_f16 36 GPRs and 46 groups. | L15-E235 | **Adopted: E187's device-load loops unrolled 16 times where they also store to device memory, 8 where their loads all step more than 64 bytes an iteration** (AGX TTI deviceUnrollFactor, `-agx-device-unroll-store`, `-agx-device-unroll-strided`; the other kernels E234's twice-run -O3 improved on both cycles and tput): a copy's iteration is a load and a store, a column's loads each a cache line of their own (the stride read from the 32-bit index under the GEP's extension where the address is no recurrence, as at MLIR's linearized indices). | L15-E236 | **Adopted: innermost loops versioned on their buffers' 4-byte alignment** (new AGXAlignmentVersioning, before the load / store vectorizer, `-agx-alignment-versioning`; E188's lead 1 without the simulator's follow-up 15): a half buffer is known aligned to its element only (Metal asks a binding's offset to be aligned to the data the function reads; MLIR's memrefs promise no more), so a half row loads two bytes at a time and a vector<4xf16> as four 16-bit loads, wide loads being recorded at 4-byte alignment only (B33). A single-block innermost loop (simplified, one exit) with at least 4 simple device accesses of 16 bits or more whose alignment a 4-byte aligned kernel argument base would raise (SCEV: the offset from the base's trailing zeros) becomes `if ((base & 3) == 0 for each such base) { the loop, those accesses at min(4, 2^tz) } else { the loop }`, the loop cloned with its preheader, the exit's LCSSA phis given the clone's values; the test is of kernel arguments, so no thread diverges. The vectorizer merges the aligned copy's accesses. Upper bound first measured by marking every device access align 4 (unsound in general): also h2, add_f16x2, vred16, silu_f16x4 (straight-line code: not versioned). MLIR bench and newk, cycles / tput (agx_cost's buffers are aligned, the copy it runs): dot_f16x4 28216.0 -> 10026.0 / 1163.6 -> 858.5 (-26.2%), 28 -> 32 GPRs, 55 -> 52 groups; gemv_f16 7425.9 -> 3228.8 / 295.1 -> 177.0 (-40.0%), 28 -> 32 GPRs, 52 groups; rowcopy_f16 17563.6 -> 11746.5 / 499.3 -> 273.5 (-45.2%); rowsum_f16 8825.4 -> 6357.9 / 377.6 -> 271.2 (-28.2%); every other kernel unchanged, agx-bench too. Bytes left out: dot_i8 15305.1 cycles (from 25998.7) but tput 1294.1 -> 1377.5 (+6.4%), 29 -> 33 GPRs and 50 groups, as a 4-byte load leaves each byte in a 16-bit half that the sign extension (xor / isub on 32 bits) first copies out (doing it on the halves would need imadd's 16-bit sign-extended sources, unrecorded). A misaligned buffer runs the original loop after the test (and, or, a compare: 3 or 4 instructions). The functional model gives the old outputs bit for bit for rowsum_f16, gemv_f16, dot_f16x4 and rowcopy_f16 with every half buffer at offset 0, 2 or 6 bytes (both copies; the run harness now maps buffers at an offset), and NumPy's within 4.5e-5 (rowcopy_f16 exactly). Also: the option named like the pass made opt abort at start again (E220's mistake); the pass is agx-align-versioning. lit alignment-versioning. | adopted | -- |

| L15-E239 | **Adopted: a region of few device loads kept in its original order where its schedule would cut the SIMD-groups resident on a core 1.8 times or more** (AGXScheduleDAGMILive, `-agx-sched-core-occupancy`): AGXSchedKeepOccupancy reverts a region whose schedule lowers the threads a threadgroup may have (maxThreadsForHalves: nothing below 52 GPRs), but the SIMD-groups resident on a core (AGX Performance Simulator dispatch: min(96, 2 floor(3328 / (2 halves))), which agx_cost's tput and agx-bench's groups use) fall from 17 GPRs. A tiled matrix product's inner loop (threadgroup loads, then fmas) the generic scheduler reorders all loads first: 28 -> 60 halves. The rule, per region: fewer than 4 device loads (the unrolled device-load loops of E187 / E234 / E235 lose without their clusters) and the cap at the new pressure (rounded to the allocation's 8 halves) below the old one by 1.8 times. agx-bench matrixMul (CUDA sample): 41 -> 21 GPRs, cycles 5480.6 -> 5262.3 (-4.0%), tput 189.4 -> 117.1 (-38.2%), 572 -> 566 bytes (G13G the same); MLIR bench gemm_smem_tile: 35 -> 19 GPRs, 40 -> 80 groups, cycles 5457.8 -> 5256.3 (-3.7%), tput 180.0 -> 112.5 (-37.5%); every other kernel unchanged. Tried first: the per-core cap for every region (colsum, dot_f16x4, dot_i8, dot_vec4, gemv_f16 much worse: their clustered loads); for regions of fewer than 4 device loads at any cut (agx-bench nbody tput -16% but cycles +19%, sgemm_reg4x4 -4% / +10%, gelu_f16 -4% / +17%, swiglu -4% / +13%: mixed), at 1.5 times (nbody still in), at 2 times (gemm_smem_tile out: its old cap is the 96 maximum); and shorter clusters of device loads (8, 4, 2) before reverting: nested2d cycles 78512.9 -> 49943.3 but 14 -> 42 GPRs, tput 1791.6 -> 2411.6. A second schedule() of a region needs the strategy's initPolicy again (GenericScheduler's bottom index). The functional model gives the old outputs bit for bit for matrixMul (64 x 64 x 64, 32 x 48 x 80) and gemm_smem_tile (K = 64, 32), within 1.1e-5 of a float64 product. Baseline updated. lit sched-core-occupancy. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E240 | **Adopted: E236 for the PTX front end's addresses** (AGXAlignmentVersioning: baseAndOffset, argumentTerm): its kernels take i64 arguments and compute addresses as integers (inttoptr (base + offset), base ptrtoint (addrspacecast (inttoptr %arg))), so SCEV's pointer base of each access was the inttoptr, no argument. Now the base may be a kernel argument or casts of one (the same for every thread), found as a term (or a ptrtoint term) of the integer sum, through recurrence starts; the test is of that value, which must dominate the preheader. And a loop whose exit block the path around it shares gets a dedicated exit first (formDedicatedExitBlocks). agx-bench attn_decode (half K and V rows, 4 halves a lane each): cycles 29591.6 -> 27052.9 (-8.6%), tput 2535.1 -> 2368.1 (-6.6%), but 33 -> 37 GPRs, 2254 -> 2678 bytes (both loops) and 4 -> 6 waits (G13G the same); every other agx-bench, MLIR bench and newk kernel as after E236. The functional model gives the old outputs bit for bit for attn_decode (4 heads, seq 512 and 37; q, K and V each at offset 0, 2 or 6 bytes: both copies), within 2e-7 of a float64 attention. Baseline updated. lit alignment-versioning (@ptx). | adopted | -- |
| L15-E241 | **Adopted: a SIMD-group's inclusive integer add scan by shuffles as the hardware prefix** (AGXCodeGenPrepare simdScans, `-agx-simd-scans`): the Hillis-Steele scan x' = x + (lane >= d ? shuffle_up(x, d) : 0) for d = 1, 2, 4, 8, 16 (CUDA's __shfl_up_sync scan, CUB's warp scan; MLIR's gpu.shuffle up with a select on its validity) is simd_prefix_iadd (exclusive, every generation has it) of x0 plus x0: 2 instructions for 15. i32 add only (wrapping addition gives the same sum in any order; a float scan would round differently). The selects may test lane > d - 1, lane >= d, lane != 0 (d = 1), or the inverse with the arms swapped; a chain that stops before 16 (a scan of segments) or tests other lanes stays. A shuffle from an inactive lane is undefined (Metal, CUDA, MLIR), so where some lanes are inactive the prefix of the active ones is one of the original's results; with a partial SIMD-group (48-thread workgroups) the two are the same, as each lane only reads lanes below it. MLIR bench warp_scan: 21 -> 7 instructions, 5 -> 4 GPRs, cycles 154.2 -> 125.7 (-18.5%), tput 8.5 -> 5.9 (-30.6%); every other kernel unchanged (agx-bench's scans call the prefix directly). The functional model gives the old outputs, and NumPy's 32-lane wrapping cumulative sums, for warp_scan (8192 threads, small and full-range i32 values; and 48-thread workgroups). lit simd-scan. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E242 | **Rejected (mixed): E235's strided-load loops unrolled at runtime trip counts** (AGX TTI prototype, `-agx-strided-runtime-unroll`: UP.Runtime with a remainder, for loops whose device loads all step more than 64 bytes and whose trip count is not a constant; E187 left runtime trip counts alone after R12 / R20). The kernel it was meant for, newk colsum_dyn, does not qualify: its stride is the runtime leading dimension, not a constant. The one it changed, softmax_rows (MLIR bench): factor 4 cycles 3122.4 -> 2758.1 but tput 225.7 -> 252.6, 26 -> 36 GPRs; factor 8 2443.8 / 251.2, 45 GPRs. Reverted. Also measured, not pursued: a float max butterfly (shuffle_xor and fmaxf, softmax and attention) as simd_fmax would not be bit for bit the same (the hardware's float SIMD max drops NaNs and may order the zeros either way, and the PTX lowering canonicalizes NaNs and flushes denormals); full-width integer butterflies, which would be exact, occur in no kernel of the three sets. | rejected | -- |
| L15-E243 | **Rejected (worse): a loop's guarded blocks of loads and arithmetic run for every thread, the loads from a known-good address where the guard is false** (AGXCodeGenPrepare prototype, `-agx-speculate-guarded-loads`): conv3x3's nine taps (if inside the image: two loads, a wait, an fma) each wait alone under their own execution mask. The block T of pred: br c, T, M (results only in M's phis, at most 16 instructions) moved into pred, each load from select(c, p, q) with q the address of an access of the same type that runs whenever T would (a load dominating the guard, or a load or store post-dominating T, its address cloned at the guard; a phi of copies of one instruction taken as that instruction: conv3x3's only such access is the output store), and the phis as selects. On conv3x3 every 64-bit address select is two icmpsels and the loads lose their base + index forms, and the scheduler still waits per tap: cycles 47401.4 -> 68085.1, tput 2518.6 -> 3215.9, 39 -> 44 GPRs, 1206 -> 1608 bytes. What would be needed: the guards are loop-invariant, so a select of each tap's invariant offset before the loop (base + selected offset in the loop) rather than of the addresses. Reverted. | rejected | -- |
| L15-E244 | **Adopted: the threadgroup's exact shape from MLIR's known_block_size** (convert-gpu-to-air: "agx-threads-per-threadgroup"="X,Y,Z" beside E229's thread count; AGXSpecialRegisterRanges, `-agx-threadgroup-shape-ranges`): known_block_size promises the launch's shape, but only its product reached the backend, so in a 16 x 16 threadgroup each position was bounded by 256. Now each position is below its extent and each threadgroup size is exactly it. MLIR bench gemm_smem_tile: 87 -> 86 instructions, cycles 5256.3 -> 5255.2, tput 112.5 -> 111.7 (-0.7%); every other MLIR bench and newk kernel unchanged (MLIR already folds extents of 1 and the sizes to constants), agx-bench too (no such attribute). | L15-E245 | **Adopted: a two-block loop exiting from both blocks run as one block with one exit** (AGXCodeGenPrepare mergeLoopExits, `-agx-merge-loop-exits`): H: br e1, XH, T; T: (side-effect-free work); br e2, XT, H (Mandelbrot: the escape test in the header, the iteration count in the latch) was structurized as an if inside the loop with flags for which exit was taken, every iteration. The PTX front end addresses a field as gep i8 (inttoptr (b + x)), c; an access with an index register has no immediate offset, so each such access took a 64-bit addition every iteration (agx_cost: about 4 cycles of a per-core unit with groups sharing a core). With b + c hoisted, the access takes x as its index register; each new base is a register pair live through the loop, hence the bound. embedding (a float4 row copied as two i64 that may overlap): the loop's two 64-bit additions go, cycles 722.9 -> 648.6 (-10.3%), tput 35.0 -> 26.3 (-24.9%), gprs 12 -> 14; conv3x3 (nine taps of the input row at -W-1 ... W+1): cycles 18846.5 -> 17028.5 (-9.6%), tput 1348.3 -> 1276.0 (-5.4%), gprs 55 -> 62, bytes 1760 -> 1818 (both GPUs); nothing else changed in agx-bench, the MLIR bench or newk. Bounds 2 and 4: embedding only; 12 and 16: conv3x3 74 GPRs, tput 1316.0. alive-tv: the rewrite (positive and negative c, loads and stores). Functional model: embedding (3 widths, exact copies) and conv3x3 (two shapes, within 3e-6 of float64) bit-identical to the old compiler; agx-stress ok. lit hoist-address-offsets. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E263 | **Measured: the off-by-default AGX options again, after E252-E262** (as E261 found one that had become a gain). agx-bench, G14C: atomic-optimizer compact tput 77.5 -> 183.0; cas-batch-try-first, unsplit-shuffles, declare-tree-gprs: no change; enable-sink-remat ddot +19.1% cycles; sched-lane-masks attn_decode cycles -0.5% but tput +0.06%, ddot -0.1%: mixed; launch-divisors permute4d cycles -7.7%, tput -6.6%, but 18 more uniform halves the driver must preload (a launch-time contract, not a code change); element-aligned-vectors q4_matvec cycles -20.8% / tput -2.9% / gprs 24 -> 30, q8_matvec gprs 55 -> 26 / tput -5.8% / cycles +26.3%, attn_decode mixed, and it assumes 8- and 16-bit element vectors need only the element's alignment, not measured on hardware (B33 has 32-bit elements at 4 bytes). None adopted. | measured | -- |
| L15-E264 | **Not changed: maximumnum's zero fix and canonicalization** (attn_decode's fmaxf: and, fcmpsel gtn, fmul 1.0, two fcmpsel eq). PTX max.f32 is maximumnum, which orders -0 below +0: the and of equal operands' bits is that order, and the multiplication by 1.0 the flushed canonical value of two equal denormals (agx-tv, E-entries on minimum / maximum). Nor the precise exp in MLIR softmax (about 20 instructions): the hardware exp2 is tens of ulp off; x - max <= 0 would drop the upper clamp and overflow tests, but the maximum is of values loaded again in a later loop, which nothing proves unchanged. | measured | -- |
| L15-E265 | **Adopted: narrow an induction its latch's unsigned exit test bounds** (AGXNarrowInductions narrow, `-agx-latch-bound-narrowing`). MLIR's grid-stride loop over a 2^20-element memref keeps an i64 counter {0, +, umin(grid * 256, 2^20)} for its exit test against umin(n - i, 2^20) (the index's 32-bit counter is separate). Scalar evolution's range of the counter is its maximum trip count (4095) times the step's maximum, [0, 4293918721): not below 2^31, so the counter stayed 64-bit, a 64-bit addition and a 64-bit compare (three compare-selects) per iteration. Where the loop continues only while inc <u b (or slt with samesign), the counter is at most max(start, b - 1) and the increment (nuw) at most that plus the step: below 2^31 here (2^20 - 1 + 2^20), and E-earlier's narrowing (with its bound compare in 32 bits) applies. MLIR bench grid_stride: 39 -> 33 instructions, gprs 11 -> 9, cycles 541.2 -> 522.1 (-3.5%), tput 30.5 -> 21.0 (-31%); nothing else changed in agx-bench, the MLIR bench or newk. alive-tv: the argument scaled to i16 -> i8 (step umin(s, 16), bound umin(b, 16), every trip count: 17 unrolled iterations); the full function times out (float, memory). Functional model: grid_stride (n = 0, 256, 5000, 70000, 2^20, 3 * 10^6; 1 to 16 threadgroups) bit-identical; agx-stress ok. lit narrow-latch-bound. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E266 | **Not adopted: grid_stride's two counters as one** (after E265 its loop steps the trip counter from 0 and the index from the global id by the same stride: the exit test b' <u x + (b0 - a0) on the index instead, where neither side can wrap). It needs the global id's bound, which MLIR's `gpu.global_id upper_bound 1048576` states but GPUToAIR does not carry (the range goes on the three-component call only when all three are bounded). Tried: an `llvm.assume(id <u ub)` from GPUToAIR, made the special register read's range by AGXSpecialRegisterRanges: opt's passes saw the assumption first and widened the loop's pointers to 64-bit induction variables (two 64-bit additions per iteration, as L14-E1 found for early ranges), and an assume turns the op's poison (upper_bound's semantics) into undefined behaviour; a function attribute from the bound would apply to reads the op does not make (after CSE). The merge alone changes no kernel of the three sets. All reverted. | rejected | -- |
| L15-E267 | **Not done: vector.load / vector.store indices as a loop's offset bound** (loopOffsetBound, which narrows an scf.for to i32 offsets from the memref.load / store indices, would give saxpy_vec4 and scale_f16x4 the clamp grid_stride has, and E265 their counters). The vector dialect's LangRef: "'vector.load' permits out-of-bounds reads. Support and implementation of out-of-bounds vector loads is target-specific" (vector.store likewise): an index past the extent is not undefined, so it bounds nothing. Reverted before measuring. | rejected | -- |
| L15-E268 | **Adopted: E247's guard-free copy as one block, its inttoptrs of one address as one** (AGXAlignmentVersioning versionGuards, `-agx-guard-versioning-merge`). The copy's guarded blocks are a chain once their tests go; merged, the load/store vectorizer (per block) sees the taps together. In conv3x3 it still did not join them: each guarded block had its own inttoptr of the same integer address (the PTX front end's), and the vectorizer takes each inttoptr for a base of its own; folded, the nine weights wp[0..8] (consecutive, 4-byte aligned) are two xyzw loads and one x load, with three bases instead of nine (E262's), 18 loads -> 13 per iteration. agx-bench (both GPUs): conv3x3 bytes 1818 -> 1728, gprs 62 -> 55, cycles 17028.5 -> 16389.1 (-3.8%), tput 1276.0 -> 1222.9 (-4.2%); nothing else changed in agx-bench, the MLIR bench or newk. The merge is a CFG cleanup and the fold of a pure cast; the vectorizer is upstream's. Functional model: conv3x3 (four shapes) and embedding bit-identical; agx-stress ok. lit guard-versioning (its four taps now one xyzw load). The same fold for every block before the vectorizer changed no agx-bench kernel (opt's GVN folds them; only the copy makes new ones): not kept. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E269 | **Measured: llc's numeric AGX options around their defaults, and nested2d's serial loads** (after E268). agx-bench and the MLIR bench: agx-postra-cluster-wait 4 and 16, agx-headroom-gprs 16 and 32, agx-merge-loop-exits 32, agx-fold-condition-chains 3: no change; agx-mem-cluster 32: q8_matvec 55 -> 19 GPRs but cycles 2777.5 -> 6950.9 and tput +1.0%, sgemm_reg4x4 mixed; agx-offset-index-max 2: MLIR conv3x3 tput 14.9 -> 13.8 but cycles 250.7 -> 370.0. newk nested2d (an unrolled row of 16 xyzw loads, each waited for before the next: 78512.9 cycles): device-load clusters of 2, 4 or 8 and the original-order guard off (agx-sched-keep-occupancy=0) cut cycles to 97994.5 / 49943.3 / 24074.5 but take 38 to 74 GPRs, 42 to 22 resident groups, tput 1791.6 -> 2397.4 to 2512.8: the serial order is the throughput one. None adopted. | measured | -- |
| L15-E270 | **Adopted: e^(x - m) without its overflow tests where m is at least x** (AGXMathExpansion expHiLo / ldexp `NoOverflow`; AGXCodeGenPrepare expBelowLoopMax, `-agx-exp-below-loop-max`; the local form, `-agx-exp-max-difference`). The precise exp clamps its argument above at 89 and selects +inf where the scaled bits overflow (2 of its ~22 instructions). A softmax's exponent x - m is at most 0 (or a denormal where the maximum's comparison flushes) or a NaN, where neither ever fires. Proved for: m = maxnum / maximumnum / maximum (x, y) itself; and m an earlier loop's running maximum (every iteration, of a load) whose address expression is x's load's but for its loop's recurrences in place of x's loop's (the same operands: iteration k of either reads one address), x's loop running at most as many iterations (backedge-taken counts equal or ule), nothing between the two loops or in them that writes memory or has side effects (a barrier), through a SIMD-group fmax reduction of it (at least the lane's own value) and an LCSSA phi whose other value comes only where the first loop did not run, the two loops having one guard condition. Measured by hand first (xent's loop without the two: tput -11.2%). agx-bench (both GPUs): cross_entropy bytes 814 -> 780, gprs 24 -> 21, cycles 13728.2 -> 13436.9 (-2.1%), tput 821.6 -> 728.5 (-11.3%); MLIR bench softmax_rows (its second pass; the third stores into the row it reads, in place) 145 -> 140 instructions, gprs 26 -> 23, cycles 3122.4 -> 3083.1, tput 225.7 -> 215.6 (-4.5%); nothing else changed, newk unchanged. The expansion with and without the flag on exp(a - maxnum(a, b)): bit-identical over 10,485,760 inputs (special values pairwise, scaled normals, random bit patterns). Functional model: cross_entropy (n = 64, 7, 1, 33 with +-inf, NaN, denormals; values up to 80 sigma) and softmax_rows (normal, x60, +-inf / NaN / 3e38 / a row of -inf) bit-identical; agx-stress ok. lit exp-below-max. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E271 | **Adopted: E270's local form through a SIMD-group (or quad) fmax of x itself** (agx-bench softmax_row: x - simd_reduce(x, fmax); the reduction is at least this lane's x, NaNs dropped). softmax_row bytes 392 -> 364, cycles 244.1 -> 240.5 (-1.5%), tput 23.5 -> 21.6 (-8.1%), both GPUs; nothing else changed. Functional model: softmax_row (normal, x100, +-inf / NaN / a SIMD-group of -inf / denormals, random bit patterns) bit-identical. lit exp-below-max (@simd). | adopted | -- |

Also checked in this round with no change to make: erf's 82 instructions are a coefficient-selected rational with one division (31 mov_imm of its constants: the uniform-constant route needs the launcher), the expansions' asin / acos / atan already take one polynomial or division of the selected argument, expm1's exp and log are both read on the same lanes, and sin and cos of one argument share their reduction and polynomials through CSE (the |x| >= 2^22 hardware fallbacks are different sequences). | adopted | -- |

| L15-E335 | **Adopted: bfloat sqrt as the float x rsqrt_special(x) rounded to bfloat** (AGXCodeGenPrepare bfloatSqrts, option -agx-bfloat-sqrt-product; not under ieee-fp32): a bfloat square root was promoted to the correctly rounded float one (remainder correction, scaling below 2^-80) and rounded; the float product alone, rounded to bfloat, is the same for every bfloat on agx_run (all 65536). newk: bf16 sqrt tput 9.3 -> 4.9 (26 -> 12 instructions). agx-bench, agx-stress unchanged. Test: bfloat-sqrt.ll. | adopted | -- |
| L15-E336 | **Rejected: half division without the remainder correction** (lowerFDIV's correctly rounded half path, q = a y with y the refined float reciprocal, 26 -> 24 instructions): the exhaustive sweep of all 2^32 half pairs on agx_run found 5980 different quotients, all subnormal half results (e.g. 0x5b / 0x4b00: 0x6, now 0x7), where a y's float error decides the rounding to half's coarse subnormal steps. The correction stays. Also sampled (not adopted): bfloat division as a rcp(b) in float (48 -> 29 instructions with a scaling for |b| > 2^125): quotients exactly at the flush threshold 2^-126 (0.50390625 / (1.0078125 2^125)) flushed to 0, the reciprocal's error putting the product below it. | rejected | -- |

Rejected per kind: the hardware exp (1 f16 input further off), and erf's division (8 f16 inputs different, 6 further off). f32 erfc unchanged. newk: f16 erfc tput 35.3 -> 30.7 (104 -> 91 instructions), bf16 37.9 -> 33.3. MLIR bench unchanged. Test: gpu-to-air.mlir (@half_erfc). | adopted | -- |

Kept: half atanh (105 -> 90 instructions), bfloat tanpi (107 -> 87). The gate now rebuilds opt as well. Test: air/math-half-fast-air.ll. | adopted | -- |

Not adopted. | rejected | -- |

(-0 below +0) with NaN tests on the bits, instead of the library's lt / eq / unord. Simulator, busiest core, 32,768 threads: f64 -> s32 (rni) -> f64 2,394 -> 1,735 (-28%), min.f64 1,347 -> 1,127 (-16%). Rejected alongside: also recovering `__air_f64_{floor,roundeven,unord,eq,lt}` as double operations (+-1%) | adopted | -- |

Revisit with an AGX LSR cost (isLSRCostLess) that weighs registers | -- | -- |

| L2-E1 | **Rejected: InstCombine on the raised AIR** (inside the raise, target-neutral, kernels only). co -0.13% cycles / -0.34% tput, lb -0.11%, but stencil +77%: InstCombine sinks the loads the CUDA source hoists above its bounds check, and its code sinking cannot be switched off from a pass here (a per-context library option). **Measurement pitfall:** `opt -mtriple=agx -passes='agx-raise-to-air,function(instcombine)'` showed ldmatrix -69%: AGX's alias analysis takes AIR's address space 4 (the generic hint) for AGX's constant memory and InstCombine deleted the stores. Never run AGX-target IR passes over AIR. Kept: agx-lower-air also recognizes the `x == 0` form of the .ftz flush that InstCombine makes | no gain worth it | -- |
| X4 | Metal pipeline legalizes the hints: generic -> device, 65-128-bit integers as limbs, RMW loops (778b681ba) | n/a (Metal side) | Metal writer 241 -> 246 of 248 modules; limbs = LLVM on 20000 random i128 / i96 cases (lli) |

Compile time: `zoc` (debug build) over the 40 largest PTX tests 258.7 s direct vs 265.1 s through AIR (+2.5%);
`agx-raise-to-air` itself ~1.6 ms per module, the AGX side of the AIR (`agx-lower-air` + codegen) no slower than
direct.

## Measured: IEEE binary32 in the default mode (`-mattr=+ieee-fp32`)

PTX keeps f32 subnormals without `.ftz` (sm_20+) and rounds `div.rn.f32` / `sqrt.rn.f32` correctly; the AGX f32 ALU
flushes subnormals and its division and square root are approximations. The PTX front end's default mode follows the hardware;
`zoc --strict-fp` (the `ieee-fp32` feature, through `air.ieee-fp32` on the AIR route) follows PTX. The suite costed
with `llc -mattr=+ieee-fp32` against the default (G14, `agx_cost`): `tput` 4.8x the default's (geometric mean over
36 kernels; 10 unchanged, without affected f32 arithmetic), up to 42x (`sgemm_reg4x4`; `layernorm_f16` 39x,
`reduce_shfl` 35x, `saxpy` 19.6x). Split with `-agx-ieee-fp32-no-denormals` (ieee-fp32 with every function's
float denormals flushed): correctly rounded division and square root alone 1.22x (7 kernels change, up to 5.05x),
denormals the rest, 3.95x. Kept as the opt-in strict mode, not the default.

Policy (the user's): anything that costs performance goes into strict floating point (`zoc --strict-fp`, the
`ieee-fp32` feature), the default stays the hardware's. So the cos reduction that meets PTX's 2^-20.4 on
[-2pi, 2pi] (a compare and select) is ieee-fp32 only, and sin(-0) = -0 comes from AGXStrictFP's small-argument
path, not from a select in the default lowering (llvm/test/CodeGen/AGX/trig-strict.ll).

## Research sources (policy)

Experiments are based only on publicly published work: open-access papers (arXiv, ACM / IEEE papers with public
copies, university repositories), open-source compilers (LLVM, Mesa) and public documentation. **Patent documents
are not used as sources**: search results that are patents or patent applications are skipped and nothing is
implemented from them. This is not a freedom-to-operate review: a published technique can still be covered by a
patent, which only a legal review can establish. Techniques already shipped in open-source compilers are preferred.

| Round | Source | Kind | Used for |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Saumya, Sundararajah, Kulkarni, "DARM: Control-Flow Melding for SIMT Thread Divergence Reduction", CGO 2022 (arXiv 2107.05681) | open-access paper | R7 |
| 1 | Lee et al., "Convergence and scalarization for data-parallel architectures", CGO 2013 | paper (public copy, Berkeley) | R1-E6 (deferred) |
| 1 | LLVM AMDGPU GCNSchedStrategy, UniformityAnalysis, SpeculativeExecution | open-source (Apache-2.0 with LLVM exception) | R11, R13, tput metric |
| 1 | Magni, Dubach, O'Boyle, thread coarsening studies (Edinburgh) | papers (university repository) | R1-E6 (deferred) |
| 1 | Lee et al., many-thread-aware prefetching for GPGPUs, MICRO 2010; NVIDIA developer blog on prefetching | paper, public blog | R12 |
| 1 | "Characterizing Warp Divergence from Pascal to Blackwell" (arXiv); NVIDIA Research, SPMD divergence management (2014) | open-access papers | R11 |
| 2 | Osama, Merrill, Cecka, Garland, "Stream-K", PPoPP 2023 (arXiv 2301.03598) | open-access paper (motivation only; the implementation is plain split-K as in open-source BLAS libraries) | A12 |
| 1 | Tillet, Kung, Cox, Triton (MAPL 2019); ML-Triton (arXiv 2503.14985) | open-access papers | background |

## Compile time (policy)

Every adopted change records its `llc` compile-time cost. A change that makes a real difference to the generated
code but costs significantly more compile time (over ~10% of `llc` time on the kernel sets, or more than a few
seconds on a single large kernel) is not enabled by default: it gets its own `llc` option, listed here, and
`zoc` and its runtime pass it only on request. So far none qualifies:

| Change | llc time | Default |
|---|---|---|
| A3 post-RA MachineScheduler | +2% (4.45 -> 4.55 s over 209 files) | on |
| A1 / A2 memory clustering | not measurable | on |
| A15 uniform constants | within noise (3.28-3.41 s vs 3.41-3.45 s over the agx_libs kernels) | off in llc, on in the PTX front end |
| A18-A21 (store-vector pressure, vector access shapes, shared divisors, 32-bit counters) | within noise (4.35 / 4.61 s on vs 4.42 / 4.58 s off over the agx_libs kernels, two runs) | on |

## Model gaps that decide the entries above

1. Occupancy (`tput`): resident groups = min(96, 2 * floor(3328 / (2 * register halves))), whole workgroups,
   64 KiB threadgroup memory per core. A1-A3 and R1, R9 depend on it.
2. Memory after ALU (A1, A2): `mem_quiet_device_store_ref` 33.6, `mem_quiet_load_ref` 20. Does a `wait` or a
   load between stores reset the stall? Do 8-16 back-to-back loads issue as modelled?
3. G13 numbers reuse G14C rows; G13 tile GEMMs (matrix ops emulated, 128 GPRs, spills) move +-20% with any
   scheduling change.
4. Launch overhead, coalescing and cache behaviour are not in `cycles` / `tput`.
5. Uniform registers (A15): the model charges nothing for a uniform source or for more preloaded uniforms. Does a
   larger uniform count slow the dispatch, or limit resident workgroups?

AGX-Performance-Simulator `tasks/ptx-frontend-agx-followups.md` lists the same gaps from the simulator's side.
